| ID | Decision | Options (lean) | Blocks |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Where AI results appear now that AI mode is retired | (A) opt-in "AI tags in grid" setting, matching the earlier anticipated setting **(lean)**; (B) inspector and palette only; (C) a dedicated AI review surface (not a mode) | 6.1, 6.2, 6.5 |
| D2 | Fate of the legacy engine and DINO | Delete; archive to a tag/branch then delete **(lean)**; keep dormant | 2.4-2.6 |
| D3 | What counts as "Edited", and whether edits affect thumbnails and exports | Keep external-variant meaning and add a separate "Adjusted" flag **(lean)**; or unify | 5.3 |
| D4 | Winner handling defaults and semantics | Keep Copy as an option; decide whether async with error toast is acceptable; whether to change the *default* for new installs | 4.1c |
| D5 | The word "Session" | Rename the annotation namespace (e.g. "Profile") **(lean)**; or rename the product concept | 3.4, 7.2 |
| D6 | Shortcut scope | Fully rebindable including review keys, or curated. And which `Ctrl+Alt+P` command keeps the key | 1.1, 3.2 |
| D7 | ~~What "Duplicates" means to the user~~ **Decided 2026-09-27: exact + near-identical bursts** (perceptual-hash/similarity based, not whole-session clustering) | Names and scope of the Settings page and filters | 4.3, 7.2 |
| D8 | "Catalog" vs "Library" naming | UI-label rename only **(lean)** | 7.2 |
| D9 | AI v4 (dimension scoring): finish, park or drop | Also decides the fate of `quality/` and AI entry points | 6.3, 6.4 |
| D10 | Appetite for data/organisation migration | Keep "Codex" and document; or migrate with cache pinning | 3.1, 3.5, 3.6 |
| D11 | WinUI migration (two git stashes) | Keep parked, or drop; affects how much to invest in Qt structure | 4.4 |
| D12 | Editor undo scope | Global adjustments only, or masks, retouch and crop too | 5.1 |
| D13 | Dev tools in product (UI prototype) | Delete, or behind a developer flag | 2.8 |
| D14 | Updater integrity | Require a hash always, or allow unverified installs | 1.3 |
| D15 | Platform support | Linux and macOS are built in CI but PocketDrop's DLL is Windows-only; is cross-platform still a goal? | 2.2, 4.6 |

## 5. Work items by phase

Notation: **Type** . **Risk** (L/M/H) . **Touches** (FILES, EDITS, SETTINGS, ANNOT, SESS, DB, AI-DATA, PKG, UPDATE, KEYS, EXPORT, NAS, COMPAT).

### Phase 0: Trust the ground (no behaviour change)

**WI-0.1 Land and lock the session's work.** *Test/infra + bug correction . L . SETTINGS, KEYS*
- **Finding:** N8, N9, P7; session-caused tests.
- **Current:** Uncommitted changes: transfer dialog, watchdog, edited-folder discovery, settings redesign, crop/mask/editor-render changes, restore-position, hover, zoom slider and more. 6 tests fail because of them.
- **Cause:** Behaviour was changed on request without updating the tests that encode the old behaviour. The watchdog keeps a file open.
- **Areas:** `tests/test_editor_crop_retouch`, `test_editor_render`, `test_settings_dialog`, `test_perf`; `image_triage/perf.py`; `image_triage/preview.py` pool use.
- **Deps:** none. Everything else depends on it.
- **Approach:** (1) Update the 5 tests to the intended new behaviour. (2) Make the stall watchdog opt-in on the performance-logging toggle, close its file on disable or exit, and never start under tests. (3) Give the edited-file discovery its own single-thread pool. (4) The user commits, in logical chunks.
- **Risk:** Low; mostly tests.
- **Validate:** Full-suite diff equals exactly the 6 declared tests.
- **Done:** The baseline has no session-caused failures and the work is committed.
- **Disposition:** Fix.

**WI-0.2 Test runner hygiene and hermetic environment.** *Test/infra . L*
- **Finding:** T1, T2, T7, T8, N1.
- **Current:** 2 import errors, 1 hanging test, a real GPU worker spawned, tests can touch the real registry, and both `unittest` (CI) and `pytest` (local) are used.
- **Cause:** No shared fixtures, no timeouts, no markers.
- **Approach:** Add `conftest.py`: offscreen Qt application fixture; redirect `QSettings` and app-data to a temp dir; a fixture that fails any test spawning `mask_engine_worker`; `importorskip` for optional deps; a `requires_models` marker; per-test timeout. Quarantine the hanging test (skip with reason; deleted with the legacy code). Make `scripts` importable or fix the import. Choose **pytest** as the single runner.
- **Risk:** Low, but "redirect QSettings" must not hide real registry writes in production code.
- **Validate:** Suite runs to completion with no process left behind; running twice gives the same result.
- **Done:** Deterministic full-suite run in a few minutes.
- **Disposition:** Fix.

**WI-0.3 Failure ledger and triage.** *Test/infra . L* (Depends on 0.1, 0.2)
- **Finding:** Appendix A; classification in Part 2 section 2.
- **Approach:** Encode the 31 failures as strict xfails or repairs per the table. Investigate the two `catalog_repository` failures and the six `decision_harvest` failures **before** deciding whether they are stale tests or real drift.
- **Validate:** 0 unexplained failures; every xfail names the item that will resolve it.
- **Done:** Ledger committed.
- **Disposition:** Fix / investigate first (2 + 6).

**WI-0.4 Real CI gate.** *Test/infra . M . PKG* (Depends on 0.2, 0.3)
- **Finding:** N1.
- **Approach:** Add a headless full-suite job (excluding `requires_models`). Keep packaging jobs but decouple them from legacy test modules.
- **Risk:** CI minutes; Windows vs Linux differences in Qt offscreen.
- **Done:** The full suite runs on every push and fails the build on regressions.
- **Disposition:** Fix.

**WI-0.5 Characterization tests and a real-window harness.** *Test/infra . M . FILES, ANNOT, EDITS, NAS* (Depends on 0.2)
- **Finding:** N2, T5, and the safety net for 1.2, 4.1, 6.2.
- **Current:** No tests for winner sync, move/copy/delete/recycle/restore, undo, batch plans, or decision/XMP merge.
- **Approach:** Pin current behaviour against a temp folder for: winner toggle in COPY/HARDLINK/LOGICAL including unmark and failure rollback; `_move_records_by_paths` and `TransferWorker` (rename vs copy, cancel, rollback, unique names); undo for annotation/move/delete; batch rename/resize/convert plans; `discover_edited_paths`; annotation load precedence (session store vs XMP); folder view-state and restore-position; settings round-trip; both shortcut stores. Build a harness that instantiates the real `MainWindow` offscreen against temp settings and services, and **measure** how slow that is. If prohibitive, use thin seams instead.
- **Risk:** The harness may expose latent bugs; record them in the ledger rather than fixing inline.
- **Validate:** Each characterization test fails when the behaviour it pins is deliberately altered (mutation spot-check).
- **Done:** Every file-mutating path has tests that describe it as it is today.
- **Disposition:** Fix.

**WI-0.6 Baselines and a manual smoke script.** *Test/infra . L*
- **Approach:** Record startup time, folder open (local and NAS), winner toggle latency, slider tick latency, popout open. Write a 15-step manual smoke list: open folder, mark winner/reject, undo, move, popout edit, save, AI setup check. Capture `HKCU\Software\Codex\Image Triage` and `HKCU\Software\ImageTriage` via `reg export`.
- **Done:** Numbers and exports stored outside the repo.
- **Disposition:** Fix.

**WI-0.7 Data safety backups (user-run).** *Migration/data . L . ANNOT, AI-DATA, DB*
- **Approach:** Before any data or settings work: copy `decisions.sqlite3`, `library.sqlite3`, `catalog.sqlite3`, and **both** copies of `global_adapter_labels.sqlite` (the live one is under the Store-Python package path, N3), plus the registry exports.
- **Done:** Dated copies exist.
- **Disposition:** Fix (user action).

### Phase 1: Small defects with tight blast radius

**WI-1.1 `Ctrl+Alt+P` collision.** *Bug correction . L . KEYS* (Needs D6)
- **Finding:** U3.
- **Current:** `next_ai_pick` and `share_to_phone` both bind it; Qt then fires neither.
- **Cause:** Independent shortcuts assigned in one large action table with no checker; the Settings conflict checker only covers its 23 rows.
- **Approach:** Give one command a different default once D6 is decided, then add a test that asserts no two actions share a default. Extend the runtime conflict check to cover all actions, not only the registry rows.
- **Risk:** Low. No stored shortcut overrides exist on this machine.
- **Validate:** The uniqueness test; manual: both commands fire.
- **Done:** No duplicate defaults; the test prevents new ones.
- **Disposition:** Fix.

**WI-1.2 Winner un-mark safety.** *Bug correction + behavioural change . M . FILES, NAS* (Depends on 0.5)
- **Finding:** P3.
- **Current:** Un-marking deletes `_winners/<name>` if it exists.
- **Cause:** No provenance check on what the app created.
- **Approach:** Delete only if the file is provably the app's copy: same file (hard link), or size and mtime match the source, or recorded in a small manifest. Otherwise leave it and tell the user. Investigate first whether real folders already contain user-added files in `_winners`.
- **Risk:** Medium. A change of what un-mark does to files; a wrong rule leaves stray copies.
- **Validate:** Characterization tests for all three modes, plus "same-name different file survives".
- **Done:** Un-mark never deletes a file the app did not create.
- **Disposition:** Fix (after the investigation).

**WI-1.3 Updater integrity.** *Investigation, then behavioural change . M . UPDATE* (Needs D14)
- **Finding:** P4.
- **Current:** The hash is checked only when provided.
- **Approach:** Verify what the GitHub release feed actually supplies (`digest` on assets). If always present, require it and fail closed; otherwise decide policy. Keep `test_updater` green.
- **Risk:** Medium. An overly strict rule could block legitimate updates.
- **Done:** A defined behaviour when the hash is missing, tested.
- **Disposition:** Investigate first.

**WI-1.4 Popout header fixture.** *Bug correction . L*
- **Finding:** E5 (reclassified, N7).
- **Approach:** Fix the test fixture (use `EMPTY_METADATA`); optionally add `or EMPTY_METADATA` in the header as defence in depth.
- **Done:** `test_preview_polling` passes.
- **Disposition:** Fix.
- **Status: DONE** — see "WI-1.4 Popout header fixture: DONE (completed within WI-0.3)" in the Implementation log. This entry's own header just lacked the status line.

### Phase 2: Remove noise, leaves inward

**WI-2.1 Reachability tooling and deletion ledger.** *Test/infra . L*
- **Finding:** Section 7 inventory; the limits of name-based analysis.
- **Approach:** Commit the analysis scripts used for the audit (unreferenced defs, unused imports, unconnected signals, unused keys). Add a whitelist for dynamic access (`getattr(self, name)` sites, Qt overrides). Keep a **ledger** of every deletion: what, why, evidence, tests touched.
- **Done:** Re-runnable report; ledger started.
- **Disposition:** Fix.

**WI-2.2 Repo and asset hygiene.** *Safe cleanup . L . PKG* (Depends on 0.1; some need D15)
- **Finding:** A24, A25, A27, A28, A29, N11.
- **Approach:** Unused splash images (5, about 15.7 MB) and the two duplicate PNGs; stray root files (`xbutton.png`, `heartbutton.png`, zips, empty ssh files, `package-lock.json`); untracked 78 MB installer and installed `onnx` under `sandboxes/`; stale `image_triage/engine/*.pyc`. **File and folder removal is the user's action**; exact literal paths will be provided. For `pocketdrop.dll`, decide policy (commit vs build in CI, D15). Check `pyproject.toml` package declarations after 4.6.
- **Risk:** Low, but `freeze_support.py` names `splash_background-v4.png` explicitly (currently unreferenced by code but required by packaging): keep it.
- **Validate:** `test_freeze_support`; a packaging dry run.
- **Done:** Only referenced assets remain and the tree is clean.
- **Disposition:** Candidate for removal (with user-run commands).

**WI-2.3 Peel dead MainWindow code, orphan attributes, unemitted signals, unused imports.** *Safe cleanup . L-M* (Depends on 0.5, 2.1)
- **Finding:** A18, section 7 (38 methods, 39 attributes, 44 unused imports, `dispute_*` and `InspectorPanel` signals, popout helpers).
- **Current:** The unused imports hide which AI-training tasks and dialogs are still reachable.
- **Approach:** Rounds. Round 1 removes unused imports (safe and mechanical), then re-runs the analysis. Round 2 removes methods with zero references in code, tests and strings. Repeat until stable. Do not remove "test-only" methods without deciding whether the test itself is obsolete.
- **Risk:** Low. The risk is dynamic access, which the whitelist catches.
- **Validate:** Full suite diff; app starts; a smoke run of each menu.
- **Done:** The analysis reports no unreferenced MainWindow methods beyond the whitelist.
- **Disposition:** Candidate for removal (verified per round).

**WI-2.4 Retire the dormant DINO, ranker and training-UI stack.** *Architectural change . M . SETTINGS, AI-DATA, COMPAT* (Depends on 2.3; needs D2)
- **Finding:** A5, A7, I6, S1 (DINO page), S3.
- **Current:** DINO is forced off in three places. `TrainRankerDialog`, source pickers and the progress dialog are never instantiated. `FilterMode.DINO_*` exists.
- **Cause:** The pivot to CLI-Culler left the old scaffolding.
- **Approach:** Remove in this order: (1) unreachable window code and imports; (2) unreachable `ai_training` members; (3) the dormant DINO run stage and settings page. **Keep** `FilterMode.DINO_REMOVED/RESCUED` values (or map them) until we confirm saved filter presets (`filters/saved_queries`) do not contain them, because deserialising an unknown enum value could break loading. **Never touch** `global_adapter_labels.sqlite` or registered training sources.
- **Risk:** Medium. Touches AI/training data code paths and persisted filters.
- **Validate:** Reachability report; open a saved filter containing each `FilterMode`; AI run end-to-end on a small folder; adapter training still works.
- **Done:** No DINO or ranker UI, settings or run stages remain; live adapter flows unchanged.
- **Disposition:** Candidate for removal, pending D2.

**WI-2.5 Carve out `ai_workflow.py`.** *Internal refactor . M-H . AI-DATA, SETTINGS, COMPAT* (Depends on 2.4)
- **Finding:** A4, S4.
- **Current:** `window._ai_runtime` (an `AIWorkflowRuntime`) still drives device preference, model installation status and the setup dialogs. `aiculler_workflow` imports paths, metrics parsing and hidden-root helpers from it.
- **Cause:** The "runtime" concept predates CLI-Culler.
- **Approach:** (1) Move the live pieces (`AIWorkflowPaths`, `build_ai_workflow_paths`, hidden-root names, metrics parsing, device/worker capacity, artifact readiness) into a small module, **keeping the on-disk layout byte-identical**. (2) Move device selection and model-installation state onto the CLI-Culler runtime. (3) Then delete `AIRunTask`, staging and the AICullingPipeline command runners. `ai_workflow_cache` rows in the catalog database must still resolve.
- **Risk:** Medium-high. Existing per-folder `.image_triage_ai` data must remain readable; device selection must not regress.
- **Validate:** Open a folder with existing AI data and results load; device preference persists; `test_aiculler_workflow` and the catalog cache tests.
- **Done:** No live code imports the old runner code.
- **Disposition:** Refactor, then remove.

**WI-2.6 Decommission `AICullingPipeline/`.** *Architectural change . H . PKG, CI, COMPAT* (Depends on 2.4, 2.5, 0.4; needs D2)
- **Finding:** A6.
- **Current:** CI requires `AICullingPipeline/app|configs|scripts`; `freeze_support` stages it; `ai_python_runner` prepends its root; about 103 legacy tests exercise it.
- **Alternatives:** (A) delete outright; (B) tag/archive, stop packaging, keep one release cycle, then delete **(lean)**; (C) keep dormant and documented.
- **Approach:** Stop staging and CI checks first; update `test_freeze_support` and the Linux test list; remove legacy tests with the code; update README and docs. A real MSI build plus the clean-machine matrix is the acceptance gate.
- **Risk:** High for packaged builds; low for the running app.
- **Validate:** A clean MSI build; an installed app runs a full AI cull; frozen-app smoke.
- **Done:** The engine is out of the shipped app, CI and the default test run.
- **Disposition:** Candidate for removal, blocked.

**WI-2.7 Settings-dialog dead surface, dead toolbar customizer, dead keys.** *Safe cleanup . L* (Depends on 2.4; coordinate with 3.3)
- **Finding:** A15, S1, S2, S3, S4.
- **Approach:** Drop the four hidden controls (dispute weight, base weight, near-duplicate threshold, DINO workers) **after** deciding whether adapter labelling still needs the first two as real settings. Drop the four unused callbacks and `ToolbarCustomizerDialog`. Stop *reading* dead keys. **Do not delete registry values**; only stop referencing them.
- **Risk:** Low, except that dispute weight and base weight feed live adapter code (`window.py:17447`, `_apply_base_score_blend_to_workflow`). **Verify before deciding** whether they become visible settings or fixed constants.
- **Done:** Every constructor argument and widget in the dialog has a consumer.
- **Disposition:** Investigate first (two settings), otherwise remove.

**WI-2.8 UI prototype and dev tooling.** *Safe cleanup . L* (Needs D13)
- **Finding:** U8.
- **Approach:** Remove or gate `generated_prototype.py`, the `open_ui_prototype` action, its shortcut target and its menu item. `prototype_style.py` is **live** (used by `FolderTreeView`); rename it later, do not delete.
- **Done:** No dev-only window in the product menus.
- **Disposition:** Decision, then remove.

### Phase 3: Foundations (settings, shortcuts, persistence)

**WI-3.1 Settings identity and registry inventory.** *Migration/data . M . SETTINGS, COMPAT* (Needs D10)
- **Finding:** A20, S3/S7, N10.
- **Current:** Five `QSettings()` constructions; the organisation is "Codex"; `ui/shortcuts.py` uses a *different* identity (`ImageTriage`).
- **Alternatives:** (A) rename the organisation with copy-migration and pin the cache path; (B) keep "Codex" and document; (C) unify the stray identity onto the existing one **(lean now)**. Do A only if wanted.
- **Approach:** Introduce one settings accessor; read the old identity as a fallback; write only to the current one. Produce a read-only inventory of registry values with no code reference.
- **Risk:** Medium for A (the cache and every setting move); low for C.
- **Validate:** Round-trip tests; a registry export diff before and after.
- **Done:** One settings identity; documented.
- **Disposition:** Refactor, with the rename deferred pending D10.

**WI-3.2 Unify shortcuts.** *Architectural change . M . KEYS, SETTINGS* (Depends on 1.1, 3.1; needs D6)
- **Finding:** A10, U2, U3.
- **Current:** Two editors, two stores (per-action keys vs a JSON blob), different IDs. The window applies its system after the registry one, and captures the "default" *after* overrides, so its "Reset" restores the customised key.
- **Cause:** The window's binding system grew first; the registry was added later for the Settings page.
- **Approach:** Investigate first: enumerate every action, every hard-coded key handler in `grid`/`preview`, and the palette. End state: one registry describing every binding (actions plus review keys), one store, one editor UI (Settings > Shortcuts). Migrate by reading both old stores. Decide which hard-coded keys become rebindable (D6).
- **Risk:** Medium. Keyboard is the core interaction; test on the real grid.
- **Validate:** The uniqueness test; a table-driven test that each default key triggers its action; manual pass over the grid, popout and palette.
- **Done:** One store, one editor, no default drift, collision checker covers everything.
- **Disposition:** Consolidate.

**WI-3.3 Reconcile Settings with real consumers.** *UX change . M . SETTINGS* (Depends on 2.7, 3.1; needs D1, D5)
- **Finding:** S1-S5, U7.
- **Approach:** Add settings that are missing (theme, performance logging, and the AI-tag choice from D1). Remove duplicated toggles (View menu vs Settings) or make one authoritative. Rename "Processing workers" to match its stored meaning, or fix the key. Add a test that every field of the settings result has a consumer.
- **Risk:** Medium. Behaviour of `restore_folder_position` and similar new switches must stay.
- **Done:** Each setting has one home and one consumer.
- **Disposition:** Refactor and UX change.

**WI-3.4 Annotation persistence semantics.** *Investigation . M . ANNOT, SESS, DB* (Depends on 0.5; needs D5)
- **Finding:** A19, A22, N5.
- **Current:** Session store and XMP both feed annotations; a non-empty session record overrides a sidecar; an empty one does not.
- **Approach:** Write a specification of precedence, prove it with the characterization tests, then decide whether the "sidecar resurrects a cleared mark in another session" case is reproducible. Fix only if it is real. Do **not** alter the schema in this item. Rename "Session" in the UI only after D5.
- **Risk:** High if rushed (annotations are the culling work).
- **Done:** A written precedence rule with tests; any confirmed bug fixed or ledgered.
- **Disposition:** Investigate first.

**WI-3.5 Thumbnail cache policy.** *Performance / migration . M . CACHE, NAS* (Depends on 3.1)
- **Finding:** A23 (strengthened by N4: 3.2 GB, 72,860 files).
- **Alternatives:** (A) cap by size with LRU eviction and keep path-keyed **(lean first)**; (B) also key by content (name, size, mtime) so moves do not invalidate; (C) leave unbounded.
- **Approach:** Add a background, throttled eviction with a configurable cap; do it in-app only for the app's own cache directory. Changing the key invalidates the existing cache once, so treat B as a separate step.
- **Risk:** Medium. The organisation rename would move this directory (see 3.1).
- **Validate:** Unit tests with a temp cache and size cap; a manual check on the real cache.
- **Done:** Cache size stays under the cap.
- **Disposition:** Fix.

**WI-3.6 Data placement (Roaming vs Local; Store-Python redirect).** *Investigation . M . AI-DATA, COMPAT* (Needs D10)
- **Finding:** A21, N3.
- **Approach:** Measure what the **running** process sees. Classify each file as irreplaceable (`global_adapter_labels.sqlite`, 425 labels), derived (embedding workspace) or cache. Only after that consider relocating derived data, using an explicit migration, never implicit. Test in Store Python **and** the frozen app.
- **Risk:** High for irreplaceable data.
- **Disposition:** Investigate first; defer.
- **Status: DONE (2026-10-01).** Migration executed and independently verified — see the dated write-up below. The user explicitly chose to do the full migration now rather than leave it deferred, after being shown the risk.

### Phase 4: Structural enablers

**WI-4.1 File-operation service (three sub-steps).** *Architectural + behavioural + performance . H . FILES, ANNOT, NAS, EDITS* (Depends on 0.5, 1.2; needs D4)
- **Finding:** P1, P2, N9, and the Move-to dialog gaps.
- **Current:** `_sync_winner_copy` copies inline before the undo entry and persistence; `_apply_ai_culling` moves per record with a full view rebuild each time; `_move_records_by_paths` also removes records one by one.
- **Cause:** File I/O, undo, persistence and view refresh are interleaved in `MainWindow` methods.
- **Approach:**
  - **4.1a Extract** winner sync, move/copy/delete, recycle, and undo construction into a module with a UI-free API. No behaviour change; the characterization tests must pass unchanged.
  - **4.1b Batch removal API:** remove N records, then one view refresh. Route `_move_records_by_paths`, Apply-AI and drag-drop through it.
  - **4.1c Async winner copy:** run the copy in the annotation worker with optimistic UI, a visible failure notice and rollback. Decide (D4) how undo behaves while a copy is pending, and keep Copy mode working on the NAS.
- **Risk:** High. This is the path that writes user files.
- **Validate:** Characterization suite; a stress test of 300 moves (time, view rebuild count); a NAS manual test.
- **Done:** No file copy or move on the UI thread; one view refresh per batch; behaviour otherwise identical.
- **Disposition:** Refactor, then behavioural change.
- **Status: DONE (2026-09-27).** All three sub-steps landed — see the dated write-ups below ("4.1a partially done", "WI-4.1b done: batched removal API", "WI-4.1c done: async winner copy"). This header never got its own status line added at the time (doc-lag, same pattern as WI-5.2/WI-1.4/WI-6.1 earlier this session) even though the work was finished and WI-4.2/WI-4.4 were already noted as unblocked by it.

**WI-4.2 Job and progress consolidation.** *Internal refactor . M* (Depends on 4.1)
- **Finding:** A11, P8 (`processEvents`).
- **Approach:** Extend `JobController` with byte and speed reporting, cancel and detail text, then migrate the transfer dialog and the raw `QProgressDialog`s. Keep the stage-aware AI dialog and the inline overlay separate (Part 2 section 3). Remove `processEvents` calls where a worker now exists.
- **Risk:** Medium (reentrancy behaviour changes).
- **Done:** One generic progress path; no `processEvents` in file operations.
- **Disposition:** Consolidate (generic ones only).

**WI-4.3 Duplicate/similar-frame systems.** *Investigation + decision . M* (Needs D7)
- **Finding:** A13, U5.
- **Approach:** Document what each engine produces and who consumes it; measure whether pHash is computed twice per folder; decide whether to share the computation and align vocabulary. Do **not** merge engines. Settings > Duplicates keeps its scope but is renamed or explained.
- **Done:** A one-page map and a decision.
- **Disposition:** Investigate first.

**WI-4.4 MainWindow decomposition.** *Architectural change . H* (Depends on 0.5, 2.3-2.6, 4.1, 3.2; needs D11)
- **Finding:** A1, A2 (init size), P6.
- **Sizing (measured):** about 7,000 lines (27%) AI/ML related; 4,250 toolbar/topbar/palette/menus; 2,570 scan/records-view/filter/search; 2,050 annotations/review/undo; 1,690 file ops; 650 catalog/library; 1,500 layout/appearance/dialogs.
- **Alternatives:** (A) mixins (cheap, no coupling reduction); (B) feature controllers with explicit interfaces **(lean)**; (C) prune only.
- **Order:** AI orchestration (already shrunk by 2.4-2.6) -> toolbar/palette/menus -> file-ops service (from 4.1) -> catalog/library -> **last** records-view/scan (highest coupling). Keep thin delegating methods until callers migrate.
- **Risk:** High. Attribute ownership is tangled (558 attributes); use the real-window harness.
- **Done (per slice):** Public behaviour unchanged; the slice's state lives in its controller; the harness and full suite pass.
- **Disposition:** Refactor incrementally; the last slice deferred.
- **Status (2026-09-29): FULLY DONE, including the previously-deferred last slice.** Done: menu-builder extraction (`ToolbarMenuController`), batch-rename controller, folder-ops controller, recycle-bin controller, jiggle-mode editor **deleted outright** (dead feature, not extracted), catalog/library controller (`CatalogController`), record-list state ownership (`RecordsRepository`), the full move/copy/delete/rename/undo orchestration relocation (`RecordOpsController`), the command palette (`CommandPaletteController`), and — at your request, going beyond the plan's own allowance to leave it deferred — the records-view/scan slice (`RecordsViewController`). See the dated write-ups below for each.
  1. ~~Toolbar jiggle-mode editor~~ **Done 2026-09-28 — deleted, not extracted.** Confirmed dead (killed by a later toolbar redesign, no working entry point left) and removed outright rather than decomposed into a controller.
  2. ~~Command palette~~ **Done 2026-09-29 — see the dated write-up below.** Extracted as `CommandPaletteController`, same window-back-reference pattern as every other WI-4.4 controller, rather than the per-subsystem-provider restructuring floated earlier — see the write-up for why.
  3. ~~Move/copy/delete/undo orchestration~~ **Done 2026-09-28 — see the dated write-up below.** Both halves complete: state ownership (`RecordsRepository`) and the ~44-method orchestration relocation (`RecordOpsController`).
  4. ~~Catalog/library slice~~ **Done 2026-09-28 — see the dated write-up below.** Extracted the clean ~27-method subset (catalog roots, refresh task, virtual collections, folder-records cache pass-through); left the review-cache status/debug badges and the sidebar Projects panel on `MainWindow` as too entangled with other subsystems.
  5. ~~Records-view/scan slice~~ **Done 2026-09-29 — see the dated write-up below.** You chose to attempt this after WI-4.4's original scope closed, despite the plan explicitly allowing it to stay deferred permanently as "highest coupling." Extracted as `RecordsViewController`.
  - **WI-4.4 is closed in full.** No deferred slices remain.

- **WI-4.4 item 2 finished: command palette relocated (2026-09-29).** New `image_triage/command_palette_controller.py::CommandPaletteController`, holding only the window back-reference (no state of its own — `_command_palette_open`, `_active_command_palette`, `_command_palette_dialogs`, the two `QShortcut` instances and `_recent_command_ids` all stay on `MainWindow`, same as before). Moved: `_open_command_palette`, `_setup_command_palette_shortcuts`, `_set_command_palette_shortcuts_enabled`, `_apply_command_palette_shortcut`, `_ensure_command_palette_dialog`, `_build_command_palette_commands` (the 468-line one), `_preset_subtitle`, `_clean_command_text`, `_toggle_state_text`, `_remember_recent_command`. Kept as thin `MainWindow` delegates: `_open_command_palette` and `_setup_command_palette_shortcuts` (both have external callers — `preview.command_palette_requested`, a status-bar hint click, and the app's own `__init__`) and `_handle_command_palette_finished` (kept deliberately, not because of an external caller, but because `CommandPaletteDialog.finished` connects to it and `QObject.sender()` tracking requires the connected slot to be a bound method of the QObject that's meant to observe it — `MainWindow`, not the plain-Python controller — so the delegate calls straight into `controller.handle_finished(result)`, which still safely reads `window.sender()` since that's set for the whole synchronous call chain starting at the QObject slot). The other 7 (`_set_command_palette_shortcuts_enabled`, `_ensure_command_palette_dialog`, `_build_command_palette_commands`, `_preset_subtitle`, `_clean_command_text`, `_toggle_state_text`, `_remember_recent_command`) had zero remaining callers once the controller's own methods called each other directly, so they were deleted rather than kept as unnecessary delegates — same pattern as every other WI-4.4 slice's dead-delegate cleanup.
  - **On the "needs restructuring into per-subsystem providers" note from the original audit:** didn't do that. The straight single-controller move (same pattern used for `CatalogController` and `RecordOpsController`) kept the change mechanical and low-risk, and `_build_command_palette_commands` was messy in the sense of being *long*, not in the sense of having tangled internal logic — it's a flat sequence of `add_action_command(...)`/`PaletteCommand(...)` calls with no branching complexity worth decomposing further. Splitting it into providers would have been an abstraction the task didn't need.
  - **Two unused imports cascaded**: `FOCUS_ASSIST_COLORS`/`FOCUS_ASSIST_STRENGTHS` in `window.py` (their only use was inside the moved preview-command block) and a leftover `RecordFilterQuery` import in the new controller file (imported but never used there — the one place that needed it, `_matching_saved_filter_preset`, stayed on `MainWindow`). Also dropped the now-unused `PaletteCommand` import from `window.py`'s own `.ui` import block.
  - **Verification**: reachability clean (0 orphan modules, 0 unused imports, 0 unreferenced methods) after the cleanup; full suite: 1514 passed, 1 skipped, 12 xfailed, only the 2 known-pre-existing-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` tests failed — same baseline as every other slice this phase. No test changes needed: every external call site (menu actions, the `Ctrl+K` shortcuts, `preview.command_palette_requested`, the status-bar hint) still calls the same `MainWindow` method names.

- **WI-4.4 item 5 finished: records-view/scan slice relocated (2026-09-29), the plan's own "last slice deferred" allowance.** You chose to attempt this after confirming WI-4.4's agreed scope ("items 2 and 3") was already closed. A read-only investigation first mapped the cluster precisely: the plan's "~2,570 lines" estimate was already stale — `RecordsRepository` and the other three slices had already peeled off adjacent code, leaving **~1,950-2,000 lines across ~78-83 methods**. The investigation also surfaced the clearest concrete evidence of "highest coupling": `RecordOpsController.rekey_filter_metadata_after_moves` was reaching directly into `window._filter_metadata_by_path`/`_filter_metadata_loaded_paths`/`_filter_metadata_record_paths` — bypassing any accessor, a real state-ownership violation baked in during WI-4.4 item 3. Two design decisions were confirmed with you up front: (1) one controller, not split into several, to keep the pattern consistent with every other slice; (2) fix the `RecordOpsController` violation as part of this slice.
  - **What moved:** new `image_triage/records_view_controller.py::RecordsViewController`, covering six sub-areas — folder loading/scanning (`_load_folder`, `_load_virtual_scope_records`, the four `_handle_scan_*` signal handlers, `_apply_loaded_records` and its chunking/enrichment helpers), the records-view rebuild pipeline (`_apply_records_view`, `_finalize_records_view_display`, chunked-render helpers, sort/rank helpers), filter-query and search-bar wiring (`_set_filter_mode` and friends, `_apply_filter_query_change`, the filter-metadata background index), saved filter presets (matching/apply/save/delete, the toolbar menu builders), and unified/semantic/face search orchestration (`UnifiedSearchTask`/`UnifiedSearchSignals` — previously module-level classes in `window.py`, moved here since they belong to this cluster — plus the semantic- and face-index background passes and the people-search dialog). Left on `MainWindow` as before: state itself (`_filter_query`, `_records`, `_folder_records`, `_records_view_cache`, all the scan/search/index tokens — same non-ownership-transfer pattern as every other slice except `RecordsRepository`), the module-level path-key helpers `_memory_path_key`/`_search_match_path_key` (moved alongside `UnifiedSearchTask` into the new module and imported back into `window.py`, since keeping them in `window.py` while the class that used them moved would have meant a circular import), and a ~190-line "borderline" set of folder-navigation and phash-prefilter helpers the investigation flagged as adjacent rather than core (`_remember_current_folder_view_state`, `_apply_folder_view_state`, `_refresh_directory_navigation_buttons`, `_load/_save_phash_prefilter_settings`, `_refresh_prefilter_decisions_for_current_folder`, and similar) — not part of this cluster's own concern, so left where they were.
  - **The state-ownership violation, fixed:** `RecordOpsController.rekey_filter_metadata_after_moves`'s body (the dict surgery on `_filter_metadata_*`) moved onto the new controller as `RecordsViewController.rekey_filter_metadata_after_moves`; `RecordOpsController`'s own method is now a one-line delegate to `window._records_view.rekey_filter_metadata_after_moves(...)`, and the now-unused `CaptureMetadata`/`replace` imports were dropped from `record_ops_controller.py`.
  - **Why intra-cluster calls use direct `self.method()`, not a window round-trip:** unlike the blanket-safe fallback used in a couple of single-call spots in earlier slices, every one of the ~78-83 moved methods calls its moved siblings directly on the controller (`self.apply_records_view(...)`, not `window._apply_records_view(...)`). This was worth the extra transcription care because it kept the same "delete truly-dead delegates" step from previous slices meaningful here too — reachability could actually tell a delegate with a real caller from one that only existed because it stayed in the window round-trip.
  - **Two hazards specific to this slice, both caught by verification, not review:**
    1. A method transcription miss: `_cancel_hidden_ai_results_load` (a "hidden AI results" background-task canceller, called from `_load_folder` but logically belonging to a different, still-in-file AI subsystem) sat physically between two methods that *were* moving in the source file. It was correctly identified as not-core and meant to stay on `MainWindow`, but got deleted along with its neighbors during the line-range splice and never restored — a pure copy-paste-boundary mistake, not a design error. Caught immediately by the first full test run (`AttributeError: 'MainWindow' object has no attribute '_cancel_hidden_ai_results_load'`, 44 failures) and fixed by restoring the method verbatim in `window.py`.
    2. Six unit tests (`tests/test_background_index_arbitration.py`'s 5 tests, `tests/test_window_catalog_cache.py`'s 3 chunking/metadata-index tests) called `MainWindow._suspend_background_indexing`/`_resume_background_indexing`/`_should_chunk_loaded_records`/`_reset_filter_metadata_index` directly on a lightweight `SimpleNamespace`/stub object rather than a real window, to exercise the logic without building a whole `MainWindow`. Once those became one-line delegates to `self._records_view.method(...)`, the stubs (lacking a `_records_view` attribute) broke. Rewrote both files to construct a `RecordsViewController(stub)` and call the controller directly instead of the `MainWindow` delegate, monkey-patching the controller's own now-intra-cluster methods (`maybe_start_semantic_index`, `metadata_prefetch_seed_paths`, `enqueue_filter_metadata_paths`) where the old stubs used to override them at the window level. This in turn revealed 2 more delegates (`_maybe_start_semantic_index`, `_should_chunk_loaded_records`) that had only ever been reachable through those old test call sites — removed them too, along with `_selected_filter_mode` and 25 others confirmed dead via reachability + grep, once the test rewrite was in place.
  - **Verification**: reachability clean (0 orphan modules, 0 unused imports, 0 unreferenced methods); full suite 1514 passed, 1 skipped, 12 xfailed, only the 2 known-pre-existing-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` tests failed — re-run twice to confirm two other single-run failures (`test_char_batch_rename_controller.py`'s rename test, `test_thumbnail_cache_eviction.py`) were pre-existing order-dependent flakiness, not caused by this change: both passed standalone and on the second full-suite run.

**WI-4.5 UI-as-state-holder cleanup.** *Internal refactor . M* (Depends on 6.1; touches D1)
- **Finding:** A17.
- **Approach:** Trace every reader of the hidden `mode_tabs` and the popout's legacy analysis panel. Move state to plain objects, then remove the hidden widgets.
- **Disposition:** Investigate first.
- **Status: PARTIAL (2026-09-29) — `mode_tabs` done, the analysis panel deliberately deferred.** An investigation found the two halves had very different risk profiles.
  - **`mode_tabs` (done):** a hidden `QTabBar` in `window.py` kept alive purely as a state holder for `_ui_mode`, per the comment "AI Review is retired; the tabs stay only as the manual-mode state holder." Traced every reader/writer (~10-12 call sites) and confirmed it was low-risk, mechanical dead weight — `_ui_mode` was already a plain attribute; `mode_tabs` was just upstream plumbing that produced it via one signal connection, and could never actually leave index 0 since `_handle_mode_tab_changed` forces it back immediately. **Removed** the widget entirely: its construction/layout/hide (`window.py`, `MainWindow.__init__`), `_refresh_mode_tabs_width` (definition + 2 call sites), the two vestigial `setCurrentIndex(0)` resets in the adapter-review-labeling flow, and the now-unused `QTabBar` import. Simplified `_set_ui_mode` (no more `mode_tabs.currentIndex()` guard — just calls `_handle_mode_tab_changed(0)` directly) and `_switch_review_mode_to` (now an explicit no-op with a comment, matching its already-inert real-world behavior). `tests/test_nav_rail.py`'s `ManualOnlyModeTests` pinned the old `mode_tabs`-correction behavior directly (constructing a fake `mode_tabs` and asserting it gets reset) — rewritten to test the simplified behavior instead, since the scenario it pinned (a stale non-zero tab index) can no longer occur once the widget doesn't exist.
  - **The popout's analysis panel (deferred):** found real, live indirection — `set_focus_assist_color_by_id`, `cycle_focus_assist_color`, and `set_focus_assist_strength_by_id` change state by calling `.setCurrentIndex()` on hidden combo boxes (`focus_assist_color_combo`/`focus_assist_strength_combo`, both permanently invisible since Studio layout — confirmed the only layout that ever runs — hides their parent `analysis_panel`), relying on the resulting `currentIndexChanged` signal to actually apply the color/strength change, clear caches, and trigger a re-render. This **works correctly today** — it is not a bug, just an indirect architecture. Fixing it means extracting the real state-change logic out of the signal handlers into direct-call helpers and rewiring 4 external call sites (command palette, keyboard shortcuts) to use them — a moderate-risk change to live interactive preview/render code (focus peaking, used during actual photo review), for a purely architectural benefit with no functional payoff. Given the risk-to-value ratio and no one available to watch for a live-preview regression, left this half alone rather than push it through unsupervised. Also flagged, not touched: the "legacy" (non-Studio) preview layout is now confirmed **permanently dead** — `_studio_layout_active` is forced `True` immediately after construction (`preview.py:~1169-1170`) and never set back to `False` anywhere — so `analysis_panel` is always hidden in practice; a full sweep of the ~16 `if getattr(self, "_studio_layout_active", False):` branches throughout `preview.py` (collapsing them to their always-true branch, per this session's established dead-code pattern) is a separate, larger cleanup than WI-4.5 asked for and is a good candidate for its own future work item.
  - Reachability clean; full suite 1514 passed, only the 2 known-pre-existing-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` tests failed.

**WI-4.6 Editor engine import and packaging.** *Migration/packaging . M-H . PKG* (Depends on 2.6; needs D15)
- **Finding:** A8, A29.
- **Alternatives:** (A) vendor `photo_terminal` into the package; (B) declare `cli_editor` as a real dependency **(lean)**; (C) keep the path hack, documented.
- **Risk:** Medium-high: the frozen app copies it to `lib/photo_terminal`, and a wrong change breaks the editor at startup (a class of failure `setup_msi.py` already documents).
- **Validate:** Frozen-app smoke; a `pip install` of a wheel imports the editor.
- **Disposition:** Investigate first.
- **Status (2026-09-29): DONE — option (A), vendored.** You chose to vendor rather than the plan's "lean" default (B), after a read-only investigation confirmed the concrete facts: `cli_editor/photo_terminal` was a fully tracked, independently-versioned package (its own `pyproject.toml`, name `terminal-photo-editor`, its own `photoedit` CLI entry point) reached via a `sys.path` hack from 3 call sites, plus a 4th script (`scripts/tone_control_calibration.py`) using an *incompatible* second import convention (`cli_editor.photo_terminal.X` as a namespace package, vs. the other three sites' `sys.path.insert` + bare `photo_terminal.X`). `editor_render.py` additionally had an undeclared import-order dependency — it assumed one of the other two modules had already run and mutated `sys.path` before its own lazy import fired. This resolved D15 as *not actually a blocker* for WI-4.6: the platform-support decision affects `pocketdrop.dll` (WI-2.2) and CI's Linux job, not this packaging choice — vendoring is OS-agnostic either way. D15 itself is still open for those other items.
  - **What moved:** `cli_editor/photo_terminal/*.py` → `image_triage/photo_terminal/` (`git mv`, history preserved); `cli_editor/README.md` → `image_triage/photo_terminal/README.md`; `cli_editor/docs/edit-session-schema-v1.md` → `image_triage/photo_terminal/edit-session-schema-v1.md`. Deleted (superseded, not merely dead): `cli_editor/pyproject.toml` and `cli_editor/photoedit_cli.py` — the standalone package identity these described no longer exists. Added `image_triage/photo_terminal/__main__.py` (so `python -m image_triage.photo_terminal` still works) and a `photoedit = "image_triage.photo_terminal.cli:main"` entry in the root `pyproject.toml`'s `[project.scripts]`, so the standalone CLI survives the vendoring rather than being silently dropped.
  - **Import sites fixed** (all `sys.path` hacks removed, all imports now ordinary relative imports): `image_triage/ui/photo_editor_panel.py` (3 top-level imports + 1 lazy one, `from photo_terminal.X` → `from ..photo_terminal.X`), `image_triage/edit_storage.py` (`from .photo_terminal.session import ...`), `image_triage/editor_render.py` (the lazy `apply_retouch` import, with its now-obsolete "only on sys.path once the editor UI has set it up" comment removed since the import-order hazard no longer exists), `scripts/tone_control_calibration.py` (`from cli_editor.photo_terminal...` → `from image_triage.photo_terminal...`). `photo_terminal`'s own internal cross-module imports were already relative (`.adjustments`, `.io`, etc.) and needed no changes.
  - **MSI freeze step**: `freeze_support.py`'s raw `include_files` copy of `cli_editor/photo_terminal` → `lib/photo_terminal` was removed — `photo_terminal` now travels automatically as part of the `image_triage` package cx_Freeze already follows from the entry script, the same way every other `image_triage.*` submodule does. As a defensive safety net (since `editor_render.py`'s import is lazy, and this exact file already has one precedent — `image_triage.ui.layout_ratios` — where cx_Freeze's static scan missed a real import), added explicit `image_triage.photo_terminal*` entries to `setup_msi.py`'s `includes` list rather than trusting the scan alone. This could not be verified by an actual MSI build in this sandbox; the plain-`pip install` / `import` path (the actual bug in A8) **was** verified directly (see below).
  - **The two-import-convention bug, resolved by construction**: since every call site now imports `image_triage.photo_terminal` as an ordinary subpackage, the `cli_editor.photo_terminal.X` vs. bare `photo_terminal.X` split is gone — there is exactly one way to import it now.
  - **Divergent Pillow pins resolved by deletion**: `cli_editor/pyproject.toml`'s own `Pillow>=10.0` (vs. the root project's `Pillow>=10.4`) no longer exists as a separate declaration once that file was removed.
  - **8 test files updated** to match: `test_adjustments_vectorized.py`, `test_curves.py`, `test_vignette.py`, `test_editor_crop_retouch.py`, `test_editor_tool_rail.py` (all dropped their own `sys.path` hack and switched to `from image_triage.photo_terminal...`), `test_tone_controls.py` and `test_mask_refinement.py` (switched from the namespace-package `cli_editor.photo_terminal.X` style to `image_triage.photo_terminal.X`), and `test_freeze_support.py` (removed `test_frozen_layout_ships_cli_editor_package_on_the_import_path`, which tested the now-deleted raw-copy mechanism; added `test_packaging_scripts.py::test_vendored_editor_engine_is_named_explicitly_for_cx_freeze` in its place, asserting the new `setup_msi.py` includes entries via the file's existing AST-based literal-scanning helpers).
  - **Verification**: reachability clean (0 orphan modules, 0 unused imports, 0 unreferenced methods) — this also surfaced and fixed 2 pre-existing unused imports inside the vendored package itself (`ImageDraw` in `adjustments.py`, `WriteError` in `cli.py`), invisible to the reachability tool while `cli_editor/` lived outside `image_triage/`'s scan scope. Full suite: 1514 passed, 1 skipped, 12 xfailed, only the 2 known-pre-existing-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` tests failed. Also directly verified (not just via the test suite) that `from image_triage.photo_terminal.adjustments import EditRecipe`, `...cli import main`, and `...session import new_session` all import cleanly with zero `sys.path` manipulation — the actual bug A8 described.

The investigation confirmed both exist and are conceptually the same ~35-40 line calculation, but they render through genuinely different primitives (PIL pixel arrays for the CLI/session renderer vs. Qt gradients for the real-time interactive overlay) — sharing one implementation would mean abstracting behind both output types or switching one side's rendering primitive, which is a real design decision beyond "fix the packaging," not a mechanical follow-on from vendoring.

**WI-4.7 Copy progress, confirmation, and RAW+JPG bundling choice.** *UX + behavioural . M* (new, raised by the user 2026-10-01)
- **Finding (new, not in the original audit):** three real gaps in the move/copy UX, found by the user clicking through the live app after WI-5.3/6.2/6.3 landed:
  1. **Copy has no progress dialog at all.** Confirmed in code: `RecordOpsController.copy_records_by_paths`/`copy_selected_records_to_destination` (`image_triage/record_ops_controller.py`) is a plain per-file loop with no `run_move_transfer` — unlike `move_records_by_paths`, which already routes through `transfer_progress.run_move_transfer` (progress + cancel) since WI-4.1b. Move has progress; Copy does not.
  2. **"Copy to folder" / "Copy to recent destination" has no confirmation step.** Picking a destination immediately copies — confirmed `copy_selected_records_to_destination` has no confirmation dialog of any kind before calling `copy_records_by_paths`.
  3. **No completion dialog with an "Open destination" button**, for either Move or Copy — both just show a status-bar message on success (`window.statusBar().showMessage(...)`) and, for Move, a `QMessageBox.warning` only on failure. Nothing lets the user jump straight to the folder they just moved/copied into.
  4. **RAW+JPG pairs move/copy together implicitly, with no explicit choice.** `window._record_paths(record)` (bundles `companion_paths`, i.e. a RAW's paired JPEG) is passed into every move/copy call (`TransferItem(..., window._record_paths(record))` for moves; the same bundling applies to single-record copy) with no UI surfaced to decide per-operation whether companions should come along.
- **Approach (not yet scoped in detail — do this before implementing):** (a) route Copy through the same `run_move_transfer`-style progress+cancel mechanism Move already has, or a sibling "copy transfer" variant if the semantics differ enough (copy failure modes differ from move's); (b) add a confirmation step before Copy/Move-to-folder commits (likely matching WI-6.2's precedent of a reviewable thumbnail-grid dialog, for consistency, though a plain count+destination confirmation may be enough — worth asking the user which); (c) add a completion dialog (or extend the existing progress dialog to a completion state) for both Move and Copy with an "Open destination" button (likely `os.startfile`/platform-equivalent reveal, check for existing precedent elsewhere in the app, e.g. PocketDrop or the handoff builder, before inventing a new one); (d) make RAW+JPG bundling an explicit per-operation choice rather than a silent default — needs a product decision on the default (bundle on, with an opt-out? or ask every time?) before implementing.
- **Disposition:** DONE (2026-10-01) — built slice by slice, starting with Copy's progress dialog, then finishing the remaining three slices together once scoped.
- **Slice 1, Copy progress dialog: DONE (2026-10-01).** Investigated before building: the plan's assumption that Copy could just reuse `run_move_transfer` with a different `verb` turned out to be wrong — `TransferWorker` unconditionally deleted the source file after every transfer (`os.remove(source)`, or an `os.rename` which *is* the delete for same-volume transfers), with no copy/move distinction beyond the dialog's displayed text. Passing `verb="Copying"` alone would have shown a copy-looking dialog while silently deleting the originals.
  - **Real fix**: added a `keep_source: bool = False` param to `TransferWorker`. When true: the `os.rename` fast path is skipped entirely (a rename can't leave the source behind, so even same-volume copies now do a real byte copy), the final `os.remove(source)` is skipped, and `_rollback` (now an instance method, was `@staticmethod`) deletes the partial/finished destination file instead of moving it back to the source (since the source was never touched). Renamed the public entry point `run_move_transfer` → `run_file_transfer(..., keep_source: bool = False)` to reflect it now does both (its only real call site was `RecordOpsController.move_records_by_paths`; updated it and its test's patch target).
  - **One real UX decision, asked and answered**: what happens to files already copied to the destination if the user cancels partway through? **Decided: delete them too** — cancelling a copy means nothing happened, matching Move's own existing philosophy ("a cancel or error never leaves a half-moved item behind," now documented for both modes in `TransferWorker`'s class docstring).
  - **`RecordOpsController.copy_records_by_paths`** (backing both `copy_selected_records_to_destination`/"Copy to recent destination" and `batch_copy_records`/"Copy to folder") rewritten from a plain per-file loop into the same `TransferItem`-batch shape `move_records_by_paths` already used, calling `run_file_transfer(..., verb="Copying", keep_source=True)` — so both of the user's named entry points ("copy to folder", "copy to recent") now get the real progress+cancel dialog. No undo stack entries are pushed for copies (copying doesn't remove anything from the view, so there's nothing to undo — matches the pre-existing single-record `copy_record_to`'s behavior, confirmed by reading it first). Copy failures now report via `QMessageBox.warning("Copy Failed", ...)`, matching Move's existing failure-reporting shape.
  - **Tests**: 3 new direct `TransferWorker` unit tests in `tests/test_char_transfer.py` (same-volume copy leaves the source and does a real byte copy, not a rename; cancel-mid-copy deletes the destination and leaves the source alone; a failed item in a copy batch rolls back only the destination, other items still succeed) and 2 new `MainWindow`-level tests in `tests/test_char_move_delete_undo.py` (a copy batch routes through `run_file_transfer` with `keep_source=True`; a copy failure reports a warning and counts only the successes).
  - **Verified**: full suite 1564 passed (up from 1559 — the 5 new tests), only the 2 known-pre-existing-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` tests failed; reachability clean (0 orphan modules, 0 unused imports, 1 pre-existing unreferenced method, unchanged from baseline).
- **Slices 2-4, done together (2026-10-01):** the user chose to finish the whole work item in one pass rather than scope each slice separately. Four decisions were asked and answered first: confirmation applies to **both** Move and Copy (not just Copy); the confirmation is a **simple text dialog** ("Copy 3 image(s) to <folder>? Continue?"), not a thumbnail grid like WI-6.2's Apply AI Decisions — this is a routine, frequent action, not a rare one-shot one; the completion dialog with "Open Destination" shows **only for batches of 2+** (a single file keeps the existing status-bar-only behavior); and the RAW+JPG choice lives as a **checkbox in the confirmation dialog, remembering the last choice** via a new `TRANSFER_INCLUDE_COMPANIONS_KEY` setting (default `True`, preserving today's always-bundled behavior until the user actually unchecks it once).
  - **New `image_triage/ui/transfer_dialogs.py`**: `confirm_transfer(...)` (a `QMessageBox` with a `QCheckBox` for "Include paired RAW/JPEG and sidecar files", Yes/No) and `show_transfer_complete(...)` (a `QMessageBox` with an "Open Destination" button alongside OK, calling the existing `shell_actions.open_in_file_explorer` — reused, not reinvented).
  - **`RecordOpsController.copy_records_by_paths`/`move_records_by_paths`** gained an `include_companions: bool = True` param: when `False`, each `TransferItem` carries only `(record.path,)` instead of the full `window._record_paths(record)` bundle (stack variants, companions, sidecars). Default `True` keeps every other existing caller (`_apply_ai_culling`, drag-drop) completely unaffected.
  - **New `RecordOpsController._confirm_and_transfer(records, destination_dir, *, mode)`** is the shared confirm → transfer → completion flow, now used by all four of the user's named and related entry points: `batch_copy_records`/`batch_move_records` ("Copy/Move to folder," file-dialog-driven) and `copy_selected_records_to_destination`/`move_selected_records_to_destination` ("Copy/Move to recent destination" — reachable from both the context-menu submenu and the command palette's recent-destination commands, so both get the fix for free). Drag-drop and single-record context-menu actions are deliberately untouched — picking a destination that way is already a visible, deliberate choice.
  - **Tests**: a dedicated `tests/test_transfer_dialogs.py` (4 tests) pins the dialog functions directly — the checkbox default actually seeds the returned choice, "No" means not confirmed, the "Open Destination" button calls `open_in_file_explorer` with the right path, "OK" alone opens nothing. 4 more tests in `tests/test_char_move_delete_undo.py` cover the controller-level flow: declining makes no file changes, accepting runs the transfer and persists the companions choice to settings, unchecking companions transfers only the primary file and leaves a real sidecar (`.xmp`) untouched at the source, and the completion dialog is skipped for a single file.
  - **Verified**: full suite 1571 passed, only the 2 known-pre-existing-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` tests failed (one run also hit the previously-identified `test_char_batch_rename_controller.py` ordering flake — re-ran in isolation, passed, confirmed unrelated). Reachability clean (0 orphan modules, 0 unused imports, 1 pre-existing unreferenced method, unchanged from baseline).
  - **WI-4.7 is closed in full.**

### Phase 5: Editor and integration

**WI-5.1 Editor undo/redo.** *UX change . M* (Needs D12)
- **Finding:** E1. The popout shows permanently disabled Undo/Redo ("not available yet").
- **Approach:** Investigate: recipes (`EditRecipe`) are replaced immutably, so global adjustments snapshot cleanly. Masks, retouch, background and crop mutate a session dict and need a clear scope. Start with recipe history; extend by D12. Until it ships, hide the disabled buttons.
- **Validate:** Round-trip tests; interaction with the render-service drop rules.
- **Done:** Ctrl+Z in the editor and enabled buttons.
- **Disposition:** Investigate first, then implement.
- **Status: DONE, minimal scope (2026-09-29).** D12 decided as "ship no undo now" (see the tracker) given an in-progress C++ editor rebuild (librawops) that makes investing in this editor's undo mechanism low-value. Took the plan's own documented fallback instead: removed the two permanently-disabled `_mockup_undo`/`_mockup_redo` `QToolButton`s from `image_triage/preview.py` (creation block, compact-mode sizing/visibility wiring, and the now-single-selector `mockupDisabledAction` QSS rules) rather than leaving dead affordances that falsely imply undo is coming. `tests/test_preview_mockup_layout.py` updated (2 stale visibility assertions removed, since the buttons no longer exist to be invisible). Reachability clean; full suite 1514 passed, only the 2 known-pre-existing-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` tests failed. Investigation findings on the two option's real feasibility/cost are preserved in D12's tracker entry for whoever picks real undo back up post-librawops.

**WI-5.2 Mask geometry parity.** *Test/infra + internal refactor . M* (Depends on 0.5)
- **Finding:** A9/E3.
- **Approach:** Add tests asserting the overlay strength field equals the renderer's for shapes under crop, rotate and flip, then decide if the overlay can call the renderer's function. Keep the interactive low-resolution path.
- **Disposition:** Investigate first.
- **Status: DONE (2026-09-27)** — see the dated write-up below ("WI-5.2 done: mask geometry parity"). Reframed from "two rasterisers" to one shared rasteriser with a single intentional divergence (the guide image under crop/rotate); parity-pinning tests added; accepted as a tradeoff, not a bug.

**WI-5.3 What "Edited" means, and downstream use of edits.** *Behavioural + UX . M-H . EDITS, EXPORT* (Needs D3)
- **Finding:** U4, E2.
- **Staged:** (1) a cheap flag for "has an editor recipe" from the sidecar folder listing; (2) a separate badge or filter; (3) thumbnails rendered with the recipe, cached by recipe hash; (4) opt-in "apply edits" for resize/convert/handoff/PocketDrop.
- **Risk:** High for exports (a wrong render silently exports the wrong pixels); NAS listing cost for (1).
- **Disposition:** Decision first, then staged.
- **Unblocked 2026-09-29 — D3 decided: unify.** See D3's tracker entry. Proceeds through the plan's full 4-stage approach ((1)-(4) above), not the lean "Adjusted flag" shortcut.
- **Status: DONE (2026-09-29).** Implemented in two passes: a foundation (data-model unification + a new headless, mask-aware render function), then three consumers built on it (thumbnails, export paths, PocketDrop).
  - **`has_edits` unified**: `image_triage/edit_storage.py::session_has_edits(image_path)` — a cheap existence check (no JSON parse unless a sidecar is actually found) that returns True only when the session's `operations` or `masks` list is non-empty (opening the editor with no changes does not count). `ImageRecord` (models.py) gained `has_editor_session: bool`; `has_edits` is now `bool(edited_paths) or has_editor_session`. Populated at scan time in `scanner.py` (both `ImageRecord` construction sites), via a deferred import to avoid an existing `scanner`↔`edit_storage` cycle.
  - **New headless render pipeline**: `image_triage/edit_render_headless.py::render_edited_image(source_path, *, target_size=None) -> QImage | None` — reuses the existing `CpuEditorRenderBackend` (previously only reachable from the interactive editor and `editor_copy.write_edited_copy`'s Save Copy flow). The session→render-input assembly logic (`masked_adjustments`, mask-group helpers, `_mask_source_size`) was extracted out of the live `PhotoEditorPanel` into free functions in a new `image_triage/edit_session_geometry.py`, which the panel now calls internally instead of duplicating — both the live editor and headless callers share one implementation. Background-blur/lens-blur use new pure cache-path lookups (`subject_masks.subject_mask_cache_path`, `depth_maps.depth_map_cache_path`) that check for an already-computed ML matte/depth file without ever triggering fresh inference from a headless call; if not cached yet, that one effect is silently skipped for that render (global + mask adjustments still apply) rather than blocking on a model run inside a thumbnail/export loop.
  - **Real pre-existing gap found, out of scope, flagged for a future item**: `background_mode`/`background_amount`/`background_color`/`lensblur_amount`/`lensblur_focus` are not actually persisted to the saved session schema at all (`photo_editor_panel.SESSION_OPS` has no entries for them) — a saved session can never carry a background/lens-blur setting today, so the new headless spec-builders for those two effects are correct and forward-compatible but currently unreachable in practice. Not fixed here; would be its own small work item if wanted.
  - **Thumbnails**: `ThumbnailKey` (cache.py) gained an `edit_state` field (the sidecar's own `(mtime_ns, size)`, or a `(0,0)` sentinel when there is none) so an edited photo's thumbnail is correctly invalidated when its edits change; `THUMBNAIL_CACHE_VERSION` bumped 4→5 (one-time full cache invalidation). `ThumbnailTask` renders through `render_edited_image` when a sidecar exists, falling back to the untouched fast path (`load_image_for_display(..., prefer_embedded=True)`) for unedited photos and for any render failure.
  - **Export paths**: `image_resize.py` (used directly, and reused by `workflows/export.py`) and `image_convert.py` all now check for a sidecar before their existing load step and render through the new pipeline when one exists (full resolution, not the thumbnail's downscaled path), falling back to the original file on any failure so a corrupt sidecar can't fail a batch export. Confirmed `tests/test_char_batch_plans.py`/`test_production_workflows.py`'s existing byte-identical-copy characterization tests still pass verbatim (none of their fixtures write sidecars).
  - **PocketDrop, opt-in**: new `APPLY_EDITS_TO_POCKETDROP_KEY` setting (off by default, in Settings next to the existing "AI tags in grid" toggle from WI-6.1). When on and a selected record has real edits, `_send_selection_to_pocketdrop` renders a temp JPEG and sends that path instead of the original; off, or no edits, or a failed render, sends the original file exactly as before. PocketDrop's native `pd_add_paths` call has no per-transfer completion callback, so temp renders go to a dedicated `PocketDrop/EditedExports` scratch folder with an opportunistic 24h-age cleanup sweep before each send, rather than deleting on an uncertain completion signal.
  - **Known limitation, accepted rather than built out**: saving edits in the popout editor does not push an immediate refresh to the main grid's record list (the editor's save path and the grid's `MainWindow._records` list aren't currently wired together) — the "Edited" filter/badge and thumbnails pick up new edits on the next natural folder rescan, not instantly. Building live cross-widget refresh plumbing for this was judged separate, speculative work beyond this WI's scope.
  - **Verified**: full suite 1554 passed (up from the 1514/1517 baseline as new tests were added across both passes), 1 skipped, 12 xfailed, only the 2 known-pre-existing-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` tests failed — confirmed independently by the coordinating session, not just the implementing agents. Reachability clean throughout (0 orphan modules, 0 unused imports, 0 unreferenced methods).

**WI-5.4 Click-select add/subtract.** *UX change . L-M* (Depends on 5.2)
- **Finding:** E6. The engine already accepts labelled points.
- **Disposition:** Planned.
- **Investigated 2026-09-29, implementation deferred pending a design decision.** The engine side is a non-issue: `labels: list[int]` (SAM's native 1=include/0=exclude convention) is already threaded end-to-end and untouched from `prompt_masks.ensure_prompt_mask` down through `PromptMaskTask` → `MaskEngineService.infer_prompt` → the worker → `sam_worker.SamEngine.segment` — nothing there needs to change. The gap is entirely UI/interaction: `MaskOverlay.mousePressEvent` only handles left-click, emits a label-less `point_picked = Signal(float, float)`, and every click currently spawns its own independent single-point, always-positive `PromptMaskTask` — there is no existing modifier-key/right-click handling to repurpose (genuinely blank slate, not dead code to flip on), and no point markers are ever drawn on canvas (so visual include/exclude feedback is new UI, not a recolor).
  - **The real fork, left for you to decide:** (a) a true multi-point session — accumulate a growing points+labels list feeding *one* SAM call, so a negative click carves out part of an existing selection (the actually useful SAM UX, but requires touching the session-accumulation state machine: `_prompt_session_active`/`_prompt_session_root_id`, the hover-preview cache, and `_prompt_meta`'s point storage, which currently has no label slot); vs (b) just varying the label on each independent single-point click (mechanically much smaller — extend the two signals and their ~4 call sites — but a lone negative point with no accompanying positive point means nothing to SAM, so this is a weaker feature that might not actually be useful on its own).
  - Note the codebase already has an unrelated `combine: "add"|"subtract"` concept (compositing whole separate mask *components*/bitmap layers) that is easy to confuse with this finding's "labelled points" (refining *one* SAM query with mixed +/- points within a single call) — they are different mechanisms; the plan's E6 finding is about the latter.
  - Not implemented this pass — picking the wrong one of (a)/(b) risks either a confusing, functionally-useless feature or throwaway session-state work. Revisit once you're back to pick a direction.
- **Status: DONE (2026-09-29).** You chose (a), true multi-point session, with right-click as the subtract gesture.
  - **Signal/input layer** (`image_triage/ui/mask_overlay.py`): `point_picked` now carries a third `label: int` arg (1 = include, 0 = exclude). `mousePressEvent` restructured so right-click, when click-to-select is armed, emits `point_picked(x, y, 0)`; every other interaction (handles, create tools, scene picking) stays left-click-only exactly as before. Added visual feedback that didn't exist at all previously: `set_state(prompt_points=...)` accepts the active selection's accumulated points, and a new `_paint_prompt_points` draws small green/red dot markers (include/exclude) at each one; the existing point-pick hint text now reads "right-click to exclude part of it" once a selection exists.
  - **Session model** (`image_triage/ui/photo_editor_panel.py`): rather than unify the whole session (which the investigation flagged as ambiguous for disjoint multi-object selections), kept the existing "each left-click adds a new unioned component" behavior completely unchanged, and added subtract as refinement of whichever component was *most recently touched* (`_prompt_active_component_id`). A right-click appends `(point, 0)` to that component's accumulated `_prompt_meta[id]["points"]`/`["labels"]` (new field), re-runs `PromptMaskTask` with the full accumulated list, and replaces that one component's bitmap in place via the existing `_update_bitmap_mask_asset` — the same in-place-overwrite mechanism "Refine Edges" already used, reused rather than reinvented. New methods: `_handle_overlay_subtract_point`, `_start_prompt_subtract_task`, `_handle_prompt_subtract_finished`/`_handle_prompt_subtract_failed`. `_refine_next` (the "Refine Edges" queue) now also passes `labels=` when replaying a component's stored points, so a previously-subtracted region doesn't silently get re-included on refine. Session subtitle click-count switched from `len(group members)` to an explicit `_prompt_session_click_count` counter, since a subtract no longer creates a new group member to count.
  - **Coordinate-space bug caught during implementation, not by a test:** `_prompt_meta` stores points normalized to 0..1, but `MaskOverlay._to_display` expects source-pixel coordinates — the first draft of the `prompt_points` wiring in `mask_overlay_state()` would have silently drawn every marker in the top-left corner of the image. Caught on a careful re-read before running anything, fixed by scaling normalized points back to pixels using `_mask_source_size()` before handing them to the overlay.
  - **Also fixed in passing (unrelated to this WI):** 3 test files (`test_popout_editor_masks.py`, `test_mask_panes.py`, `test_semantic_masks.py`) still imported `from photo_terminal...` (the bare pre-vendoring style) — a leftover WI-4.6 miss, caught when the full suite run surfaced a collection error. Updated to `from image_triage.photo_terminal...`.
  - **Tests:** added 3 new tests to `tests/test_scene_regions.py::ScenePanelWiringTests` (a bare right-click with nothing selected is a no-op; a right-click after a left-click selection accumulates the correct points/labels and calls the subtract task; `_handle_prompt_subtract_finished` updates the component in place and records the new points/labels) — no existing test exercised any of this new code path. Full suite: 1517 passed (3 more than the 1514 baseline — the new tests), 1 skipped, 12 xfailed, only the 2 known-pre-existing-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` tests failed. Reachability clean.

### Phase 6: AI experience (after decisions and cleanup)

**WI-6.1 AI visibility.** *UX/behavioural . M* (Needs D1; depends on 2.3-2.5, 3.3)
- **Finding:** I1, N6.
- **Approach:** Implement the chosen option; for (A) an opt-in setting that turns `set_show_ai_annotations` on, plus auto-loading saved results. Keep the manual-clean default.
- **Disposition:** Decision, then fix.
- **Status: DONE (2026-09-29, achieved earlier via WI-3.3, never cross-referenced).** Both halves of the D1(A) approach already exist and were verified working:
  - **The opt-in setting**: `SHOW_AI_TAGS_IN_GRID_KEY` (`window.py:1994`), added in WI-3.3's 2026-09-27 write-up, off by default (`window.py:2841`), wired straight into `grid.set_show_ai_annotations(...)` (3 call sites: `window.py:2842`, `10352`, `19901-19902`) — this is the "manual-clean default" requirement, and it's a real toggle, not the dead `_ui_mode == "ai"` check WI-3.3 found and replaced.
  - **Auto-loading saved results**: a pre-existing background loader (`_schedule_hidden_ai_results_load`/`_start_hidden_ai_results_load`/`_handle_hidden_ai_results_loaded`, `window.py:16295-16374`, originally built to support instant Manual↔AI tab flips before that mode was retired) runs unconditionally on every folder open (called from `RecordsViewController.handle_scan_cached`/`handle_scan_finished`/`handle_scan_children`) and silently populates `self._ai_bundle` from any saved AI results/catalog cache for that folder — with no UI mode switch, exactly as N6's "do not reintroduce a mode switch" note requires. Once populated, the grid displays tags whenever the opt-in setting is on; nothing further was needed.
  - **Why this was missed**: the auto-load mechanism predates D1 and was built for a different, now-retired purpose (the Manual/AI tab), so nothing in the codebase or prior write-ups connected it to WI-6.1's own checklist entry — a documentation cross-reference gap, not a code gap. No new code was written for this pass; this write-up exists to close the item and correct the checklist.

**WI-6.2 Apply AI Decisions.** *Behavioural . H . FILES, ANNOT* (Depends on 4.1, 6.1)
- **Finding:** I2.
- **Approach:** Show a reviewable list, run through the file-operation service with progress and cancel, and make it a single undoable batch.
- **Disposition:** Refactor, then behavioural change.
- **Status: DONE (2026-09-30).** Its two dependencies turned out to already be satisfied (WI-4.1 was fully done 2026-09-27 but never got a status line on its own header — fixed as a doc-lag correction above; WI-6.1 done 2026-09-29), and the "single undoable batch" half of I2 was already built as part of WI-4.1c (one shared `batch_id` across the AI-Pick move and Reject recycle). The two real remaining gaps:
  - **Reviewable list, as a thumbnail grid (your call over a plain filename list)**: new `ApplyAIDecisionsDialog` (`image_triage/ui/apply_ai_decisions_dialog.py`) replaces the old count-only `QMessageBox.question`, showing the actual AI-Pick and Reject photos as thumbnails grouped under "AI Pick → _winners (N)" / "Reject → Recycle (N)", reusing the main grid's shared `ThumbnailManager` (cache-hit first, async request on miss) rather than freezing the dialog decoding hundreds of images synchronously. Keeper/Needs-Review counts stay as plain text (they aren't moving).
  - **Progress + cancel for the winners move, not the recycle move (your call, matching existing recycle-is-fast precedent elsewhere in the app)**: the AI-Pick → `_winners` loop now goes through the same `transfer_progress.run_move_transfer` path ordinary drag-drop/batch moves already use (via `RecordOpsController.move_records_by_paths`, which gained an optional `batch_id` param so it can join the Apply-AI-Decisions batch instead of minting its own) — real progress dialog, real cancel, safe for NAS-scale winners moves. Reject → recycle is untouched: still a fast per-path loop, no dialog, sharing the same batch_id.
  - **Cancel-partway correctness verified**, not just assumed: a new test cancels after 1 of 3 winners files actually moved and confirms the moved one is gone from source/visible at destination/undoable, the other two are completely untouched on disk and in the grid, the reject loop still completes normally, the status message reports only the real count, and one Undo reverses the whole mixed batch cleanly.
  - **Verified**: confirmed independently (not just by the implementing pass) — full suite 1560 passed, only the 2 known-pre-existing-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` tests failed; reachability clean (0 orphan modules, 0 unused imports, 0 unreferenced methods). Every other `move_records_by_paths` caller (drag-drop, manual batch move) confirmed unaffected — no `batch_id` argument at those call sites, so they still mint their own as before.

**WI-6.3 AI entry-point consolidation.** *Investigation . M* (Needs D9)
- **Finding:** I5. About 35 AI actions and 3 overlapping dialogs.
- **Approach:** Map what each dialog owns, pick canonical flows, hide the rest from menus first (reversible), delete later.
- **Disposition:** Investigate first.
- **Unblocked 2026-09-29 (D9 decided: park)** — the actual investigation/consolidation work itself is still open; D9 only resolved the ambiguity of whether AI v4 would be adding new entry points to account for (it won't, while parked).

Presented the findings and 4 real judgment calls to the user rather than guessing:
  - **Duplicate "Run" entry point** (`run_ai_culling` "Run Review" toolbar button vs. `open_ai_workflow_center` "AI Workflow Center..." menu item — same slot, different label/icon): **kept the menu item, hid the toolbar button.** Removed `run_ai_culling` from `WORKSPACE_TOOLBAR_DEFAULTS["ai"]` (window.py) only — it stays in `WORKSPACE_TOOLBAR_ALLOWED_ITEMS` so a user can still add it back via toolbar customization, and stays palette-reachable (`ai.run_pipeline`). Fully reversible, per the plan's "hide first" approach.
  - **Guided AI Cull** (genre-preset picker that feeds the same Cull & Score pipeline, with its own "Open Workflow Center" escape hatch): **kept as its own entry point**, no change — judged to add real value (genre-tuned defaults) distinct from the Workflow Center.
  - **Taste Calibration and Dispute Current AI Decision**: **declared dead features by the user and removed outright** (not hidden) — `TasteCalibrationDialog` (whole file), its action/slot/palette entry, `build_calibration_pairs`/`CalibrationPair` (sole consumer was the wizard); the `dispute_current_ai_result` action/slot/palette entry (its underlying chord-based dispute path, `_handle_dispute_label_requested`/`grid.dispute_label_requested`, is a separate mechanism and is untouched and still live). `review_ai_disagreements` (an independent filter-navigation action, not a correction mechanism) was explicitly confirmed out of scope and left alone.
