Flagged as its own possible future item if the user still wants it gone, not removed unilaterally on this pass.
  - **Backend/CLI boundary respected**: confirmed `GlobalAdapterLabelStore`, `aiculler_db_path`/`global_aiculler_db_path`, `latest_adapter_model_version`, `load_adapter_status_summary`, and `delete_adapter_model` are still used by the dispute-label flow, Apply AI Decisions, quick-rerank, or have dedicated backend test coverage independent of the removed GUI actions — kept, not deleted. Checked the standalone `aiculler/` CLI directly: none of the removed names appear there.

Verified via `git diff`/`git show HEAD` that this was a genuine new orphan introduced by this pass (not pre-existing baseline noise, which the removal agent's own report had incorrectly claimed for it), confirmed zero remaining references anywhere, and removed both methods (~85 lines). Reachability back to its 1-item pre-existing baseline (`_register_child_process`, confirmed present in committed `HEAD` already, untouched by this work).

### Phase 7: UX vocabulary and docs

**WI-7.1 Discoverability.** *UX . L*
- **Finding:** U1. The menu bar is hidden by design; about 16 actions are in no menu.
- **Approach:** Ensure every action is in the palette; consider labelled entries for the orphan actions. Keep the hidden menu (a deliberate v5 decision).
- **Status: DONE (2026-09-29), with one caveat found along the way.** An investigation cross-referenced every one of the ~106 `MainWindowActions` entries against both the hidden menu bar (`image_triage/ui/menus.py`) and the command palette (`CommandPaletteController.build_commands`) — the original "~16 orphans" count was stale (most had since been absorbed into one or the other); **10 genuine orphans remained**, all AI-related: 7 completely unreachable AI-adapter-training actions (`download_ai_model`, `open_ai_data_selection`, `train_ai_ranker`, `train_ai_ranker_from_global`, `evaluate_ai_ranker`, `score_ai_with_trained_ranker`, `review_ai_adapter_labels` — reachable only via internal `.setEnabled()` calls, no shortcut, no menu, no toolbar default) and 3 reachable only if a user manually customizes their workspace toolbar to include them (`taste_calibration`, `dispute_current_ai_result`, `review_ai_disagreements`). Added all 10 to `CommandPaletteController.build_commands` under the existing "AI" section, following the established `add_action_command(...)` pattern — the palette already skips disabled actions automatically, so these simply appear once each action becomes enabled.
  - **Caveat found while verifying:** `dispute_current_ai_result`'s enablement check (`window.py:11022-11029`) requires `self._ui_mode == "ai"`, which can never be true again since the 2026-09-19 AI Review mode retirement (`_set_ui_mode` forces manual unconditionally — see `docs/ai_mode_retirement.md` item 4, which already catalogs this exact case as "disconnected, needs a decision"). So this one palette entry is harmless but cosmetic — the action itself stays permanently disabled regardless of where it's registered, and fixing that for real means deciding whether/how disputing an AI result should work in manual-only mode, which is out of scope for a discoverability pass and belongs with the other `ai_mode_retirement.md` disconnected items instead. The other 9 additions are genuinely functional once their existing enablement conditions are met (verified `taste_calibration` and `review_ai_disagreements` are not `_ui_mode`-gated).
  - Did not touch the hidden menu bar (the "consider labelled entries" half of the approach is optional and left for later — deciding submenu placement is a separate, more involved change than registering a palette command) and did not resolve the `download_ai_model`/`install_ai_runtime` duplicate-label overlap the investigation flagged, since that's a product judgment call (is `download_ai_model` even still a distinct user-facing action, or legacy?) rather than a mechanical fix.
  - Reachability clean; full suite 1514 passed, only the 2 known-pre-existing-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` tests failed.
- **Disposition:** Planned.

The "Settings presets" leg turned out to be a non-issue in the UI: `settings_dialog.py::WorkflowPreset` is a real internal class, but its only user-facing label is already "Profile preset" (renamed from "Session preset" under D5/WI-3.4) — the word "Workflow" never reaches the user for that concept, so per this plan's established "UI-label rename only" policy (D8's precedent) there was nothing to change there; the internal class-name similarity to `workflows/models.py::WorkspacePreset` (a different, unrelated settings-vs-layout pair) is a developer-facing naming wart, not a user-facing one, and was left alone rather than doing a code rename beyond this item's "label only" scope. Kept "workflow" in search keywords alongside "export" so existing muscle memory still finds things. No internal class/key names changed (`WorkflowRecipe`, `WorkflowPreset`, `recipe_key_for_name`, etc.), matching D8's precedent. Verified: no test referenced any of the old strings; full suite 1558 passed (the one extra failure beyond the 2 known-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` tests was `test_char_batch_rename_controller.py`'s previously-identified ordering flake — re-ran in isolation, passed 3/3, confirmed unrelated); reachability clean (0/0/1, the 1 being the same pre-existing baseline item from before this change).

### Phase 8: Performance and reliability polish

- **WI-8.1 Toolbar rebuild and startup.** *Performance . M.* Finding: P5, P6. Profile the roughly 12 call sites of `_rebuild_topbar_action_stack` (472 ms max on the UI thread); skip rebuilds when nothing changed. Independent of 4.4.
  - **Measured first, 2026-10-02 (read-only pass, real `MainWindow` on an offscreen 2560x1440 screen, sandboxed settings/data, 87-rebuild realistic session).** The audit's numbers were from before most of this effort's refactors and did not hold up: there are **7** rebuild call sites, not ~12; the worst single rebuild measured was **62 ms**, not 472 ms (the 472 ms is plausibly GIL contention from scan/metadata worker threads running Python code during a real session — a rebuild makes ~74k PySide calls that each release and re-take the GIL; reproduced as 45 -> 57/61/72 ms with 1/2/4 busy GIL-releasing decode threads, and unbounded with a busy pure-Python thread — it never reproduced on a quiet process). `__init__` is ~1,211 lines (audit: 1,301). Findings that did hold: (1) **~85% of every rebuild (37 of 42 ms) was one function**, `_trim_icon_transparency`, a pure-Python 64x64 `QImage.pixelColor()` loop (73,728 calls per rebuild across 18 buttons), unmemoized; (2) **52% of rebuilds were redundant** (44-45 of 87 produced widgets visually identical to the ones they replaced — 38 of 39 calls from `_apply_layout_ratios`, which fires on every layout pass); (3) a **latent bug masked by those redundant rebuilds**: `_update_action_states()` pushes checked state for `compare`, `auto_advance`, `burst_groups`, `burst_stacks`, `show_hidden_folders` (and grid/details/zen) under `QSignalBlocker`, so `action.changed` never fires and checkable topbar buttons go stale (action checked, button not) until an incidental rebuild happens to heal them — invisible in the default layout (it contains no checkable items) but real for user-customised layouts; (4) **two per-rebuild leaks**: 18 popup `QMenu`s parented to `MainWindow` and never deleted (`app.allWidgets()` 2047 -> 3613 over 87 rebuilds, app-wide `setStyleSheet` ~10% slower), and `action.changed` lambdas that were never disconnected (+10 handlers per rebuild); (5) startup (~2.0 s `__init__`, 3.3 s to a settled first frame) is dominated by the eagerly built `FullScreenPreview` (~1.45 s of it: a 1,119-widget tree plus the app-wide stylesheet repolish it triggers, ~770 ms).
  - **Stage A DONE (2026-10-02), low-risk fixes, independently verified (full suite 1648 passed, 0 failed; reachability 0/0/1 baseline).**
    - **Icon trim**: `_trim_icon_transparency` now computes the alpha bounding box with numpy over a `Format_Alpha8` plane instead of the pixel loop. Pixel-identical to the old loop (checked across 37 `QImage` formats x 5 cases x 4 sizes before adopting it, then pinned permanently by `tests/test_topbar_icon_trim.py`, which keeps a verbatim copy of the old implementation and asserts old == new over every `WORKSPACE_TOOLBAR_FLUENT_ICONS` icon, nav icons, color variants, null/transparent/single-pixel/ring/non-square/random edge cases, padding 0-40 and -5, plus a >=5x speed guard). Steady-state forced rebuild: **~42 ms -> 4.7 ms**; `tests/perf_baselines.py` gained a loose rebuild budget (20 ms vs 4.7 measured).
    - **Rebuild change-guard**: `_rebuild_topbar_action_stack(mode=None, *, force=False)` skips when an input key (visible slot count, manual/ai slot tuples, `_toolbar_profile()`, `_theme`, `_toolbar_placement`, `_ui_mode`) equals the previous build's key AND the previously built widgets are verifiably still intact (`_topbar_stack_intact()`: counts match, every widget still in its grid, no `RuntimeError` from a deleted widget). Live action state is deliberately *not* in the key (popup menus are built from shared actions and follow state live; the icon cache isn't either, which is why theme/icon refresh forces). Everything stays synchronous — no timer/coalescing was introduced, because the resize path documents that deferring layout caused a visible launch jump. Only the `_apply_layout_ratios` call site (the source of 38 of 39 redundant calls) runs un-forced; the other six (placement change, initial build, overflow, display-profile change, themed-icon refresh, workspace-toolbar mirror) pass `force=True` — each audited individually (the overflow site's own visible-count check is already its change detection; the icon-refresh site just cleared a cache the key can't see; etc.). Scenario: 85 rebuild requests -> 47 actually rebuilt, 38 skipped at ~2.8 ms total.
    - **Sync step (fixes the latent stale-checked bug rather than just no longer masking it)**: new `MainWindow._sync_topbar_action_buttons()` walks both pages' action-backed buttons through the existing `_sync_topbar_action_button_for`, called at the end of `_update_action_states` and after the blocked `compare_mode`/winner-ladder/AI-group-compare/burst-lock pushes (~0.3 ms per call). `test_blocked_checked_state_pushed_by_update_action_states_reaches_the_buttons` puts all six checkable items in both pages and drives them through the real `_update_action_states`; **verified to fail without the fix** (temporarily removing the sync call produced `compare (manual): button is stale (action checked, button not)`) — so it also proves the bug existed before this change, not just that the guard would have introduced it.
    - **Leaks fixed**: popup menus are re-parented to their button (passing `menu.windowFlags()` — a plain `setParent` would reset `Qt::Popup`; a test checks `isWindow()` and that the menu still pops), and the `action.changed` lambdas became a module-level `_TopbarActionSync(QObject)` parented to the button, so its connection dies with it. Over 87 forced rebuilds with a real event-loop turn each: QMenu growth **+1566 -> +0**, `allWidgets` **+1566 -> +0**, `changed` handlers per storm 126 -> 996 growing -> **10 flat**, `app.setStyleSheet` no longer drifts. All three leak tests fail when the leaks are reintroduced.
    - **Same-theme guard on `FullScreenPreview.apply_theme`**: skips the restyle when the studio layout is active and `(theme, dialog height, ids of panes)` equals the key recorded at the end of the last *successful* application (those are the only inputs `_apply_studio_theme` actually consumes: the theme — hence gamma — via histogram/pane painting, the dialog height via the type-size-scaled stylesheet, and the panes it restyles; interface size/display profile reach the dialog through `apply_display_profile`, not the stylesheet). The preview used to apply the identical studio theme twice at startup (once in its own init, once from `MainWindow._apply_appearance`, ~230 ms); a real-`MainWindow` startup test now asserts exactly once (it was twice). 6 of its 7 tests fail with the guard disabled.
    - **Numbers (scratch scenario, original code vs now)**: total rebuild time over the 87-call scenario **2878 ms -> 275 ms**; mean per rebuilt **33.9 -> 5.9 ms** (max 10.1); `MainWindow.__init__` median **2163 -> 1784 ms cold, 1976 -> 1653 ms warm** (-379/-323 ms); menus on the window after the scenario 1649 -> 117; stale-widget check (live bar vs a forced fresh rebuild after each of 18 phases) identical every time. 38 new tests across `tests/test_topbar_icon_trim.py`, `tests/test_preview_theme_guard.py`, `tests/test_topbar_rebuild_guard.py` (+ the perf baseline).
    - **Left as observations, not fixed**: the preview guard only helps when the incoming theme equals the preview's own (true for the default Slate theme; a different saved theme still pays ~230 ms because the studio stylesheet *text* doesn't depend on the theme but `apply_theme` still re-runs — a stylesheet-text-equality check would close that); `_apply_studio_theme` sets the identical stylesheet twice on first run (once itself, once via `_apply_mockup_metrics`); each theme *switch* costs ~1.3 s inside `app.setStyleSheet` (Qt re-polishing the whole tree — inherent to Qt, not something this item touches).
  - **Stage B DONE (2026-10-02): lazy `FullScreenPreview` construction, the ~1.45 s startup cost.** Independently verified (full suite 1660 passed; the only failures were the 2 known-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` tests plus the previously-identified batch-rename ordering flake, re-confirmed 3/3 passing in isolation; reachability 0/0/1 baseline).
    - **Design**: `MainWindow.preview` is now a lazy property backed by `self._preview` (set to `None` at the very top of `__init__`), so the ~96 existing references keep working unchanged and only *genuine use* forces construction. A non-forcing `_preview_if_built()` and `_preview_is_visible()` serve everything that only configures or inspects. All ~96 reference lines were classified: 51 genuine use (stay on the property, force the build), 10 push window state into the viewer (now non-forcing — display profile, theme, review shortcuts, the auto-advance/compare/auto-bracket toggles, the Settings-dialog apply, collection browse mode), 13 existence/visibility checks (now non-forcing), 22 init wiring (moved into the builder).
    - **Builder** (`_build_preview()` / `_configure_new_preview()`): constructs the viewer, publishes `self._preview` *before* configuring it (so a re-entrant access can't recurse; if configuration raises it resets to `None` and `deleteLater()`s), then applies the window's *current* state in the old startup order — the 16 signal connections, photoshop/auto-advance/preload/auto-bracket setters, review shortcuts (via a new shared `_push_review_shortcuts()` that `_apply_shortcut_overrides` also uses), the preview-parented Ctrl+K `QShortcut` (new `CommandPaletteController.attach_preview_shortcut()`, copying the main shortcut's key/enabled state; `setup_shortcuts` now only creates the main one), `apply_display_profile`, `apply_theme` (a no-op via the stage-A guard when the theme already matches), and a replay of compare mode / collection-browse mode, which the window can enter while no viewer exists. Compare *count* is deliberately not replayed — `_open_preview` sets it on every open.
    - **Non-obvious sites**: `_handle_preview_filmstrip_thumbnail_ready` fires on every thumbnail while a folder loads and had to be non-forcing; `_open_preview`'s first line is non-forcing so opening a folder card doesn't build the viewer (the real use later in the function forces it); `_maybe_open_startup_quick_view` is deliberately left forcing (it only reaches the viewer in quick-view mode, which needs it anyway, and an existing test fakes the window for it).
    - **Quick-view launch** needed no special code: `_maybe_open_startup_quick_view` -> `_open_preview` -> `self.preview` builds synchronously when the image is found; a test with a real `MainWindow(launch_target=..., quick_view=True)` proves the viewer stays unbuilt until the image opens and is visible afterwards.
    - **Idle pre-build, opt-in from the app entry point only**: `MainWindow.schedule_deferred_preview_build(delay_ms=800)` starts one window-owned single-shot timer; no-op if already built or already scheduled; when it fires it builds only if `self._preview is None and self.isVisible()` (a window closed/hidden in the meantime is skipped; a destroyed window takes its timer with it). Called once from `main()` after the window is shown, guarded by `if not quick_view` — never from `__init__`/`showEvent`, so unit tests that construct and show windows never get a surprise ~600 ms build mid-test. The 800 ms is a tunable guess; the build can land while the first folder is still loading (a scan-in-progress gate + re-arm would fix that — not added, to keep it simple).
    - **Numbers** (2560x1440 offscreen, 5 interleaved fresh-process runs each, median; HEAD from a `git archive` copy, "before B" from a snapshot of the stage-A tree taken before any B edit): `MainWindow.__init__` warm **2022 (HEAD) -> 1641 (stage A) -> 427 ms** (-79% vs HEAD), cold **2187 -> 1842 -> 616 ms**; widgets after construction **2140 -> 2129 -> 960**; `show()` 76 -> 27 ms; first loop pass 481 -> 199 ms. The cost moved, it didn't vanish: first `window.preview` access ~600 ms (build) + ~196 ms (open) — so a user who presses Space within ~1.4 s of launch pays ~0.8 s instead of ~0.2 s, but one who waits for the idle pre-build gets the same ~190 ms first open as before; later opens are 20 ms either way.
    - **Equivalence evidence**: the viewer was dumped from the eager (stage A) tree and the lazy tree in three scenarios (defaults; seeded non-default preferences/overrides; settings changed after startup — theme, gamma, Settings dialog, auto-bracket, compare, shortcut overrides, collection mode). Identical in all three: all 76 simple attributes, the Ctrl+K shortcuts, the receiver counts of all 16 signals, the viewer stylesheet, and every per-widget stylesheet. The only differences are in hidden, never-shown state (placeholder 640x480 geometries because no layout/resize reached the hidden viewer, 10 extra `FilmstripThumb` placeholders, un-reset analysis-panel label text, a combo-popup enabled flag) — first open re-lays everything out, which is why first-open cost is identical either way.
    - **Tests**: new `tests/test_preview_lazy_build.py` (15 tests) — a tripwire (`FullScreenPreview.__init__` patched to record-and-raise) stays quiet through `__init__`, `show()`, theme/gamma/display-profile/resize changes, `_update_action_states`, shortcut overrides, the three toggles, opening a folder, selection/rating changes, the command palette, collection mode, zen mode, the Settings dialog and window close (with a positive control proving the wire works and leaves no half-built viewer); property/idempotence/closed-window/never-shown/destroyed-window semantics; `main()` opts in only for non-quick-view launches; equivalence replay (settings changed *before* the build, then asserted on the built viewer) and the reverse (changed after); the late-viewer shortcut; signals-reach-the-window; quick-view. Two existing tests changed because the contract changed, not because behaviour was weakened: `test_main_window_startup_restyles_the_preview_only_once` now asserts 0 studio-theme applications at startup and exactly 1 when the viewer is built, and `test_collection_mode._ModeHost` gained `_preview_if_built()`.
    - **A trap worth recording**: `QApplication.setStyleSheet` repolishes every live widget, and late in a full test run thousands of leaked widgets from earlier tests make one apply take *minutes* (it hung the first draft of the tripwire test). The new tests patch the app-wide stylesheet/palette to no-ops (the viewer's styling is per-widget). The pre-existing `test_main_window_startup_restyles_the_preview_only_once` takes ~100 s of the ~4-minute suite for the same reason — a candidate cleanup (give it the same app-style no-op), left alone as out of scope.
    - `app.aboutToQuit.connect(shutdown_mask_engine)` now happens at build time instead of startup — harmless, the service also registers an `atexit` hook.
  - **Not done (all measured small after the fixes above, deliberately left)**: lazy/removed never-shown AI topbar page (~5 ms/rebuild now), suspending rebuilds during `__init__` (~12 ms), deferring the AI-runtime status calls (~70 ms: `default_ai_workflow_runtime()` and `_refresh_ai_runtime_preferences` run WMI/distribution lookups for a retired mode), the hidden legacy workspace-bar widgets (~23 ms), numpy-ifying `_fluent_filled_icon` (~110 ms across 14 icons under cProfile), `_apply_display_profile`'s provisional-size double rebuild, the first-run double `_apply_default_workspace` (~25 ms). Module import (~755 ms) was not broken down. First-run DB schema creation (~175 ms) is inherent.
  - **Overall (P5/P6): `__init__` ~2.0 s -> ~0.43 s warm; a toolbar rebuild ~42 ms -> ~5 ms with ~half as many happening.**
- **WI-8.2 NAS strategy for watch and refresh.** *Investigation . M . NAS.* Finding: P9. `QFileSystemWatcher` does not observe shares; decide on polling.
- **WI-8.3 Silent exception audit.** *Internal refactor . L.* Finding: P8 (250 broad excepts, 15 silent in window). Log instead of swallow, one module at a time.
  - **Status: DONE (2026-10-01), scoped to `window.py` (the audit's own specific target).** A read-only investigation first found something more fundamental than "add log lines": **this codebase had zero error-logging infrastructure at all** — `perf_logger()` is an opt-in JSONL *timing* stream gated behind a Settings toggle, not a general error logger. "Log instead of swallow" required building that infrastructure from scratch, which the user approved.
  - **New `image_triage/app_logging.py`**: `configure_app_logging()` attaches a `logging.handlers.RotatingFileHandler` (5 MB, 3 backups) to the root logger, called once from `main.py` before any other setup, always on, independent of the Diagnostics → Performance Logging toggle. The log file (`errors.log`) lives alongside the existing `perf.py` log directory (`performance_log_dir()`), reusing that convention rather than inventing a new location. After configuration, any module uses the ordinary `logging.getLogger(__name__)` pattern with zero further setup.
  - **~24 except blocks fixed in `window.py`**, in every case *only* adding a log call inside the existing `except` — no fallback value, control flow, or user-facing behavior changed anywhere: the native-frame install/maximize/restore fallbacks (a whole UI feature or window-chrome path silently turning itself off with no trace); `_write_child_sync_state`'s `OSError` (desyncs the popout editor's child process invisibly); both `_restart_app_for_development` swallows (directly relevant to the "zombie GPU process" bug that code exists to prevent); the post-`missing_ok=True` cleanup catch; the AI Review entry point's dispute-badge load (today indistinguishable from "nothing was ever disputed"); a grid scroll-into-view failure; and the full ~15-20-site AI/adapter-integration cluster (`_refresh_adapter_status_indicator`, winner scores, face records, image categories, prefilter decisions, ingested-paths, the adapter-version lookup, `_recompute_user_label_bucket_overrides`) where a genuinely broken adapter DB and "this folder just has no AI data yet" previously looked identical — the fallback-to-empty/None behavior is unchanged, there's just a trace now when it's a real failure. The two explicitly-intentional swallows (`_save_aiculler_global_label`/`_save_aiculler_global_reason_tags`, which already had a design comment explaining why the global DB being unwritable is tolerated) got a `debug`-level log, not `warning` — consistent with "intentional, not a bug signal."
  - **9 sites deliberately left untouched**, confirmed genuinely fine by the investigation: well-understood Qt lifecycle races (`RuntimeError: wrapped C/C++ object has been deleted`, several already carrying their own explanatory comments), an opportunistic PocketDrop temp-file sweep explained in its own docstring, an enum-matching probe loop where failure is the expected/common case, a cleanup-of-a-cleanup with no consequence either way, and a few other low-stakes already-tolerant fallbacks.
  - **Out of scope, flagged for a future pass, not done here**: the investigation's codebase-wide hotspot scan found `ai_probe.py` (~12 `except Exception` sites) and `imaging.py` (~11) as the next-highest counts after `window.py` — not deep-dived, since `ai_probe.py` sits in the same AI-integration surface as the fixed cluster and could plausibly have the same "broken DB looks like no data" shape on the other side of that boundary.
  - **Tests**: new `tests/test_app_logging.py` (4 tests) — the log directory is created if missing, `logger.exception(...)` actually writes to `errors.log`, `configure_app_logging()` is idempotent (no duplicate handlers on a second call), and the logger works identically regardless of whether the opt-in performance-logging toggle is on or off (proving independence from that system).
  - **Verified independently, not just by the implementing pass**: full suite 1575 passed, only the 2 known-pre-existing-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` tests plus the previously-identified `test_char_batch_rename_controller.py` ordering flake (re-confirmed in isolation) failed. Reachability clean (0 orphan modules, 0 unused imports, 1 pre-existing unreferenced method — `MainWindow._register_child_process`, confirmed present on the clean committed baseline too, not a regression from this work).
- **WI-8.4 Renderer/GPU seam.** *Deferred.* Finding: E4; future.

## 6. Sequencing logic and parallelism

- **Phase 0 must finish first.** Without a green, hermetic, CI-gated baseline nothing after it can be judged.
- **Phase 1 can run alongside Phase 0**, once 0.5 has the tests for 1.2. 1.1, 1.3 and 1.4 are independent.
- **Phase 2 comes before Phase 4** because removing dead AI code (about a quarter of `MainWindow`) makes the decomposition seams visible and shrinks what has to be tested.
- **3.2 (shortcuts) and 3.3 (settings)** must precede any UI/vocabulary change, so labels and keys change once.
- **4.1 precedes 6.2** (Apply AI) and 4.2.
- **Editor work (5.1, 5.2, 5.4) is largely independent of `MainWindow`** and can proceed in parallel with Phases 2-4, gated only by 0.5 and the mask tests.
- **Deliberately postponed:** organisation rename and data relocation (3.1 rename, 3.6), GPU renderer, AI explanation chips, thumbnail rendering with edits. (Records-view/scan decomposition, previously listed here as the last slice of 4.4, was completed 2026-09-29 — see WI-4.4's dated write-up.)

## 7. Dependency map

```
Phase 0  0.1 -> 0.2 -> 0.3 -> 0.4 (CI gate)
                 \--> 0.5 (characterization + harness) --+--> 1.2, 3.4, 4.1, 5.2, 2.3
         0.6, 0.7 (baselines, backups) -> before any data/settings work (3.x)

Decisions  D2 -> 2.4 -> 2.5 -> 2.6 -> 4.6
           D6 -> 1.1 -> 3.2 -> (7.2)
           D10 -> 3.1 -> 3.5, 3.6
           D5 -> 3.4, 7.2      D1 -> 6.1 -> 6.2, 6.5, 4.5
           D4 -> 4.1c          D3 -> 5.3        D12 -> 5.1
           D9 -> 6.3, 6.4      D11 -> 4.4       D13 -> 2.8

Cleanup    2.1 -> 2.3 -> 2.4 -> 2.5 -> 2.6      2.7 -> 3.3
Structure  0.5 + 2.x + 3.2 -> 4.1 -> 4.2 -> 6.2
           4.1 + 2.x -> 4.4 (AI slice -> toolbar -> file ops -> catalog -> records-view)
Editor     0.5 -> 5.2 -> 5.4       5.1 (independent)       D3 -> 5.3
```

Two rules follow from the map: **nothing in Phase 4 or later starts before 0.5**, and **2.6 (packaging) never starts before 2.5 and a working full-suite CI**.

## 9. Recommended starting point

**Start with WI-0.1, then 0.2 and 0.3 together, and begin 0.5 in parallel.** In other words: get to a green, hermetic, CI-gated baseline, and write characterization tests for winner sync, moves and undo before anything else.

Why this order:
1. It resolves the failures introduced during the session, so the baseline is honest.
2. It costs nothing in behaviour and unlocks safe, provable changes everywhere else.
3. The first *behavioural* change should be **WI-1.2 (winner un-mark safety)**, because it is the highest data-loss risk and 0.5 will already cover it.
4. WI-1.1, the `Ctrl+Alt+P` collision, is a good early win once D6 (which command keeps the key) is decided.

**Decisions needed before Phase 2 or later:** D2 (legacy engine and DINO), D6 (shortcut scope and the `Ctrl+Alt+P` owner), and D4 (winner copy semantics). D1, D3 and D5 can wait until Phases 5-6, but they block those items.

No code was modified while producing this plan. The only actions were reads (code, registry, AppData), a test run, and stopping two hung test processes that had been launched for the audit.

Preloading PIL plugins fixed it, so nothing is quarantined.

  - `decision_harvest` x6: **not schema drift**. Windows keeps the sqlite file locked after `with sqlite3.connect()` until GC, so the test temp dir cannot be deleted. Repaired in the test module only. See N18.

### New findings (continued, not fixed)
- N18: **Every `with sqlite3.connect(...)` in the codebase leaves the database file locked on Windows until a GC cycle** (Python 3.13: the connection is in a reference cycle, and the context manager commits but does not close). It affects `DecisionStore` (9 sites), `CatalogRepository`, `LibraryStore` and others. Real effects: a DB file cannot be deleted, moved or replaced (backup, migration, "reset cache") right after use. Relevant to WI-3.x (data/migration) and any reset/restore path; fix once with a shared `closing()` connection helper. Not fixed here.
- N19: the two `run_ai_pipeline` stub tests and the AI-run/training/load/reset ones cover AI-mode code that was retired on 2026-09-19; they are candidates for deletion with D2 rather than rewriting in WI-0.5.

### WI-1.2 Winner un-mark safety: DONE (2026-09-26, uncommitted)
- Investigation (read-only, from the catalog database, no NAS access): 6 `_winners` folders are cataloged; the 75 files in them all match their parent folder by name and size. No user-added files were found, so the new rule strands nothing that exists today.
- Change (`window.py`): un-marking now deletes `_winners/<name>` only if it is provably Image Triage's own artifact: a symlink resolving to the source, the same file (hard link), or a copy with the same size and a modification time within 2 s of the source. Otherwise the file is left alone, and the status bar says `Winner removed: <name> (left <file> in _winners: not a copy Image Triage made)`. If the source no longer exists the copy cannot be proven, so it is kept. `_sync_winner_copy(_for_paths)` now returns the names it kept.
- Behavioural consequence to know about: a copy whose source was modified after marking is now left behind (with the message) rather than deleted. Edits made in the popout do not modify sources, so this should be rare.
- Tests: the audit hazard test was inverted, and five provenance tests plus a status-message test were added. Mutation check (treat everything as ours): 4 tests fail. Suite: 1531 passed, 5 skipped, 15 xfailed, 0 failed.
- Not changed: reject/undo/AI-apply callers still ignore the returned "kept" list (no message there), and the async/error-notice behaviour is still WI-4.1c.

### Decisions received (2026-09-26, second batch)
- D2 confirmed: **delete the legacy engine, no archive branch or tag.** It was deliberately dead and is meant to be unrecoverable. WI-2.4 therefore deletes with no safety branch; the ledger (WI-2.1) still records what was removed and the evidence it was unreferenced.
- D14: **require a verified hash.** No checksum, no install.

### WI-1.3 Updater integrity: DONE (2026-09-26, uncommitted)
- Investigation: the live feed (`api.github.com/repos/tylermcm/ImageTriage/releases/latest`) publishes `digest: sha256:...` on its MSI asset, so requiring it does not block the current release.
- Change (`updater.py`): `UpdateInfo.is_verifiable` (well-formed 64-hex SHA-256). `download_update_installer` now **refuses before any network call** when it is not, and always verifies the hash after download (mismatch still deletes the partial file). `window.py`: when an update is found but unverifiable, the prompt explains why and links the release page instead of offering a download that would fail.
- Tests: no-checksum is refused with zero network access and no files, malformed checksums are unverifiable, a GitHub payload without a digest is unverifiable, and the window prompt refuses or offers accordingly. Mutation check (removing the guard) fails the test. Suite: 1536 passed, 5 skipped, 15 xfailed, 0 failed.
- Behavioural consequence: a manifest feed (`IMAGE_TRIAGE_UPDATE_FEED_URL`) or an older release with no digest can no longer be installed through the updater. Assets uploaded before GitHub began computing digests would need re-uploading.
- Finding N26: the latest release is tagged `V2` while its asset is `Image.Triage-2.1.1-win64.msi`, and the app is 2.2.1. The updater compares the tag digits, so `V2` reads as 2.0 and would never be seen as newer. Future releases need a `vMAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` tag. Not a code defect, but easy to trip on.

### WI-2.4 pre-implementation investigation (2026-09-26, read-only, no code changed)
Findings that adjust the plan's approach (scope unchanged, method refined):
1. **`dino_prefilter.py` is not wholly dead; it is shared prefilter infrastructure.** `phash_prefilter.py` (live: the pHash cost reducer to preserve) imports `DINOPrefilterDecision`, `_bool_value`, `_clamped_score` and `_preserve_duplicate_group_representatives` from it, and `filtering.py` uses `DINOPrefilterDecision` for the "Prefilter dumped" filter. So the module cannot simply be deleted: the shared decision type and duplicate-representative logic must first be moved to a neutral module (for example `prefilter_common.py`, DINO naming dropped), and only the DINO-specific parts removed (`run_dino_prefilter_from_signal_rows`, `DINOPrefilterSettings`, the audit and report writers, the settings page). Order: extract shared, repoint importers, delete the DINO run stage.
2. **The DINO run is genuinely dormant.** `aiculler_workflow` still accepts `dino_prefilter_settings` and passes `dino_enabled`, but the settings dialog hard-codes `enabled=False`, so the stage never runs. The Filter menu still lists DINO/prefilter entries, enabled only when a folder already has `dino_prefilter` rows on disk.
3. **`FilterMode.DINO_REMOVED/RESCUED` are safe to remove.** `_enum_from_value` falls back to `All` for an unknown value, so an old saved filter cannot crash on load. Your registry export contains no saved filters and no "dino" strings. The plan's "keep the enum values until confirmed" precaution is therefore unnecessary; still update `filtering.py`, `menus.py`, the three `window.py` sites and `tests/test_filtering.py` together.
4. **Existing per-folder data**: `.image_triage_ai/dino_prefilter/*` artifacts on disk must be left untouched (only stop reading them if the filters go). `AI_PREFILTER_DUMPED` is fed by pHash decisions too, so that filter stays.
5. **Dead AI-training stack confirmed**: the 12 task classes in `ai_training.py` (`PrepareLabelingCandidatesTask` ... `BuildReferenceBankTask`, about 1,300 lines) have no importer left after WI-2.3 (only `tests/test_ai_training.py` touches `LaunchLabelingAppTask` and two helpers). `TrainRankerDialog`, `ranker_manager_dialog` and `ai_training_progress_dialog` are exported from `ui/__init__.py` but only the progress dialog is instantiated (by `window.py`), so removal order matters. Keep `global_adapter_labels.sqlite` and registered training sources untouched, as planned.
6. **Recommended sub-steps** (each ends with the full suite green): (a) remove unreachable `window.py` AI methods and the 12 `ai_training.py` tasks with their tests; (b) delete the three dormant dialogs and their `ui/__init__` exports; (c) extract the shared prefilter module and repoint `phash_prefilter`, `filtering`, `aiculler_workflow`; (d) remove the DINO run stage, settings page, filter entries and enum values. Validate with the reachability report, an AI run on a small folder, and pHash prefilter tests.

### What still says "dino" and why it is a different job
About 40 mentions remain in `window.py`, `ai_runtime_packages.py`, `ai_manifest.py`, `ai_model.py`, `ai_env.py`, `ai_probe.py`, `packaging/ai_runtime_installer.py`, `scripts/refresh_ai_runtime_lock.py`, `freeze_support.py`, `README.md` and about 40 in tests. These are **not a feature any more**; they are the AI runtime package installer where "dino" is the name of the optional PyTorch/transformers stack (`include_dino`, `--no-dino`, `dino_enabled_variants`, `-base` versus full lock files, the `DinoV3` model bundle, `dino_installed_variants`). The stack is also what the editor masking engines need, so the fix is a **rename plus a migration**, not a deletion: `include_dino` becomes something like `include_torch_stack`, and the persisted `dino_enabled_variants` key and existing lock-file names must keep working for runtimes already installed on machines. It changes what gets installed and how the MSI stages it, so it needs a real MSI build and the clean-machine matrix; that is why it belongs to WI-2.5/2.6 packaging, not here.
- Also noted: `_delete_phash_prefilter_artifacts` is now unreferenced. Its only entry point was the dead DINO step, so pHash artifacts have had no working delete button all along (pre-existing). Decide whether to wire it up or delete it.

### DINO wiped from the source (2026-09-26, uncommitted; MSI build not run)
Your instruction: no DINO anywhere in the code. Result: **`git grep -i dino` finds nothing in `image_triage/`, `packaging/`, `scripts/`, `freeze_support.py`, the CI workflows, README or the website.** What remains is only two test guards (see below) and dated historical documents.
- **The runtime installer's "dino" was really the PyTorch stack.** Renamed throughout: `include_dino` to `include_torch`, `dino_installed_variants` to `torch_installed_variants`, `dino_enabled_variants` (the persisted metadata key) to `torch_enabled_variants`, `AI_RUNTIME_DINO_*` to `AI_RUNTIME_TORCH_*`, the installer flag `--no-dino` to `--no-torch`, and the lock-refresh script flags `--no-dino` / `--dino-only` to `--no-torch` / `--torch-only`. No compatibility shim: metadata without the new key already defaults to "every installed variant has the PyTorch stack", so an existing standard runtime keeps working. **Edge case:** a runtime that was deliberately installed as base-only (`--no-dino`) will be seen as missing its PyTorch parts and offered a reinstall.
- **The DINOv3 model itself deleted:** its manifest bundle and checksums (1.2 GB weights), its capability and the `OPT_IN_CAPABILITIES` concept, the DINO default resolver and constants in `ai_model.py` (the generic `download_ai_model` and installation type stay: other models use them), the DINO probe path in `ai_probe.py` (the whole unreachable tail of `probe_torch_model`), the `AIWorkflowRuntime.model_name` / `model_installation` fields, and in `window.py` the DINO download request, setup-flow flags (always False), status methods and wording.
- **Also removed as dead:** 7 unreferenced methods flagged since WI-2.3 (five prompt-text helpers, `_path_state_cache_token`, `InspectorPanel._quick_button`). Website copy no longer advertises a DINO prefilter (it now says "Duplicate prefilter").
- **Tests:** DINO fixtures renamed; 4 tests deleted (default-DINO resolution x2, base-runtime DINO capability guard, settings-page absence guard); `test_ai_model` builds its installations directly. **Kept on purpose:** the two `assertNotIn("DINO", ...)` UI guards in `test_current_ai_workflows` and the test that a saved filter containing `"DINO Removed"` falls back to All (that one protects users' old saved filters). Suite: **1455 passed, 3 skipped, 12 xfailed, 0 failed.**

### New finding from the MSI acceptance test (2026-09-26): AI model path mismatch blocks real culling
**Not caused by WI-2.4/2.5/2.6.** Confirmed pre-existing by tracing the resolution paths and inspecting disk state after installing the built `Image Triage-2.2.1-win64.msi` and opening AI setup.

- **Symptom:** "Set Up AI" reports the GPU runtime and all three culling models (CLIP/TOPIQ/InsightFace) installed, but the main AI panel shows "Runtime: Unavailable" / Blocked, with the note pointing at a missing `TinyCLIP-ViT-8M-16-Text-3M-YFCC15M-ONNX/onnx/model.onnx`.
- **Root cause:** two independent "where do managed AI models live" schemes have diverged:
  - `ai_paths.py` / `ai_model_store.py` (current manifest-backed store, used by Set Up AI and every `resolve_*_model_installation()` call): `%USERPROFILE%\.image-triage\AI\models\...`. Verified: TinyCLIP, TOPIQ and the face pack are all genuinely installed there with valid `.bundle.json` metadata.
  - `aiculler_workflow.py::_default_aiculler_model_root()` (used by `default_aiculler_runtime()`, which backs the actual run and `aiculler_runtime_status()` in the main panel): tries `%LOCALAPPDATA%\image_triage_ai_cache\models\CLI-Culler` **first**, and returns it as soon as the directory merely *exists* — it never checks whether the specific required file is inside. On this machine that legacy folder still exists with stale contents from years ago (an old `clip-vit-large-patch14` folder, `faces/models/buffalo_l`), so the fallback chain stops there and never reaches the correct managed root.
- **Effect:** a real AI cull cannot run in the shipped app until this is fixed, even though every model is actually downloaded and verified.
- **Fix sketch (not applied):** make `_default_aiculler_model_root()` prefer `ai_paths.managed_models_root() / "CLI-Culler"` (the same root the manifest store and Set Up AI already use) ahead of the legacy `LOCALAPPDATA` cache path, or better, check that the *specific* required file exists in each candidate rather than just the directory. Needs a decision on whether to migrate/delete the legacy `LOCALAPPDATA\image_triage_ai_cache` leftovers (this is exactly the kind of dual-location drift `[[reference_store_python_appdata]]` already warned about, but for models rather than settings).
- **Status:** logged only, per your instruction. Not fixed. Recommend a dedicated work item before or alongside WI-3.x (data/paths), since it blocks the acceptance gate for WI-2.6 (a real AI cull on the installed MSI) until resolved.

Verified the underlying methods (`_open_file_associations_dialog`, `_open_keyboard_shortcuts_dialog`, `_reset_window_layout`) stay reachable through real menu actions; only `_show_workspace_toolbar_editor` had no other caller. `preset_save_callback` was checked too and is genuinely used (kept).

### WI-3.1 Settings identity and registry inventory: DONE (2026-09-27, uncommitted)
- **D10 decided:** migrate off "Codex", with cache pinning (option A from the plan), not keep-and-document. Org name for the *registry* identity: "Image Triage", single-level (`HKCU\Software\Image Triage`, no doubled `Image Triage\Image Triage` segment) — your explicit ask.
- **Scope choice:** `QCoreApplication.setOrganizationName("Codex")` in `main.py` is left as-is for now. It drives two different things: (a) the default `QSettings()` constructor, and (b) `QStandardPaths` (thumbnail cache, `decisions.sqlite3`, `AppLocalData`). This work item only touches (a). Moving (b) is real user-data migration (3.2 GB cache, the annotations DB) and stays scoped to WI-3.5/3.6, matching N4's warning that the two are coupled and shouldn't be done in the same step.
- **New module `image_triage/app_identity.py`:** the single settings accessor.
  - `user_settings()` — Windows: constructs `QSettings(r"HKEY_CURRENT_USER\Software\Image Triage", QSettings.Format.NativeFormat)` directly (verified this gives exactly `HKCU\Software\Image Triage` with keys directly under it, not Qt's usual org/app two-level path, and not the `...\OrganizationDefaults` suffix an organization-only constructor produces). Non-Windows falls back to `QSettings("Image Triage", "Image Triage")`.
  - `legacy_settings_sources()` — read-only handles to the two old identities: `("Codex", "Image Triage")` and the stray `("ImageTriage", "ImageTriage")` `ui/shortcuts.py` used.
  - `migrate_legacy_settings_once()` — copies every legacy key into the current store on first run (marker-gated, so it's a cheap no-op after that), never overwriting a key already present in the new store. Called from `main.py` and from `MainWindow.__init__` (the latter so tests that build a `MainWindow` directly, bypassing `main.py`, still get it).
- **Consolidated 6 call sites onto it:** the 4 bare `QSettings()` constructions (`window.py`, `preview.py`, `pocketdrop/panel.py`, `ui/photo_editor_panel.py`) and the stray `ImageTriage/ImageTriage` identity in `ui/shortcuts.py` (deferred import, to keep that module loadable standalone outside the package, per its own docstring constraint). Removed the now-unused `QSettings` imports.
- **`packaging/image_triage_cleanup.py`** (the uninstall/reset script) updated to also delete the new `Software\Image Triage` registry tree, alongside the still-present old `Software\Codex\Image Triage` and `Software\ImageTriage` trees (so a full uninstall or `--mode uninstall` cleans up whichever identity a given install actually used).
- **Hermeticity:** the direct-registry-path construction is a call shape `tests/conftest.py`'s sandbox didn't anticipate (it only intercepted the two-arg org/app form). Extended `_HermeticQSettings.__init__` to also redirect any `HKEY_...`-prefixed first argument into its own file inside the sandbox. Added `test_direct_registry_path_qsettings_never_touches_the_real_registry` to `test_hermetic_environment.py` to pin it.
- **Regression found and fixed during validation:** `test_preview_mockup_layout.py` and `test_pocketdrop.py` used a bare `QSettings()` themselves to seed/clear state before constructing `FullScreenPreview`/`PocketDropPanel` — which now read from a *different* store than the test was clearing, since those classes moved to `user_settings()`. Caused 2 real (reproducible, not flaky) failures: a 1px layout mismatch and a stale-state click-target test. Fixed by switching both test files to `user_settings()` too. `test_char_view_state_and_shortcuts.py::test_the_registry_store_uses_a_different_settings_organisation` pinned the old bug (two different identities) as expected behaviour; rewritten to `test_the_registry_store_now_shares_the_main_settings_identity`, asserting they now match.
- **`tests/harness.py`** (`prepare_application()`) now clears `user_settings()` and every legacy source between tests, instead of the bare `QSettings()` it used to clear (which, after this change, is no longer what the app reads).
- New `tests/test_app_identity.py`: round-trip, migration copies legacy keys, migration never overwrites a value already in the current store, migration is a no-op after the first run.
- Updated `docs/manual_smoke_test.md`'s registry-export step to the new single path, with a note on why the two old exports are now only useful for diagnosing the migration itself.
- **Tests:** full suite **1458 passed, 3 skipped, 12 xfailed, 0 failed.** Reachability report: 0 unused imports, 0 unreferenced methods (the new module's exports are all referenced).

### WI-3.5 Thumbnail cache policy: DONE (2026-09-27, uncommitted)
- **Chose option A (lean first):** cap by size with LRU eviction, keep the existing path+size+mtime keying (option B, content-only keying, stays a separate future step per the plan).
- **Cap: 8 GiB**, your call — current usage is 3.2-3.4 GB, so the cap sits well above today's size and nothing gets evicted immediately; it only bites once real growth outpaces it. Deliberately left as a hardcoded constant (`DEFAULT_THUMBNAIL_CACHE_MAX_BYTES` in `cache.py`) rather than a settings-dialog value: it's a self-healing number nobody needs to tune day-to-day, and exposing it would be scope creep into WI-3.3. Trivial to promote to a setting later if that changes.
- **`DiskThumbnailCache` (`image_triage/cache.py`):** added `max_bytes`, `enforce_size_cap()` (globs the cache dir, sums sizes, and — only if over the cap — deletes oldest-by-mtime files until back under it; returns bytes freed) and background-thread-triggered, throttled eviction: a non-blocking sweep fires once at construction (so an existing over-cap backlog trends down over time, not only after 200 new saves) and again every 200th `save()` after that. A non-reentrant lock (`_eviction_lock`, `acquire(blocking=False)`) skips triggering a second sweep if one is already running.
- Mtime-as-recency is a deliberate simplification: since cache keys already bake in path+mtime+file-size, revisiting an unchanged photo is always a load (cache hit), never a rewrite — so a file's on-disk mtime is genuinely "last time this thumbnail was newly created," a reasonable LRU proxy without a separate access-time index.
- **Tests:** new `tests/test_thumbnail_cache_eviction.py` (no-op under cap, oldest-first eviction, stops once back under cap, background eviction completes and releases its lock without blocking `save()`). Full suite: **1462 passed, 3 skipped, 12 xfailed, 0 failed.**

### WI-3.6 Data placement: migration executed and verified (2026-10-01)
- **Context:** the 2026-09-27 investigation below recommended deferring the full migration as high-risk, but also flagged a separate, real finding (N26). The user explicitly chose to do the full migration now rather than wait, after being shown the risk and the two-tier option (small N26-only fix vs. the full migration).
- **Non-negotiable safety rules set before any code was written:** take a fresh backup first (the existing 2026-09-26 one was 5 days stale relative to live data); copy old data, never move or delete it, so every old location survives indefinitely as an implicit backup; never overwrite anything already at a destination (idempotent); verify every copy (SQLite `PRAGMA integrity_check`, or SHA-256 for plain files) and only make it visible at its real destination via an atomic rename after verification passes; deliberately exclude the multi-GB thumbnail cache (safe to lose, rebuilds on first launch) from migration entirely.
- **Fresh backup**: `C:\Users\tylle\ImageTriage-baselines\data-backup-2026-10-01\` — `decisions.sqlite3`, `library.sqlite3`, `catalog.sqlite3`, `global_adapter_labels.sqlite`, for both the Store-Python dev path and the real/frozen-app path (8 attempted, 7 existed and all passed `integrity_check=ok`; the 8th, the real-context `global_adapter_labels.sqlite`, doesn't exist — see the divergence finding below, not caused by this work).
- **New `image_triage/app_data_migration.py`**: `migrate_legacy_app_data_once()`, built on the exact same copy-only/marker-gated/never-overwrite shape as WI-3.1's `app_identity.migrate_legacy_settings_once()`. Reconstructs the old org="Codex" `AppDataLocation` path directly from the `APPDATA` env var (Qt's own documented Windows formula) rather than by flipping `QCoreApplication`'s global org name and back — a global-state mutation at startup was judged more fragile than reconstructing the path. Migrated: `decisions.sqlite3`, the per-folder `ai_training` adapter-label cache, `safe-trash` (pending-restore deleted files), plus — merged from the separate hand-rolled identity below — `library.sqlite3`, `catalog/`, the global `ai_training` adapter workspace, `scan-cache/`, `share_packages/`, `share_queue.sqlite3`. All land under one new unified root: `%APPDATA%\Image Triage\Image Triage\`.
- **N26 fixed in the same pass**: `aiculler_global_store.py::_default_user_data_root()` (hand-rolled `USERPROFILE`/`APPDATA` joining to a hardcoded `"ImageTriage"` folder, bypassing `QStandardPaths` and the org-name question entirely) now delegates to `scan_cache.app_data_root()`, which itself was switched from hand-rolled env-var joining to real `QStandardPaths.AppDataLocation`. This incidentally collapsed two *slightly different* hand-rolled implementations (one honored the `IMAGE_TRIAGE_APPDATA` test override, one didn't) into one correct one.
- **`main.py`**: `QCoreApplication.setOrganizationName("Codex")` → `"Image Triage"`, with `migrate_legacy_app_data_once()` called right after `migrate_legacy_settings_once()`, before `QApplication` construction — matching the required ordering (org name set, then migrate, then anything else touches the data). `packaging/image_triage_cleanup.py` (the opt-in uninstaller) updated with the new path so a future uninstall still cleans up correctly.
- **Real migration executed and verified on this machine's live data, not just tested in the abstract**: 807 files copied in the Store-Python dev context, 0 failed; 3 files copied in the real/non-virtualized context (the only 3 the plan targets there — see below), 0 failed. **Independently re-verified by the coordinating session, not just the implementing pass — and re-verified a second time after the session's first verification pass turned out to be reading a virtualized view (see the correction above)**: using a plain, non-packaged interpreter (`.msi_build_venv\Scripts\python.exe`), `decisions.sqlite3` is byte-identical old vs. new (106,496 B, 89 rows, `integrity_check: ok`, both sides); `library.sqlite3` (7,983,104 B) and `catalog.sqlite3` (55,779,328 B) at the new unified location both match the true pre-migration baseline exactly, with `integrity_check: ok`. A second migration run is confirmed a true no-op (`already_migrated=True`).
- **Correction (2026-10-01, later the same day): the "divergence" below was a false alarm — a tool artifact, not data loss.** The original pass reported `ai_training/` gone, `library.sqlite3` 7.9 MB → 86 KB, `catalog.sqlite3` 55 MB → 10 KB at the real/frozen-app path, flagged as a worrying unrelated finding. A dedicated re-investigation found the real cause: reading that path through any Microsoft Store-packaged process (a Store-distributed PowerShell 7, and separately confirmed for this session's own `ls`/Git-Bash — both silently redirected by Windows' per-package AppData virtualization to a private virtual-store view) returns exactly these small/missing numbers for a file that is, on disk, genuinely large and intact. Reading the identical literal path with a plain, non-packaged interpreter (`.msi_build_venv\Scripts\python.exe` — no package identity, so Windows applies no redirection) gives `library.sqlite3` = 7,983,104 B, `catalog.sqlite3` = 55,779,328 B, `global_adapter_labels.sqlite` = 598,016 B — matching the 2026-09-26 baseline exactly (the labels file even grew slightly, consistent with 5 days of normal use, not loss). All three re-verified `PRAGMA integrity_check: ok` through the trusted interpreter, independently confirmed twice (once by the re-investigation, once by the coordinating session itself after the first correction disagreed with its own `ls`-based recheck). **No data was ever lost; nothing needs recovering.** Lesson for future checks in this environment: any file-size/existence check against this app's real (non-Store-virtualized) data path must go through a plain, non-packaged executable — not PowerShell, not Git-Bash/`ls` — or it will silently report phantom shrinkage/missing files that don't reflect reality.
- **Also found, not part of the original scope, left alone**: `chatgpt_panel/` under the old Codex `AppDataLocation` (~20 MB, an embedded QtWebEngine browser profile/cache for an in-app chat panel) — looks like cache/session data, not product data; not migrated, not investigated further. `scan-cache/`, `share_packages/`, `share_queue.sqlite3` were migrated (real user content, including phone-share zips dated as recently as 2026-09-13) but no current code path was found that reads them back — flagged as possibly orphaned from a retired feature, not a consequence of this migration.
- **Tests**: new `tests/test_app_data_migration.py` (12 tests: copies into an empty destination; never overwrites existing data; never touches/modifies the old location; idempotent on a second call; a simulated corrupted SQLite copy is caught and never made visible, then succeeds on retry; the equivalent for plain-file hash mismatches), `tests/test_scan_cache.py` (2 tests), plus 1 new test in `tests/test_aiculler_global_store.py` pinning that its data root now equals `scan_cache.app_data_root()`.
- **Verified independently**: full suite 1591 passed, only the 2 known-pre-existing-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` tests failed. Reachability clean (0 orphan modules, 0 unused imports, 1 pre-existing unreferenced method, unrelated to this change).

### WI-3.6 original investigation (2026-09-27): migration deferred, per the plan's own "High risk / investigate first" disposition
- **Measured live, both execution contexts, 2026-09-27** (not just the audit-time snapshot):
  - **Store-Python dev environment** (how this session and day-to-day dev work run the app): Windows applies per-package AppData virtualization. `%APPDATA%`/`%LOCALAPPDATA%` physically resolve under `...\Packages\PythonSoftwareFoundation.Python.3.13_qbz5n2kfra8p0\LocalCache\{Roaming,Local}\...`. All of today's live data is here: `decisions.sqlite3` (89 rows), `global_adapter_labels.sqlite` (594 rows, 2.3 MB `ai_training` folder), thumbnail cache (3.4 GB).
  - **The frozen/installed MSI app** is an ordinary Win32 executable (built via `.msi_build_venv` + `setup_msi.py bdist_msi`, not a Store package) — it is **not** virtualized, so it reads/writes the genuine `C:\Users\<user>\AppData\...` path directly. This is a physically different location from the dev environment's data.
  - Confirmed both `decisions.sqlite3` (89 rows) and `global_adapter_labels.sqlite` (594 rows) are currently **byte-identical** between the two locations (row-for-row content hash matches) — no data has silently diverged or been lost between contexts as of today. But nothing keeps them in sync automatically; that's incidental, not structural.
  - The real (non-virtualized) path's `ai_training` folder is 793 MB — this is the exact stale-copy figure N3 already flagged, confirmed still present and still stale relative to the live 2.3 MB virtualized copy (old bulk workspace/artifact data from before this became a lean adapter-only store).
- **New finding (N26): a third, independent path identity.** `aiculler_global_store.py::_default_user_data_root()` doesn't use `QStandardPaths` at all — it hand-rolls `USERPROFILE`/`APPDATA` env-var joining to a hardcoded `"ImageTriage"` folder (no "Codex", unrelated to `QCoreApplication`'s organisation name and to both QSettings identities WI-3.1 unified). It's what holds `global_adapter_labels.sqlite` — irreplaceable, 594 labels — plus `library.sqlite3`, `catalog/`, and `scan-cache/`. This is a fourth settings/data identity the audit's A19/A20 findings hadn't separately named; worth its own line item, not silently folded into WI-3.1's registry work (which only covered `QSettings`) or WI-3.5/3.6's `QStandardPaths` scope (which this bypasses entirely).
- **Classification (per the plan's approach):**
  - **Irreplaceable:** `decisions.sqlite3` (annotations), `global_adapter_labels.sqlite` (594 labels), `library.sqlite3` + `catalog/` (catalog metadata).
  - **Derived** (rebuildable, but expensive/annoying to lose): `ai_training/global_adapter` workspace and artifacts, `scan-cache/`, semantic/face indexes.
  - **Cache** (safe to delete anytime): thumbnail `cache/thumbs` (now capped, WI-3.5), `cache/migration-media`.
- **Recommendation — do not implicitly relocate anything yet:**
  1. The org rename's *effect on `QStandardPaths`* (cache, `decisions.sqlite3`, `AppLocalData`) stays deferred exactly as WI-3.1 scoped it: an explicit, tested, one-time migration, not a silent path change from flipping `QCoreApplication.setOrganizationName`. The Store-vs-real-path split adds a second axis of risk on top of the org rename itself: a migration has to work correctly in *both* contexts, or it creates a third copy instead of consolidating two.
  2. `aiculler_global_store.py`'s hand-rolled path (N26) is a good, smaller, separable next step: switch it to `QStandardPaths` for consistency (matching how every other store resolves its root) with the same read-old/write-new fallback pattern WI-3.1 used for QSettings — before any org-name change, since consistency here doesn't require picking a new name yet. Not done in this pass; flagged for a future work item so it isn't rushed alongside D10's larger migration.
  3. Any future migration step should re-run this same byte-identity check (or diff row-by-row, not just counts) immediately before touching anything, since "the two copies currently match" is a fact about today, not a guarantee.
- **Disposition unchanged from the plan: investigate first, defer.** No files moved, no code changed for this work item.

### WI-3.2 Unify shortcuts: DONE (2026-09-27, uncommitted)
- **Investigated first, per the plan.** Two stores existed: `ui/shortcuts.py` ("Settings > Shortcuts", 23 per-action keys in QSettings) and `window.py`'s own `_shortcut_targets`/`ShortcutTarget` system (21 bindings, one JSON blob), opened from a separate "Keyboard Shortcuts..." dialog (`ui/keyboard_dialog.py`). 13 actions were registered in *both*, under different ID schemes (`open_folder` vs `file.open_folder`), with the window's system applied last at startup — confirming the plan's diagnosis exactly: the window dialog captured its "default" *after* the registry override already landed on the action, so its Reset restored the customised key, not the true default.
- **Bigger find during investigation:** grid.py's `keyPressEvent` hardcoded ~15 more keys in neither store (arrows, Home/End, PageUp/Down, Escape, Space, Return, Delete, Ctrl+A — navigation/destructive, out of scope by your call — plus `[`/`]`, K, M, T and the 1-5 adapter labels, which are meaningful standalone review commands). Further investigation found K/M/T/W/X *independently* hardcoded two more times, in `details_view.py` (no dynamic mechanism at all) and `preview.py` (entangled with winner-ladder mode, compare mode, loupe and zoom). You chose to extend rebinding to all three surfaces rather than leave Grid inconsistent with Details/Preview.
- **End state, matching the plan's own target exactly ("one registry ... one store, one editor UI"):**
  - **One registry:** `SHORTCUT_REGISTRY` in `ui/shortcuts.py` grew from 23 to 41 entries — the 8 window-only actions (`open_preview`, `accept_selection`/`reject_selection` now honestly defaulted to "W"/"X" instead of a runtime fallback, `keep_selection`, `move_selection`, `delete_selection`, `share_to_phone`, `open_command_palette`) plus 10 new entries for the previously-hardcoded review keys (`cycle_burst_previous`/`next`, `keep_at_cursor`, `move_at_cursor`, `tag_at_cursor`, `adapter_label_hero`/`strong`/`maybe`/`weak`/`reject`). Verified zero new collisions among all 41 defaults.
  - **One store:** the per-key QSettings scheme `ui/shortcuts.py` already had (now under the WI-3.1 identity). New `effective_shortcuts()` helper returns the override-or-default text for the 10 grid-level entries that have no `MainWindowActions` QAction (so `apply_shortcut_overrides` silently skips them, by design).
  - **One editor UI:** `ui/keyboard_dialog.py` (`KeyboardShortcutDialog`, ~185 lines) deleted. The "Keyboard Shortcuts..." action (a default pinned toolbar tool) keeps its icon, label and command-palette entry — its slot now opens Settings > Shortcuts (`_show_settings(initial_section="Shortcuts")`) instead of the second dialog.
- **grid.py/details_view.py/preview.py:** each gained a `_review_key_shortcuts: dict[str, QKeySequence]` (defaults matching what was previously literal) and a `set_review_key_shortcuts()` setter; grid's existing `set_review_action_shortcuts`/`_winner_shortcut`/`_reject_shortcut` pattern for W/X was extended to details/preview rather than replaced (kept `test_grid_interactions.py`'s existing W/X tests passing unchanged). The three `_matches_shortcut`/local duplicates were consolidated into one `keyboard_mapping.matches_shortcut()`.
- **window.py:** replaced `_register_shortcut_targets`/`_shortcut_bindings`/the old `_apply_shortcut_overrides`/`_load_shortcut_overrides`/`_save_shortcut_overrides`/`_set_action_shortcut`/`SHORTCUT_OVERRIDES_KEY`/`ShortcutTarget` with one `_apply_shortcut_overrides()`: applies the registry to every `MainWindowActions` QAction, refreshes each action's tooltip hint (previously only done for the 21 window-managed ones — Settings-page overrides could leave a stale tooltip; now consistent for all 41), computes the 10 grid-level effective values, and pushes both onto `grid`/`details_view.table`/`preview` in one place. ### WI-3.4 Annotation persistence semantics: INVESTIGATED, no code fix needed (2026-09-27, uncommitted)
- **Wrote the precedence spec by tracing the actual code**, then proved it with tests, exactly as the approach asks. `_hydrate_records_batch` (window.py): load the sidecar first (used if non-empty); load the persisted session row; the row wins over the sidecar *only if the row is non-empty*. An empty/absent row falls back to the sidecar.
- **Investigated whether "a sidecar resurrects a mark cleared in another session" (N5) is real.** Traced every write path: the normal interactive clear goes through `AnnotationPersistenceQueue`, whose worker calls `sync_sidecar_annotation` for every queued change — including empty ones — so a sidecar that already exists gets correctly emptied alongside the store row. **Conclusion: the hypothesized bug does not reproduce for ordinary use.** New tests `test_a_normal_clear_empties_the_sidecar_too_no_resurrection` and `test_a_second_session_does_not_resurrect_a_mark_cleared_in_the_first` pin this.
- **Found the one place it's structurally possible:** 4 call sites in window.py (`_decision_store.delete_annotation(...)`, all in file-delete/recycle/move flows) bypass the queue entirely, so they don't re-sync the sidecar. But the record they touch is also removed from the grid in the same action, so this is only observable again if the file comes back — e.g. an Undo restoring a deleted file. In that case the mark reappearing via the still-intact sidecar is the desired outcome of the undo, not a resurrection bug. `test_deleting_the_store_row_without_the_queue_leaves_the_sidecar_stale` documents this explicitly, with that reasoning in the test's own docstring.
- **Did not alter the schema or any persistence code**, per the plan's explicit instruction — the existing precedence logic is correct for what it needs to do.
- **D5 decided: rename "Session" in the UI.** Scope was narrow — grepped for every user-visible string containing "Session" and found exactly 3 literal occurrences plus 2 tooltips, all describing the same named-preset concept (`session_combo`/`_session_id`). Everything else matching "session" in the codebase is either the *editor's* unrelated mask-editing session (`photo_editor_panel.py`, `edit_storage.py`), a DB table name, or generic English — none of those touched, matching "do not alter the schema." Changed: "Session preset" → "Profile preset" (settings row label), `f"Session: {id}"` → `f"Profile: {id}"` (2 status-bar occurrences), and the 2 tooltips describing the same widget. Internal names (`session_combo`, `_session_id`, `SessionAnnotation`, the `sessions` SQL table) are unchanged.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_char_annotations.py` grew from 14 to 17 tests, all passing. No test depended on the renamed UI strings.

### WI-4.1 File-operation service: 4.1a partially done (winner-sync extracted); 4.1a remainder, 4.1b, 4.1c deliberately not attempted unattended (2026-09-27)
- **Found existing groundwork before writing anything:** `image_triage/file_ops.py` (pure, UI-free file primitives: `move_paths`, `copy_paths`, `create_folder`, `rename_bundle_paths`, etc.) and `image_triage/transfer_progress.py` (`run_move_transfer`, a real batched-transfer-with-progress-dialog implementation) already exist from earlier work. `_move_records_by_paths` already calls `run_move_transfer` for the actual file move — the audit's "moves per record with a full view rebuild" description is partly stale; the file I/O itself is already batched.
- **What's still true and current (N9):** `_move_records_by_paths`'s per-item loop still calls `self._remove_record(index)` once per moved record, and `_remove_record` runs a full `_apply_records_view` rebuild every time. New characterization test `test_HAZARD_moving_multiple_records_rebuilds_the_view_once_per_record` in `tests/test_char_move_delete_undo.py` pins this precisely: moving 3 files triggers 5 view rebuilds today (not 1). This is exactly WI-4.1b's target ("remove N records, then one view refresh") and the HAZARD naming/docstring explicitly says the assertion should flip to `1` once that lands.
- **Extracted winner-sync (part of 4.1a) into `file_ops.py`:** `record_paths`, `is_app_winner_artifact`, `create_winner_artifact`, and `sync_winner_copy_for_paths` are now pure, UI-free functions there. `MainWindow._record_paths`/`_is_app_winner_artifact`/`_create_winner_artifact` became one-line delegations; `_sync_winner_copy_for_paths` delegates too, passing its own `_create_winner_artifact`/`_is_app_winner_artifact` as injectable hooks (`sync_winner_copy_for_paths(..., create_artifact=self._create_winner_artifact, is_artifact=self._is_app_winner_artifact)`) so `unittest.mock.patch.object(main_window, "_create_winner_artifact", ...)` — used by `test_a_failure_part_way_removes_the_copies_already_made` to test rollback — still works on the real window. No duplicated logic between the two files. Also removed 2 further dead imports found while touching this area: `sidecar_bundle_paths` and `sync_sidecar_annotation` in `window.py` (the latter genuinely unused — the real sidecar sync only ever runs through `annotation_queue.py`, confirmed during WI-3.4's investigation).
- **Verified:** full suite **1467 passed, 3 skipped, 12 xfailed, 0 failed** (up from 1466: the new HAZARD test). Reachability: 0 unused imports, 0 unreferenced methods.
- **Why I stopped here instead of continuing into the rest of 4.1a/4.1b/4.1c unattended:** the winner-sync cluster I extracted was self-contained (no dependency on `self._decision_store`, `self._annotations`, undo, or view state) and had 27 existing characterization tests as a safety net — genuinely low-risk. The remaining piece — `_move_records_by_paths`, `_move_record_to_ai_recycle`, `_batch_apply_annotation_state`, `_apply_ai_culling` — is not: each interleaves file I/O with `self._push_undo`/`_push_undo_actions`, live annotation mutation (`self._annotations`, `self._decision_store`), the async annotation-persistence queue, and view refresh, and the plan itself rates this "High risk... the path that writes user files." Existing characterization coverage here is all single-record; there is no test today for a *batch* move/delete's undo semantics, only single-item ones. Attempting the batched-removal API (4.1b) or the async winner copy with rollback (4.1c) in the same unattended pass — without a human able to sanity-check interactive behavior like "which photo does focus land on after removing 50 records" — was judged a worse risk than pausing here with everything green. This isn't a missing decision (D4 is already settled); it's a judgment call that this specific slice deserves a session where someone can watch it run against a real folder, not just the test suite.
- **Concrete next steps for whoever picks this up:**
  1. Write batch-scoped characterization tests first (multi-record move/delete + undo, matching the existing single-record ones in `tests/test_char_move_delete_undo.py`) before changing any behavior — there's a real gap here today.
  2. Design a `_remove_records_by_paths(paths) -> int` batch API on `MainWindow`: compute the "next visible path" once from the pre-removal view, filter `self._all_records`/`_all_records_by_path` in one pass (removal by path, not by index, since indices shift as items are removed), persist the folder cache once, call `_apply_records_view` once. Route `_move_records_by_paths`, `_apply_ai_culling`, and the drag-drop path through it. The new HAZARD test should flip from `== 5` to `== 1` once this lands.
