### WI-4.1b done: batched removal API (2026-09-27, with you back and available to sanity-check)
- **Wrote batch-scoped characterization tests first**, per the plan above: multi-record move + undo (LIFO order, one independent `UndoAction` per record, matching single-record semantics) in `tests/test_char_move_delete_undo.py`, before changing any behaviour.
- **Added `MainWindow._remove_records_by_paths(paths: list[str]) -> int`**: resolves all paths to indices against the *current* view in one pass (before anything is removed, so index shifting during removal is a non-issue), computes the next-focus path once, filters `self._all_records`/`_all_records_by_path` once, persists the folder cache once, calls `_apply_records_view` once. Focus selection (the one UX judgment call, flagged before starting): the next surviving record **after the last removed one**, falling back to the nearest surviving record before the first removed one if nothing survives after — matching `_remove_record`'s own next-then-previous preference, just generalized to a removed *range* instead of one index. 6 direct unit tests cover this: single rebuild, both focus-selection branches, removing everything, a path not in the current view, and a fully-empty removal request being a true no-op (asserts `_apply_records_view` is never even called).
- **Routed all three named call sites through it:**
  - `_move_records_by_paths`: collects the moved records' original paths across the loop instead of calling `_remove_record` per item, then one `_remove_records_by_paths` call at the end.
  - `_apply_ai_culling`: `_move_record_to`/`_move_record_to_path`/`_move_record_to_ai_recycle`/`_move_record_to_ai_recycle_by_path` all gained a `defer_removal: bool = False` keyword (default preserves their exact existing single-item behaviour for every other caller — the "Send to recent destination" action, semantic-folder sorting, etc.). `_apply_ai_culling` passes `defer_removal=True` for every AI Pick/Reject move, collects the paths, and batch-removes once at the end.
  - Drag-drop (`_handle_record_drop`) already called `_move_records_by_paths`/`_copy_records_by_paths`, so it's covered for free.
- **Measured the actual improvement, not just "fixed it":** a 3-file batch used to trigger a *nondeterministic* 5-6 `_apply_records_view` rebuilds (discovered mid-investigation — `run_move_transfer` spins a nested `QEventLoop` while its worker thread runs, so unrelated pending Qt events, e.g. the folder watcher noticing the app's own file changes, can trigger extra rebuilds depending on timing). After batching, the same call is consistently 1-4 rebuilds for a 10-file batch — the deterministic O(N) component is gone, confirmed by a test that asserts the count stays under half the batch size regardless of the residual, unrelated timing noise. That residual noise (the folder watcher racing the app's own writes) is a separate, smaller finding, not blocking, not fixed here.
- **Tests:** full suite **1475 passed** (up from 1467), 3 skipped, 12 xfailed, plus the 2 known pre-existing `pythonw.exe` subprocess-handle failures (unrelated, seen throughout this session). Reachability: 0 unused imports, 0 unreferenced methods.
- **Not done here, still deferred:** 4.1c's async winner copy. That one touches the annotation-persistence worker thread directly and needs its own careful pass.

### New finding + fix (not in the original plan): undo of a batch move needed one click per file, and each click did a full folder rescan (2026-09-27)
- **How this surfaced:** asked you to manually try the batch-move work above, per the "someone should watch this run against a real folder" rationale for pausing before WI-4.1b. You found two things: the grid visually updated fine on the move itself, but (a) undo needed one click per file for a 10-file batch, and (b) each of those undo clicks did a full folder rescan, which visibly lagged.
- **Investigated before touching anything:** wrote `test_DIAG_rapid_consecutive_undos_of_a_batch_move`, firing 10 `_undo_last_action()` calls back-to-back with no waiting in between (mimicking rapid clicking) and checking whether the *final*, settled state was ever actually correct. It was — `_undo_move`'s `_load_folder(...)` reload is an async, cancellable scan (`_scan_token`), and it does eventually converge on the right answer. **So this was never a data-loss bug** — every file really does come back — but it is a genuine responsiveness problem: 10 rapid undo clicks meant 10 queued full-folder disk rescans, and the visible lag reads as "stuck" until a manual refresh either forces it or coincides with the pending scan finishing.
- **You chose to fix both, and they turned out to share one fix.** Root cause: `UndoAction` has scalar fields (`primary_path`, `original_winner`, `rating`, etc.) for exactly one record's state, so `_move_records_by_paths` pushed one independent `UndoAction` per moved file — there was no way to say "these N actions are one user gesture." Grouping them means a single Undo reverses all N *and* only reloads the folder once, since the reload no longer happens per-action.
- **Implementation:** added `UndoAction.batch_id: str = ""` — actions sharing a non-empty batch_id undo together as one unit; empty (the default) behaves exactly as before, so single-file moves, annotation toggles, etc. are untouched. `_move_records_by_paths` and `_apply_ai_culling` each generate one `uuid.uuid4().hex` per call and stamp every `UndoAction` they push with it (`_apply_ai_culling` uses **one shared id across both its AI-Pick moves and Reject recycles**, so "Undo" reverses the whole Apply-AI-Decisions action, mixed move/delete kinds and all). Split `_undo_move`/`_undo_delete` into `_undo_move_files`/`_undo_delete_files`, which do the file restore + annotation rekey and return whether a reload is needed, but no longer call `_load_folder` or show a status message themselves. `_undo_last_action` now: pops the top action, then keeps popping while the next action shares its `batch_id` (they're always contiguous, since nothing can interleave without an undo happening first); undoes each item in the batch, OR-ing together whether any needs a reload; does at most **one** `_load_folder` call for the whole batch; shows one combined status message (`"Undid 10 action(s): 10 moves"` for a batch, or the original single-item wording — `"Undid move: x.jpg"`, `"Restored x.jpg from safe trash"` — unchanged for non-batch actions). A failure partway through a batch pushes back only the not-yet-undone remainder, in the correct order to retry.
- **Tests:** rewrote the now-superseded per-item-undo characterization test (it pinned exactly the behaviour just changed on purpose) into `test_moving_multiple_records_pushes_one_undo_action_per_record_sharing_a_batch_id` (checks the batch_id is set and shared) and `test_undoing_a_batch_move_reverses_every_file_in_one_undo` (one Undo call, one `_load_folder` call, all N files back). Replaced the old rapid-fire diagnostic (no longer meaningful — a batch now needs exactly one undo, not N) with `test_undoing_past_an_already_empty_batch_is_a_safe_no_op`. Added `test_apply_ai_cullings_mixed_move_and_recycle_batch_undoes_together` covering the mixed move+delete batch case. Full suite: **1480 passed**, 3 skipped, 12 xfailed, 0 failed. Reachability clean.
- **Scope note:** `_batch_apply_annotation_state` (marking many records as winner/reject at once) still pushes one `UndoAction` per record with no `batch_id` — the same "N clicks to undo" pattern exists there too, just not what was reported. Left alone for now; the `batch_id` mechanism is generic and ready for it whenever that's worth doing.

### WI-4.1c done: async winner copy (2026-09-27)
- **Goal:** `_toggle_winner`/`_toggle_reject`/`_batch_apply_annotation_state` called `_sync_winner_copy(_for_paths)` synchronously — a real file copy/link on the UI thread — wrapped in `try/except OSError` that rolled back the annotation and popped a blocking `QMessageBox.warning("Winner Sync Failed", ...)`. On a slow disk or network share this froze the UI. Per D4 (already decided) the mechanism was mine to design; asked you for the one open UX question — how a failure should surface once it's no longer synchronous. **You chose: non-blocking status bar message + automatic revert (matching today's rollback, minus the blocking dialog).**
- **Reused existing machinery instead of building new:** `AnnotationPersistenceQueue` (in `image_triage/annotation_queue.py`) already runs decision-store writes + XMP sidecar sync on a background `QRunnable`, with an optimistic-update-then-rollback-on-failure pattern already proven for that half of annotation persistence (`_handle_annotation_persist_failed` reads `queue.rollback(path)` and reverts `self._annotations[path]` in memory, no dialog). WI-4.1c's job was to extend the *same* worker and the *same* rollback path to also cover the winner-copy file operation, not invent a second mechanism.
- **`annotation_queue.py` changes:** new frozen `WinnerSyncRequest` dataclass (`winner_enabled`, `folder`, `winner_mode`, `source_paths`); `AnnotationQueueEntry` gained an optional `winner_sync` field; `enqueue()` takes a matching `winner_sync` kwarg. `_AnnotationPersistTask.run()` now calls `file_ops.sync_winner_copy_for_paths(...)` for any entry carrying a `winner_sync`, **after** the sidecar sync and **before** putting `("ok", path)` for that entry — on `OSError` it puts `("winner_sync_failed", path, message)` and `continue`s, deliberately skipping the `("ok", ...)` put so the rollback entry in `_rollback_by_path` (only popped on `"ok"`) survives for the failure handler to consume. A successful sync that still had to leave a foreign file behind (the "kept" case — someone else's same-named file in `_winners`) puts `("winner_kept", path, "a.jpg, b.jpg")`. New `winner_sync_failed`/`winner_kept` signals on `AnnotationPersistenceQueue`, handled in `_drain_results` the same way `failed`/`warning` already are.
- **`window.py` changes:** added `_build_winner_sync_request(record, winner_enabled, folder) -> WinnerSyncRequest | None` (returns `None` inside the `_winners` folder itself, matching the old short-circuit). `_toggle_winner`, `_toggle_reject`, and `_batch_apply_annotation_state` now build the request and pass it into `_queue_annotation_persist(..., winner_sync=...)` instead of calling the sync function inline inside a `try/except` — the annotation flips optimistically and the status bar shows the immediate "Winner added"/"Winner removed" message without waiting on the file operation. `_undo_annotation` was also converted to route its winner re-sync through the same queue (an open question I flagged for myself and resolved in favor of consistency, since it already calls `_queue_annotation_persist` for the persistence half); it now also passes a `previous_annotation` snapshot so a failed re-sync during an undo can itself be rolled back the same way. New handlers `_handle_winner_sync_failed` (mirrors `_handle_annotation_persist_failed`: pops rollback, reverts `self._annotations`, refreshes the affected view, shows `"Reverted winner state for {name}: {message}"`) and `_handle_winner_kept` (shows the existing "left X in _winners: not a copy Image Triage made" wording, now arriving asynchronously instead of inline). `_batch_apply_annotation_state`'s `failures` counter is now always 0 from this path, since a winner-sync failure is no longer detected synchronously — batch winner/reject marking always reports success immediately; any individual failure surfaces later via the same async status-bar path as a single toggle would.
- **Removed as dead code**, since nothing calls them anymore: `MainWindow._sync_winner_copy`, `_sync_winner_copy_for_paths`, `_create_winner_artifact`, `_is_app_winner_artifact` (all four were pure delegations to `file_ops` functions of the same names; the worker now calls those `file_ops` functions directly). Also dropped the now-unused `create_winner_artifact`/`is_app_winner_artifact`/`sync_winner_copy_for_paths` names from `window.py`'s `file_ops` import.
- **Test rewrite:** all 17 tests in `tests/test_char_winner_sync.py` assumed synchronous completion; one (`test_toggle_winner_failure_rolls_back_the_annotation_and_warns`) explicitly asserted the now-removed blocking dialog. Split the file: the pure sync-logic tests (copy/hardlink/logical mode, existing-file/foreign-file/size-mismatch/missing-source "kept" edge cases, partial-failure cleanup, no-op inside `_winners`) now call `file_ops.sync_winner_copy_for_paths` directly — the exact function the worker calls — instead of going through a `MainWindow` at all. The `MainWindow`-level tests (`_toggle_winner` flipping state, undo, the "foreign file left behind" status message) now call `window._annotation_persistence_queue.flush_blocking()` after the toggle before asserting on the resulting file/annotation state. The failure-path test was rewritten as `test_toggle_winner_failure_reverts_the_annotation_via_status_bar`, patching `image_triage.annotation_queue.sync_winner_copy_for_paths` to raise and asserting the status bar message + reverted annotation instead of a dialog. Added two new direct unit tests on `_AnnotationPersistTask` in `tests/test_annotation_queue.py` (`test_winner_sync_runs_after_persistence_and_still_emits_ok`, `test_winner_sync_failure_skips_ok_so_rollback_data_survives`) pinning the ordering/rollback-survival design decision above at the lowest level.
- **Verified:** full suite **1482 passed** (up from 1480), 1 skipped, 12 xfailed, 0 failed, plus the 2 known pre-existing `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` failures (unrelated `WinError 50` subprocess-handle issue in this sandbox, present before this session's work and untouched by it). Reachability: 0 orphan modules, 0 unused imports, 0 unreferenced methods — unchanged from baseline.
- **What this unblocks:** WI-4.1 is now fully done. WI-4.2 (job/progress consolidation) and WI-4.4 (MainWindow decomposition — D11 already cleared this session) can start next. WI-4.3/WI-4.6 remain blocked on undecided D7/D15.

### WI-4.2 done: job and progress consolidation (2026-09-27)
- **Scope decision:** the Approach text said to "migrate the transfer dialog" into `JobController`, but the Disposition line and the underlying A11 finding both say "generic only." Asked you; you confirmed **generic only** — the Move-to transfer dialog (`image_triage/transfer_progress.py`, with its own byte/speed/ETA tracking, cancel button, and blocking `QEventLoop` pattern) stays a separate, purpose-built dialog, same as the AI stage-aware dialog (`AITrainingProgressDialog`) and the inline `BusyOverlay`. Only `JobController`'s existing ~9 consumers plus the 2 remaining raw `QProgressDialog`s were in scope.
- **Bug found and fixed (not in the original finding):** `_update_progress_dialog` was both an **instance attribute** (`self._update_progress_dialog: QProgressDialog | None = None`, storing the app-update-download dialog) and a **same-named class method** (the shared helper that applies `current`/`total`/`message` to any progress dialog). Python resolves an instance attribute before a same-named method on the class, so every call to `self._update_progress_dialog(dialog, current=..., total=..., message=..., default_label=...)` — the resize, convert, archive create/extract, workflow export, catalog refresh, and batch rename progress-tick handlers, all seven call sites — actually tried to call `None(...)` (or, once an update check had run, a `QProgressDialog` instance), raising `TypeError`. Qt's default slot-exception handling swallows this (prints to stderr, doesn't crash), and there was zero test coverage of any of these `_handle_*_progress` methods, so the bug was invisible: those six progress dialogs have **never shown a live percentage or message** — only the initial "Preparing..." label and a final flash before closing. Verified interactively via a scratch script (`window.__dict__['_update_progress_dialog']` is `None`, shadowing the class method; calling it raised the predicted `TypeError`) before touching anything.
- **Fix:** removed the colliding instance attribute entirely. The app-update download dialog now goes through the same `_show_job_progress_dialog`/`_close_job_progress_dialog`/`JobController` path as every other job dialog (`key="app_update"`), instead of its own dedicated attribute — this both fixes the bug and finishes the "generic only" consolidation for that dialog. No renaming of the method was needed; once nothing else claims the `_update_progress_dialog` name on the instance, `self._update_progress_dialog(...)` correctly resolves to the class method again.
- **Also consolidated:** `_run_ai_uninstall`'s raw, hand-built `QProgressDialog` (indeterminate, blocking `QEventLoop`, no cancel) now goes through `_show_job_progress_dialog`/`_close_job_progress_dialog` (`key="ai_uninstall"`) as well — that was the last of the "2 raw QProgressDialogs" from the A11 finding.
- **`processEvents()` removal (P8):** of the 12 `QApplication.processEvents()` calls in `window.py`, 9 were in the generic job/progress paths this WI touches (the "Preparing..." force-paint before starting a worker, and the "Refreshing library.../Packaging archive..." force-paint just before closing the dialog and running a synchronous finalize step) — all removed, since a background worker already drives every one of these dialogs and the finalize step's own work will repaint the UI naturally once control returns to the event loop. The other 3 are out of scope and untouched: 2 are a `_shutdown_child_processes` polling loop waiting on native subprocess termination (no worker involved), and 1 belongs to the stage-aware AI training dialog, explicitly kept separate. **Accepted minor UX change:** the "Refreshing library..."/"Packaging archive..." transitional label may no longer visibly paint before the subsequent blocking call (`_load_folder`, archive creation kickoff, etc.) runs — the dialog will instead appear to hold at its last progress state for that brief window. This matches the WI's own "Done" criterion ("no `processEvents` in file operations") and was judged an acceptable trade for removing 9 reentrancy-risk call sites.
- **Tests:** added `tests/test_char_job_progress.py` (6 tests, previously zero coverage of this code) — locks in that `_update_progress_dialog` is a real callable and actually updates a dialog's value/label/default-label fallback; that `_handle_resize_progress` and `_handle_catalog_refresh_progress` update their dialogs correctly (representative of all six previously-broken tick handlers, which share the same helper); and that both migrated dialogs (`app_update`, `ai_uninstall`) go through `_job_controllers`/`JobController` and clean up after themselves, with no attribute collision.
- **Verified:** full suite **1488 passed** (up from 1482), 1 skipped, 12 xfailed, 0 failed, plus the 2 known pre-existing `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` failures (unrelated, present before this session). Reachability: 0 orphan modules, 0 unused imports, 0 unreferenced methods — unchanged from baseline (the write-only-attribute list already included `_catalog_progress_dialog` before this change; not introduced by it).
- **What this unblocks:** WI-4.2 is done. WI-4.4 (MainWindow decomposition) can proceed next.

### WI-4.4 slice 1 done: toolbar/menu-builder extraction (2026-09-27)
- **Approach agreed with you:** WI-4.4 is High risk (558 tangled attributes across a 26,774-line `MainWindow`) and the plan itself calls for incremental slices in a specific order (AI orchestration -> toolbar/palette/menus -> file-ops service -> catalog/library -> records-view/scan, last, deferred). Rather than attempt a whole slice unattended, I investigated the toolbar/palette/menus slice first (139 methods, ~3,244 lines, across menu-bar builders, topbar, workspace-bar, jiggle-mode drag/edit, command palette, and toolbar context menus) and proposed starting with the cleanest, zero-external-coupling subset. You approved that scope.
- **Dead code found and removed:** `image_triage/ui/toolbars.py`'s `build_primary_toolbar` (62 lines) had zero callers anywhere in the repo — a superseded toolbar design from before the topbar/workspace-bar system in `window.py` existed. Deleted the whole module (and its private helpers `_add_toolbar_action`/`_add_toolbar_menu`, also unreferenced elsewhere) and its `image_triage/ui/__init__.py` import/`__all__` entry, per your confirmation.
- **What moved:** created `image_triage/ui/toolbar_menus.py::ToolbarMenuController` (consistent with the existing `ui/actions.py`/`ui/menus.py` extraction pattern) and moved the 9 self-contained menu/button-construction methods that read only `self.actions`/a parent widget, with no reads of any other MainWindow state and no callbacks beyond `self.actions`: `_build_popup_button`, `_build_review_toolbar_menu`, `_build_view_toolbar_menu`, `_build_projects_toolbar_menu`, `_build_catalog_toolbar_menu`, `_build_ai_results_menu`, `_build_columns_toolbar_menu`, `_build_sort_toolbar_menu`, `_build_quick_filter_toolbar_menu`, plus the two label helpers `_menu_text_with_hint` (now a `staticmethod` on the controller) and `_menu_text_with_action_shortcut`. `MainWindow` now builds `self._toolbar_menus = ToolbarMenuController(self, self.actions)` right after `self.actions = build_main_window_actions(self)`, and every one of the 11 original methods on `MainWindow` became a one-line delegate to it (per the plan's "keep thin delegating methods until callers migrate" — none of the ~15 call sites across `window.py` needed to change).
- **Deliberately deferred within this same slice** (per the investigation, not attempted here): the four `_normalize_toolbar_placement`/`_normalize_toolbar_style`/`_normalize_workspace_bar_state`/`_normalize_workspace_bar_position` methods look like clean pure functions but are tied to `MainWindow`-class-level constants (`TOOLBAR_PLACEMENTS`, etc.) shared with sibling methods (`_set_toolbar_placement`, workspace-bar state setters) that aren't extracted yet — moving them now would split state ownership across two classes for no benefit; they belong with whichever later pass extracts topbar/workspace-bar placement as a whole. `_build_command_palette_commands` (468 lines, ~18 external callbacks into filtering/catalog/records-view/review/AI/settings) and the jiggle-mode drag machinery (~1,000 lines, self-contained but large/stateful) are also deferred to later passes within this slice, per the investigation's risk ranking.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_topbar_style.py::TopbarStyleTests::test_toolbar_group_menus_include_new_workflows` called `MainWindow._build_review_toolbar_menu(host)` etc. against a lightweight fake `QMainWindow()` host (not a full `MainWindow`) to unit-test menu construction cheaply; updated it to also set `host._toolbar_menus = ToolbarMenuController(host, host.actions)`, matching the new delegation. No other tests referenced the moved methods by their unbound `MainWindow.` form.
- **Verified:** full suite **1488 passed**, 1 skipped, 12 xfailed, 0 failed, plus the 2 known pre-existing `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` failures. (One run hit an unrelated `Windows fatal exception: access violation` inside `image_triage/cache.py::enforce_size_cap`'s background cache-cleanup thread racing test-process garbage collection — did not reproduce on a clean re-run and is unconnected to anything touched here; flagging in case it recurs elsewhere.) Reachability: 0 orphan modules, 0 unused imports (after dropping the now-unused `add_ai_results_actions` import from `window.py`), 0 unreferenced methods — unchanged from baseline.
- **What's next:** WI-4.4 continues with further toolbar/palette/menu passes (topbar/workspace-bar construction, then jiggle-mode, then the command palette last), then file-ops service, catalog/library, and records-view/scan, per the plan's stated order — each as its own reviewable slice.

### WI-4.4 slice 2 attempted then deferred: jiggle-mode toolbar editor (2026-09-27)
- **What happened:** looked at the jiggle-mode drag/edit machinery (~1,000 lines, 14 methods) before moving anything, since it was the next candidate per slice 1's risk ranking. The earlier investigation's "self-contained (zero outside readers)" claim was true only for the `_toolbar_edit_*` state *attributes* — the method *bodies* turned out to be densely wired into three separate subsystems: the topbar-slot state machine (`_topbar_slots`, `_normalize_slots`, `_sync_items_from_slots`, `_save_workspace_toolbar_layouts`, `_commit_slots_and_refresh`), the command palette (`_active_command_palette`, `_command_palette_open`, `CommandPaletteDialog`), and several class-level constant registries (`WORKSPACE_TOOLBAR_ALLOWED_ITEMS`, `TOPBAR_REPEATABLE_ITEMS`, etc.) — plus `widget.installEventFilter(self)` tying into `MainWindow.eventFilter`. `tests/test_topbar_style.py` also has several existing white-box tests calling these methods unbound against fake `SimpleNamespace` hosts, each of which would need matching rewrites.
- **Decision:** flagged the actual density to you (20+ distinct external touchpoints in the first 150 of ~1,000 lines) as materially riskier than slice 1 — a mechanical "move as-is with a MainWindow back-reference" translation across this much surface is exactly the kind of manual, error-prone edit that produced this session's two prior bugs (the `_update_progress_dialog` shadowing bug in WI-4.2, the `test_topbar_style.py` break in slice 1), just at much larger scale, for a benefit (line-count reduction only, no real coupling reduction) the plan itself already ranks as the weak alternative. **You chose to defer it** and pivot to WI-4.3 instead. No code was changed for this slice.
- **What this leaves open:** jiggle-mode extraction is still on the table for a dedicated future pass — ideally one that either accepts the MainWindow back-reference approach with the time to do the ~14-method translation carefully (test-by-test), or first extracts the topbar-slot and command-palette state it depends on so jiggle-mode has less to reach across.

### WI-4.3 done: duplicate/similar-frame systems, one-page map and decision (2026-09-27)
- **Scope, per the plan:** investigation only — "Document what each engine produces and who consumes it; measure whether pHash is computed twice per folder; decide whether to share the computation and align vocabulary. Do **not** merge engines." D7 (decided earlier this session: "Duplicates" = exact + near-identical bursts) was the one blocking product decision.

**The four mechanisms, what they produce, who consumes them:**
1. **`review_intelligence.py`** (runs on every folder open). Exact duplicates via file-size bucket + SHA-1 (`_find_exact_duplicate_groups`); near-duplicates/bursts via a 64-bit **dHash** (average-hash, `_dhash`) compared by Hamming distance plus size/luma checks, with burst membership additionally requiring EXIF capture-time/lens/focal/aperture/ISO closeness. Hard-coded thresholds (`distance<=3`/`size_similarity>=0.94`, or `distance<=5`/`size_similarity>=0.90`/`luma_delta<=16`) — **not** read from Settings. Cached per-folder in `catalog.sqlite3` (`CatalogRepository.load_review_features`/`load_review_grouping`). Output: `ReviewIntelligenceBundle` with per-image `group_kind` (`exact_duplicate`/`likely_duplicate`/`burst`/`similar`, the last effectively unreachable — its detection tier was retired) and derived `is_duplicate`/`has_group` flags. **Consumers:** the "Near Duplicates" saved filter (`is_duplicate`), the "Smart Groups" filter (`has_group`), grid badges ("Exact Dup"/"Near Dup"/"Burst"), and — whenever review intelligence has run — it also **supersedes** `bursts.py` for the Smart Groups/Stacks grid display.
2. **The pHash prefilter inside the AI run** (`perceptual_hash.py` + `phash_prefilter.py`, orchestrated from `aiculler_workflow.py`). A true DCT-based **pHash** (32x32 grayscale -> 2D DCT -> 8x8 low-frequency bit-plane), grouped by union-find on Hamming distance (default threshold 6, **Settings-configurable**). Cached in a separate per-folder `phash_cache.json` (path+size+mtime keyed) — a different cache store and a different hash algorithm from #1, with no cross-reuse between them. **Consumers:** AI-run pool-removal/trash decisions, the AI-review "collapse unit" logic (the *only* true collapse mechanism among all four — semantic clusters, below, are a spread signal, not a collapse unit), the "AI Prefilter Dumped" filter/badge, and a third reader (`window.py::_aiculler_phash_group_maps`) that re-parses the same cache file with its own floored threshold for adapter-review sibling collapsing.
3. **`bursts.py`** (Smart Groups/Stacks). Pure EXIF math, no image hashing at all — same time/lens/focal/aperture/ISO thresholds as #1's burst check, **implemented a second time** (byte-for-byte equivalent logic, two places). In practice this is a **fallback only**: it's used for the Smart Groups/Stacks display exclusively when review intelligence hasn't run yet, and is superseded by #1 once it has.
4. **`aiculler` clusters** (`aiculler/semantic.py::CategoryClusterer`). CLIP-embedding content-category clustering (16 fixed categories, e.g. landscape/wildlife/portraits), persisted to `semantic_clusters`/`image_cluster_memberships`. **Not primarily a duplicate detector** — it's a diversity/"spread" signal used alongside #2's pHash groups to avoid over-selecting near-identical-category images in AI-review candidate selection. It never collapses images the way pHash groups do.
- **Confirmed dead, removed:** `perceptual_hash.find_perceptual_duplicate_groups` (9-line wrapper around the live `find_perceptual_duplicate_groups_with_stats`) had zero callers anywhere — not even in tests, despite the original audit calling it "referenced only by tests." Deleted.
- **Is pHash computed twice per folder?** Confirmed in spirit, not literally: #1's dHash and #2's DCT-pHash are two different algorithms with two separate cache stores and no cross-reuse, so the app does perceptual-similarity work twice over the same images (once per folder view via #1, once per AI run via #2) — just not the identical computation the plan worried about. No shared cache exists to bridge them. **Decision: do not build one.** Per the plan's explicit "do not merge engines," and because #1 (dHash, cheap, runs on every folder open, feeds always-on UI) and #2 (DCT-pHash, more expensive, only runs when the AI-run prefilter is enabled, feeds a different pipeline with a user-configurable threshold) serve genuinely different jobs with different cost/latency budgets — merging them risks coupling always-on folder-open latency to AI-run-specific tuning. Left as documented, known overlap rather than engineering effort to eliminate it.
- **Settings > Duplicates page scope — confirmed, matches audit's U5 claim:** every control on that page (`settings_dialog.py`'s "Duplicate check", "Duplicate distance", "Cache hash metadata", "Run diagnostics") writes only `PHashPrefilterSettings`, consumed exclusively by mechanism #2 (the AI-run prefilter) and its adapter-review collapse map. **None of it affects** the "Near Duplicates" filter, "Smart Groups", or "Stacks" (mechanism #1/#3), which use hard-coded thresholds. One nuance beyond U5's original claim: the page's "Duplicate check" toggle does additionally gate one more badge (the "AI Prefilter Dumped" grid indicator) not named in the original finding, but that's still downstream of mechanism #2, not #1/#3. Also found: `GuidedAICullPreferencesDialog`'s "Group near-duplicate photos" checkbox is a *second* UI surface editing the exact same `PHashPrefilterSettings.enabled` value.
- **Vocabulary decision (feeds WI-7.2):** per D7, "Duplicates" as a user-facing term should mean mechanism #1's `is_duplicate` (exact + likely_duplicate) — which is already exactly what the "Near Duplicates" filter uses today, so **no filter-behavior change is needed to align with D7, only a later labeling pass** (WI-7.2) to stop the Settings > Duplicates page from implying it controls the Near Duplicates filter/Smart Groups/Stacks when it doesn't. Candidate renaming for that later pass: keep "Near Duplicates" as the filter name (already matches D7's meaning); rename the Settings page from "Duplicates" to something scoped to its actual job (e.g. "AI Duplicate Prefilter" or fold its explanation text to state plainly it only affects the AI run) so it stops implying broader scope.
- **Verified:** full suite still green after the one dead-code removal (ran below); reachability clean.
- **What this unblocks:** WI-4.3 is done (investigation + decision, no engine merge, per disposition). WI-7.2 (vocabulary pass) now has a concrete map to work from instead of starting cold.

## Phase 5: Editor and integration

### WI-5.2 done: mask geometry parity — investigation, tests, and decision (2026-09-27)
- **Reframing the finding:** the audit's "two mask rasterisers" framing was imprecise. There is **one** shared rasteriser, `mask_strength_qimage()` (`image_triage/ui/mask_overlay.py:440`) — both the interactive overlay (`MaskOverlay._strength_image`, `mask_overlay.py:855-959`) and the renderer (`EditorRenderService._strength_for`, `image_triage/editor_render.py:499-549`) call it, and the crop/rotate/flip geometry warp inside it is identical for both (rasterize in source space, then warp through the same `QTransform` the pixels took) — this part already has test coverage (`MaskStrengthUnderCropTests` in `tests/test_editor_crop_retouch.py`) and does **not** drift.
- **Where the real divergence is:** the two call sites pass a different `guide_image` argument. The overlay's non-identity-view branch (crop/straighten/rotate/flip active) hard-codes `guide_image=None` (`mask_overlay.py:918-926`) because its only available guide is the *display-space* on-screen pixmap, and the raster step runs in *source* space — reprojecting a display-space guide into source space would require inverting the view transform, so the overlay author chose to skip the guide "rather than [risk it being] misaligned" (comment at `mask_overlay.py:915-917`). The renderer, by contrast, always has a source-space working image available and passes it as the guide unconditionally (`editor_render.py:429-430, 525-533`). The guide only feeds `refine_bitmap_qimage`'s edge-aware guided filter (`image_triage/mask_refinement.py:128-142, 159`) — so the practical effect is: **a bitmap mask with `edgeDetectionRadius` set renders with guided edge refinement in the final output, but without it in the live overlay, whenever a crop/rotate/straighten/flip is active.** Plain gradient masks (radial/linear) and refinement-free bitmap masks are unaffected.
- **"Drifted before" — no incident found.** No commit message or code comment references a specific past overlay/renderer mask bug; the phrase in the finding appears to paraphrase the design-intent comments already in the code (`mask_overlay.py:454-457`, `tests/test_editor_crop_retouch.py:3-6`) rather than describe a documented regression.
- **Decision: accepted interactive-performance tradeoff, not a bug to fix this pass.** The plan's own approach text says "keep the interactive low-resolution path," and the code already trades resolution for latency specifically when refinement is active (three cache-resolution tiers: `STRENGTH_CACHE_EDGE=2048` idle, `REFINEMENT_CACHE_EDGE=1280` with refinement on, `LIVE_BRUSH_CACHE_EDGE=768` mid-drag — `mask_overlay.py:54-56, 898-908`) plus purpose-built live-frame perf logging (`mask_overlay.py:947-958, 1556-1577`) — real evidence the refinement step's cost is already a known constraint, even though no checked-in benchmark proves reprojecting a guide would be too slow. Given that uncertainty and no user complaint driving this, closing the gap isn't worth the risk this pass. **Asked you; you agreed:** write the parity test the plan calls for (to pin current behavior and prevent it from silently changing further) and document the tradeoff, without attempting the reprojection or a perf-measurement side quest.
- **Tests:** added `OverlayVsRendererGuideUnderCropTests` (4 tests) to `tests/test_editor_crop_retouch.py`, alongside the existing `MaskStrengthUnderCropTests`: confirms a guide image measurably changes a guided-filter refinement at all; pins the overlay-vs-renderer divergence under a crop and under a rotation (a bitmap mask with `edgeDetectionRadius` set, called once with `guide_image=None` as the overlay does and once with a real guide as the renderer does); and confirms geometry itself still matches when refinement is disabled (the finding is specifically about the guide, not about drift returning to the geometry warp itself).
- **Verified:** full suite **1509 passed**, 1 skipped, 12 xfailed, 0 failed (beyond the 2 known-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` failures). Reachability clean.
- **What this leaves open:** if the guide-under-transform gap is ever worth closing, the concrete next step (per the investigation) is measuring `refine_bitmap_qimage` cost with a real reprojected guide at `LIVE_BRUSH_CACHE_EDGE` resolution during a live drag — not attempted here.

### WI-4.4 update: jiggle-mode toolbar editor deleted, not extracted (2026-09-28)
- **Reversal of the earlier plan:** slice 2's write-up (above) treated jiggle mode as a live feature too risky to extract into a controller in one pass. **You corrected that assumption**: the in-place ("jiggle mode") toolbar editor was effectively killed by a later toolbar visual redesign (buttons changed from app-like icons to their current form) and hasn't worked in the running app since — you confirmed neither of its two entry points (the toolbar right-click "Customize..." menu, the topbar double-click) actually do anything today, even though the Python code was still technically present and wired. You want it removed outright, not decomposed.
- **Verification before deleting:** traced every entry point (command-palette "Customize Toolbars..." command, the toolbar right-click context menu, and — found independently, not in the original slice-2 investigation — three more double-click handlers on `toolbar_stack`/`manual_toolbar`/`ai_toolbar` inside `eventFilter`) and confirmed each one's *only* effect was launching this dead editor; none served any other purpose. A dedicated mapping pass (mirroring the earlier slice-2 investigation) built a precise removal checklist and caught 8 methods and 3 `eventFilter` double-click branches the first pass had missed.
- **What was deleted from `image_triage/window.py`:**
  - The entire in-place toolbar editor: drag/drop-to-reorder, the removal badge, the "add a button" item-picker dialog, the edit-mode HUD banner (~1,013 contiguous lines: `_enter_toolbar_edit_mode` through `_rebuild_workspace_toolbar_editor`, plus `_slot_at_x`/`_commit_slots_and_refresh`/`_rebuild_toolbar_for_mode`/`_available_toolbar_items_for_mode`/`_add_workspace_toolbar_item`/`_remove_workspace_toolbar_item`/`_move_workspace_toolbar_item`/`_reset_workspace_toolbar_items`, found interleaved in the same block by the mapping pass).
  - The right-click context-menu launcher (`_configure_toolbar_context_target`, `_toolbar_context_mode_for`, `_handle_toolbar_context_event`, `_show_toolbar_context_menu`, 45 lines) plus its 11 installation call sites during widget construction.
  - 6 jiggle-only branches inside `MainWindow.eventFilter` (the HUD-drag-handle check, the in-place-drag block, the context-menu dispatch, and 4 double-click-to-enter-edit-mode branches across `topbar_action_stack`/`workspace_bar`/`toolbar_stack`/`manual_toolbar`/`ai_toolbar`) — left every unrelated branch in the same method untouched (workspace-bar drag-to-reposition, zen-mode hint overlay, breadcrumb path-edit escape, app-bar alignment, topbar overflow scheduling, folder/favorites drop handling).
  - 28 `self._toolbar_edit_*`/`_toolbar_item_picker_*`/`_toolbar_context_*` instance attributes from `__init__`, and 2 class constants (`TOPBAR_PICKER_HIDDEN_ITEMS`, `TOOLBAR_PICKER_SECTION_ORDER`) that existed only to feed the item-picker.
  - 6 more `self._toolbar_edit_mode` reads scattered outside the main cluster that the mapping pass didn't catch (in topbar-sizing, overflow-scheduling, and action-state-refresh code) — each was a guard that only ever mattered while editing; since editing can no longer happen, each simplified to its always-true branch or was deleted outright as a dead early-return. Caught these by re-grepping for the attribute name after the first round of deletions and confirming the file still imports/parses before running tests — exactly the kind of stray reference the WI-4.2 shadowing bug taught this session to check for.
  - `customize_workspace_toolbar` (the `QAction`, in `image_triage/ui/actions.py`) and its command-palette registration.
  - The orphaned `image_triage/ui/assets/minus_sign.png` (the edit-mode removal-badge icon) — confirmed zero references anywhere including `freeze_support.py`'s packaging-verification list.
  - Two knock-on orphans the reachability report caught after the main deletion: `_save_workspace_toolbar_layouts` (only ever called by the deleted add/remove/move/reset-item methods — nothing else mutates a toolbar layout now, so nothing needs to persist a changed one) and `_set_workspace_toolbar_controls_enabled` (only ever called from a deleted `_toolbar_edit_mode` guard).
- **Tests:** deleted 7 dead tests from `tests/test_topbar_style.py` (`test_toolbar_picker_assigns_every_button_to_a_named_category`, `test_toolbar_edit_banner_is_centered_below_topbar`, `test_toolbar_edit_banner_keeps_and_clamps_user_position`, `test_toolbar_edit_marker_drags_banner`, `test_toolbar_reset_requires_explicit_confirmation`, `test_toolbar_drag_can_target_the_final_edit_cell`, `test_toolbar_remove_badge_sits_on_the_button_top_right`); removed one now-dead assertion (`TOPBAR_PICKER_HIDDEN_ITEMS`) from an otherwise-valid test that also covers live constants; dropped 6 now-unused imports (`QEvent`, `QPoint`, `QPointF`, `QMouseEvent`, `QFrame`, `QMessageBox`) from that file.
- **Verified:** full suite **1502 passed** (down from 1509, minus the 7 deleted tests), 1 skipped, 12 xfailed, 0 failed beyond the 2 known-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` tests (confirmed flaky, not a regression, by re-running clean). Reachability: 0 orphan modules, 0 unused imports, 0 unreferenced methods, matching baseline.
