### WI-4.4 slice 6 done: catalog/library controller (2026-09-28)
- **Investigated first**, per the plan's disposition. Key finding that corrected an initial hope: extracting this slice does **not**, by itself, produce a "records-repository abstraction" that would make the harder move/copy/delete/undo slice (Group A/B) safe to tackle next. Neither `CatalogRepository` (`image_triage/catalog/repository.py`) nor `LibraryStore` (`image_triage/library_store.py`) has a per-record CRUD interface — both only support whole-folder (`load_folder_records`/`save_folder_records`, full delete-and-reinsert) or whole-collection (`add/remove/replace_paths_from_collection`) list operations. Building a real per-record repository facade is separate, additional design work that would sit on top of what this slice relocates, not something it hands over for free.
- **Scope narrowed from the investigation's initial estimate** once the actual code was read closely: the catalog/review-cache "status and debug badge" cluster (`_catalog_status_badge_text`, `_cache_pipeline_badge_text`, `_review_cache_summary_lines`, `_catalog_debug_summary`, `_refresh_catalog_status_indicator`) turned out to be deeply intertwined with the review-scoring/records-view-scan caching subsystem (`_catalog_load_source`, `_scan_cached_source`, `_review_grouping_cache_source`, etc.), not catalog-only — left on `MainWindow` untouched, matching how slice 1 left toolbar-placement normalizers with their non-extracted siblings. The sidebar "Projects" panel rendering (`_refresh_projects_panel`, `_update_projects_height`, `_project_id_for_item`, `_handle_project_activated`, `_show_projects_context_menu`) and `_refresh_collections_menu` were also left alone — UI-construction-heavy sidebar code, a separate concern from library/collection data operations.
- **What moved:** new `image_triage/catalog_controller.py::CatalogController`, covering: global catalog roots (`browse_catalog`, `add_current_folder_to_catalog`, `add_folder_to_catalog_prompt`, `remove_catalog_root_prompt`, `refresh_catalog_index`, `refresh_catalog_menu`), the catalog-refresh background task lifecycle (`start_catalog_refresh` + its 4 signal handlers + progress dialog show/close — same `JobController`/`_show_job_progress_dialog` pattern as every other job dialog), virtual collections (choose/resolve/create/begin/refresh/cancel/save/open/add-to/remove-from/delete, 12 methods), and the folder-records cache pass-through (`load_cached_folder_records`/`persist_folder_record_cache`/`rebuild_current_folder_catalog_cache`) — 27 methods total.
- **Coupling call:** `_collection_mode`/`_collection_target_id`/`_collection_previous_view`/`_collection_previous_inspector_enabled` are read as guard conditions in ~15 places outside this group (much like `_toolbar_edit_mode` was for jiggle mode) and `_active_catalog_task` gates the refresh-catalog action's enabled state elsewhere — all stayed on `MainWindow`, read/written by the controller through the `window` back-reference, matching the recycle-bin/folder-ops precedent. Task-lifecycle state with no external readers (`_catalog_context`, `_catalog_progress_dialog`, the `_catalog_pool` QThreadPool) also stayed on `MainWindow` for consistency with that same state, rather than splitting ownership.
- **Cleanup:** deleted `_start_catalog_refresh` and its 4 signal-handler methods outright (no delegates — their Qt connections now point at the controller's own internal handlers, so nothing on `MainWindow` called them anymore); same for `_show_catalog_progress_dialog`/`_close_catalog_progress_dialog`. Three methods (`_selected_record_paths_for_library`, `_choose_virtual_collection`, `_resolve_records_for_paths`) had zero remaining callers anywhere once their only callers moved into the controller too — deleted rather than kept as unnecessary delegates. Promoted `CatalogExecutionContext` from a `window.py` dataclass to living in the new controller module. Dropped the now-unused `CatalogRefreshSummary` import from `window.py`.
- **A real regression caught and fixed before it shipped:** the first full-suite run segfaulted (`Windows fatal exception: access violation`) partway through, always at the same test (`test_command_palette_picker.py::test_anchored_picker_reports_outside_dismissal`) — but standalone that test passed cleanly, and the crash didn't reproduce every run, which looked like this environment's known flaky-background-thread class of crash at first. It wasn't: bisecting against the pre-extraction code (via `git stash`) proved the pairing `tests/test_collection_mode.py` + `tests/test_command_palette_picker.py` was 100% new-code-only reproducible (3/3 fails on the new code, 1/1 clean on the old). Root cause: `tests/test_collection_mode.py`'s `_ModeHost` is a real `QWidget` subclass created and destroyed fresh in every one of its 11 test methods; adding `self._catalog = CatalogController(self)` created a reference cycle (`host -> catalog -> host`) that delayed the Python wrapper's garbage collection past the point its underlying Qt C++ object was destroyed, crashing on the *next* test file's first widget interaction. Fixed by breaking the cycle explicitly in `tearDown` (`self.host._catalog = None` before `self.host.close()`) — confirmed 3/3 clean afterward, plus two full-suite runs. This is a test-fixture-specific hazard (fresh-per-test `QWidget` doubles holding a cyclic controller reference), not a flaw in the controller pattern itself — the real `MainWindow` is a long-lived singleton, so the same cycle there is inert.
- **Tests:** updated `tests/test_collection_mode.py`'s `_ModeHost` (added the `_catalog` attribute the delegates now need, plus the cycle-breaking `tearDown` fix — all 11 pre-existing tests still pass unchanged otherwise); updated `tests/test_window_catalog_cache.py`'s two cache-glue test stubs (`_WindowCacheStub`, `_WindowRebuildStub`) the same way; updated `tests/test_char_job_progress.py`'s catalog-progress test to call the controller's internal methods directly now that the `MainWindow`-level ones it used no longer exist.
- **Verified:** full suite **1502-1504 passed** across several runs (1 skipped, 12 xfailed, 0 failed beyond the 2 known-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` tests, which passed clean on one run). Reachability: 0 orphan modules, 0 unused imports, 0 unreferenced methods.
- **What this unblocks:** the catalog/library investigation's own conclusion stands — this slice does not unblock Group A/B. What's left in WI-4.4: command palette (item 2), move/copy/delete/undo orchestration (item 3, now confirmed to need a from-scratch records-repository design rather than an existing seam), records-view/scan (item 5, deferred). No further catalog/library work is planned; this slice is complete as scoped.
- **Logged in `docs/deletion_ledger.md`.**

### WI-4.4 item 3 started: RecordsRepository introduced, MainWindow state now owned externally (2026-09-28)
- **Investigated first.** A dedicated pass re-mapped all ~44 move/copy/delete/undo methods and found the real mutation surface is much smaller than the method count suggests: only `_remove_record`, `_remove_records_by_paths`, `_replace_record`, `_replace_records_after_moves`, plus two "clear everything" resets and one full-folder-reload site, ever assign `self._all_records`/`self._all_records_by_path` — **7 write sites total across the entire 24,600-line file**, confirmed by an exhaustive grep, not an estimate. Every other method in the 44 is orchestration (filesystem op -> annotation rekey -> undo push -> status message) terminating in one of those seven.
- **Design decision, asked of you directly:** the investigation's proposed repository design required `MainWindow._all_records`/`_all_records_by_path` to become **read-only properties** delegating to the new repository — but that's incompatible with a "just migrate `_remove_record` first" partial slice, because a property has no way to let some call sites keep assigning the old way and others not. The alternative (a window-back-reference pass-through, like every controller so far) would have avoided that, at the cost of not actually giving `MainWindow` a real single owner for this state. **You chose full ownership now.** Since a read-only property can't be partial, this meant migrating **all seven** write sites in one pass, not just the recommended first slice — larger than originally scoped, but mechanically contained (7 sites, precisely identified) rather than open-ended.
- **What was built:** new `image_triage/records_repository.py::RecordsRepository`, owning the record list and its path index privately, with exactly four operations matched to the four real shapes of change found in the code (not a generic CRUD interface, which the investigation argued against — no caller ever adds/updates one record without also needing a batch/refocus/rekey step alongside it, which stays the caller's job): `clear()`, `reload(records)` (full-folder/virtual-scope replace), `remove_paths(paths)` (batch removal), `replace_by_old_path(records_by_old_path)` (post-rename/post-move swap, order-preserving). Read access: `all_records`, `all_records_by_path`, `by_path(path)`.
- **MainWindow changes:** `self._records_repo = RecordsRepository()` replaces the old `self._all_records`/`self._all_records_by_path` instance-attribute declarations; two new `@property` definitions (`_all_records`, `_all_records_by_path`) delegate reads to the repository, with **no setter** — every one of the ~24,500 other lines that only *read* these two names keeps working completely unchanged (property read syntax is identical to attribute read syntax), and the 6 non-`__init__` sites that used to *assign* them now call the matching repository method: the two "clear everything" resets (inside `_load_folder` and `_handle_scan_failed`) call `clear()`; `_apply_loaded_records` calls `reload(records)`; `_remove_record`/`_remove_records_by_paths` call `remove_paths(...)`; `_replace_record`/`_replace_records_after_moves` call `replace_by_old_path(...)` (unifying `_replace_record`'s old incremental dict-patch and `_replace_records_after_moves`'s full-dict-rebuild into the repository's one full-rebuild implementation — functionally identical, now consistent instead of two different approaches to the same problem).
- **View-refresh and undo, deliberately unchanged:** the repository does not call `_apply_records_view` itself (per the design discussion) — callers still decide when to refresh the view, exactly as before, since the repository has no business knowing about filter state, selection, or the grid widget. Undo (Group B) needed no changes at all: `_undo_move_files`/`_undo_delete_files` already resynchronize via a full `_load_folder` reload rather than surgically patching the record list, and that reload path now goes through `reload()` the same as any other full-folder load — no new undo-specific repository surface was needed.
- **Scope note — this is NOT yet a controller extraction.** All 44 Group A/B methods stay exactly where and as they were on `MainWindow`; only the state they mutate moved. Relocating the methods themselves into a controller (if ever done) is separate, later work — today's change only answers "who owns the record list," not "who orchestrates moves/deletes."
- **Aside flagged as dead code — was wrong, corrected below.** `_rekey_filter_metadata_after_moves` was initially reported as having no caller anywhere in `window.py` (the investigation's search covered the ~44 Group A/B methods but missed `_finalize_batch_rename`, a batch-rename-controller-supporting method elsewhere in the file that does call it). See the "item 3 relocated" write-up below for how this was caught and fixed.
- **Tests:** added `tests/test_records_repository.py` (12 tests) — unit tests for all four repository operations in isolation (reload, clear, remove batch/single/unknown-path/empty, replace single/batch/order-preservation/empty-no-op, index consistency), plus one integration test against a real `main_window` confirming the property delegation and that direct assignment (`main_window._all_records = []`) now correctly raises `AttributeError` (proving the read-only contract holds on the genuine class, not just the stub). No existing test needed to change — the entire pre-existing `test_char_move_delete_undo.py` suite (which exercises `_remove_record`/`_replace_record`/undo through the real window) passed unmodified, which is the strongest evidence the migration preserved behavior exactly.
- **Verified:** full suite **1514 passed**, 1 skipped, 12 xfailed, 0 failed beyond the 2 known-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` tests. Reachability: 0 orphan modules, 0 unused imports, 0 unreferenced methods.
- **What this unblocks:** the records list now has a single, real owner (`RecordsRepository`) instead of being a bag of shared mutable state — a genuine prerequisite for ever safely extracting the Group A/B orchestration methods or the records-view/scan slice into their own controllers, even though that extraction itself hasn't happened yet. Next natural step within item 3, if resumed: actually relocate the 44 orchestration methods to a controller now that they have a real interface to call instead of raw attribute mutation — or move to the command palette (item 2) or leave WI-4.4 here, since a clean state boundary is a reasonable stopping point.

### WI-4.4 item 3 finished: the 44 move/copy/delete/rename/undo methods relocated (2026-09-28)
- **Scope, per your instruction to finish WI-4.4 entirely** (excluding the always-optional records-view/scan slice, confirmed with you): relocate all ~44 Group A/B orchestration methods into a controller, now that `RecordsRepository` gives them a real interface to call instead of raw `_all_records` mutation.
- **What moved:** new `image_triage/record_ops_controller.py::RecordOpsController` — every method from the file-ops investigation's Group A (single/batch move, copy, delete, restore, rename, and their path-indirection adapters) and Group B (the undo stack and its per-kind undo handlers), plus the pure filesystem bundle helpers (`move_bundle`, `move_bundle_to_recycle`, `copy_bundle`, `trash_or_delete_paths`, `delete_paths_permanently`). The `UndoAction` dataclass moved with it (previously defined in `window.py`, used in 23 places across the file — confirmed nothing outside `window.py` imported it directly, so moving it and re-importing into `window.py` was a clean, contained change). Like every controller in this WI, `RecordOpsController` holds no private state of its own (`self._window = window` only) — every attribute it touches (`_undo_stack`, `_annotations`, `_decision_store`, `_records_repo`, etc.) is shared MainWindow state, read/written through the back-reference.
- **The interleaving problem:** unlike every prior slice, these 44 methods are not one or two contiguous blocks — they're threaded through ~2,300 lines of `window.py` interleaved with annotation/review/records-view code that stays put (e.g. `_tag_record`, `_batch_apply_annotation_state`, `_show_grid_context_menu`, `_next_visible_path`). Extraction required identifying exact line-boundaries for 44 methods individually via a full method-boundary map (every `def`/`@staticmethod` line in the affected range), then replacing each one — or small contiguous clusters of them — with a thin delegate in place, rather than deleting one large range.
- **All 44 delegates kept on `MainWindow` initially** (the safe default from every prior slice, since verifying "zero external callers" for 44 methods individually up front would have meant grepping each one before touching anything) — then the reachability report was used *after* the move to find the 14 that turned out to have no remaining caller anywhere (their only production callers had also moved into the controller, now calling each other directly): `_next_visible_path_after_batch_removal`, `_delete_record_by_path`, `_move_record_to_ai_recycle`, `_rename_record`, `_rekey_annotation_after_move`, `_show_undo_batch_message`, `_undo_annotation`, `_undo_move_files`, `_undo_delete_files`, `_annotation_from_action`, `_move_bundle`, `_move_bundle_to_recycle`, `_copy_bundle`, `_delete_paths_permanently` — all deleted from `MainWindow` after confirming zero references across `image_triage/` and `tests/`. The remaining ~30 delegates stay because they're wired externally: `ui/actions.py` QAction slots, `.clicked`/signal connections, batch-mode wrappers still on `MainWindow` (`_batch_keep_records`, `_batch_restore_records`), and grid drag-and-drop handlers.
- **A real regression caught by the test suite, not by review:** the full suite initially came back with a genuine, reproducible failure in `test_char_batch_rename_controller.py` (not flaky — 4/4 fails, including standalone). Root cause: `_rekey_filter_metadata_after_moves`, which the earlier records-repository investigation had flagged as dead code (no caller found) and which I deleted outright as part of this pass, actually **does** have a caller — `_finalize_batch_rename` (part of the WI-4.2/batch-rename controller work, far away from the Group A/B method cluster the investigation searched), which calls it right after `_replace_records_after_moves` and before the view refresh. Deleting it meant that call site raised `AttributeError` inside a broad `except Exception` handler, silently aborting the finalize step before the view ever refreshed — files renamed on disk correctly, but `MainWindow._records` (the display list) never updated to show it. Fixed by restoring the method as a proper `RecordOpsController.rekey_filter_metadata_after_moves` operation (not dead-weight back on `MainWindow`) plus its delegate; confirmed 4/4 clean afterward. **Lesson applied:** a "no caller found" claim from an investigation scoped to one part of a 24,000-line file is a hypothesis, not a fact — this is now the second time this session a dead-code claim turned out to be wrong once the full test suite (not just a grep) had a chance to exercise the removed path, after the WI-4.2 shadowed-attribute bug taught the same lesson from the opposite direction (silent runtime breakage vs. here, an incorrect-removal breakage).
- **Cleanup:** removed 6 now-unused imports from `window.py` (`move_paths`, `copy_paths`, `rename_bundle_paths`, `TransferItem`, `run_move_transfer`, `QFile`) — all now imported directly by the controller instead.
- **Tests:** no new tests needed — the existing `tests/test_char_move_delete_undo.py` (24 tests covering move/copy/delete/restore/AI-recycle/batch-operations/undo through the real `MainWindow`) and `tests/test_char_batch_rename_controller.py` passed with zero modifications required (beyond the regression fix above), which is the strongest available evidence this was a faithful relocation, not a rewrite.
- **Verified:** full suite **1514 passed** (stable across 4 consecutive runs after the fix), 1 skipped, 12 xfailed, 0 failed beyond the 2 known-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` tests. Reachability: 0 orphan modules, 0 unused imports, 0 unreferenced methods.
- **What this completes:** WI-4.4 item 3 (move/copy/delete/undo orchestration) is now fully done — both halves, the state ownership (`RecordsRepository`) and the orchestration relocation (`RecordOpsController`). Remaining in WI-4.4 per your scope: item 2 (command palette). Item 5 (records-view/scan) stays deferred, per the plan's own allowance and your explicit confirmation.

### A2 stage 1 done: the giant stylesheet builders split into section functions, output byte-identical (2026-10-02)
- **Scope decision.** A2 bundles two things: the 2,677-line `build_app_stylesheet` f-string and a long tail of other long functions (the audit counted 126 over 100 lines; a fresh AST survey on 2026-10-02 found **107**, because several large ones were deleted or split earlier in this effort). The stylesheet is the concrete, *mechanically verifiable* part, so it went first as a pure refactor with a hard byte-identical requirement. The long tail is split into stages: (1) stylesheets (this entry), (2) declarative builders (actions/menus/palette/CLI parser), (3) `MainWindow.__init__` (still ~1,195 lines — the "1,301-line `__init__`" half of P6 was about startup *cost*, fixed in WI-8.1; its *shape* belongs here).
- **Method (goldens before any edit).** The full text of every case was recorded from the unmodified code over a broad input grid and compared byte-for-byte after the refactor. App sheet: **354 cases / 309 unique texts** (10 appearance modes incl. AUTO x 25 gamma values; 8 synthetic palettes x 3 gammas; patched `WORKSPACE_METRICS`/`UI_FONT_STACK`/`CHECKBOX_CHECK_ASSET` sentinels, 80 cases). Studio sheet: **4,527 cases / 278 unique** (heights -5, 0, 1..4500, 6000, 8000, 10000, 100000; three scopes). People sheet: **455 cases / 449 unique**. All matched after the refactor. A second, independent check loads the *original* code from `git HEAD` under private module names and compares it with the new code under fuzzing: **0 mismatches** over 1,500 random palettes (app), 14,112 studio cases, 1,500 random palettes (people), and 35/35 pinned digests — **re-run by the coordinating session, not just the implementing pass: 0 mismatches.**
- **`build_app_stylesheet` (ui/theme.py): 2,677 lines -> a 15-line concatenation + 38 new private `_..._rules(theme)` section functions**, one per widget family (`_window_and_menu_rules`, `_phone_share_rules`, `_top_bar_button_rules`, `_settings_sidebar_rules`, `_command_palette_rules`, `_scroll_bar_rules`, ...); the 5 pre-existing helpers are untouched. The function only ever read a `ThemePalette` plus three module globals, so no context object was needed. Order lives in two tuples at the bottom of theme.py. Largest function left in theme.py is a pre-existing 95-line helper. theme.py: 3,574 -> 3,377 lines.
- **A 489-line run of rules was emitted twice, verbatim** in the original f-string (once around ~L1496-1984, again ~L2268-2756, with the settings-dialog rules between). The refactor preserves it exactly (its 9 sections are listed twice via `_REPEATED_WORKSPACE_SECTIONS`, with a comment) rather than "fixing" it — see the suspicious-CSS list.
- **`FullScreenPreview._studio_stylesheet_full` (preview.py): 542 lines -> a 4-line wrapper.** The CSS moved to a new module, `image_triage/ui/preview_studio_stylesheet.py` (9 fixed sections, 3 height-scaled sections, a frozen `_StudioSizes(height)` replacing the old closures, public `build_studio_dialog_stylesheet(scope, height)`). preview.py: 5,593 -> 5,057 lines. It is imported at module level in the same form as the existing `from .ui.theme import ...`, so cx_Freeze's static scan should pick it up; **an MSI build can't be run in this sandbox, so that is unverified there** (the earlier freeze gotcha, `image_triage.ui.layout_ratios`, was a *lazy* import; this one isn't).
- **Also covered (found by the survey):** `PeopleSearchDialog._stylesheet` (111 lines -> 2; a frozen `_PeopleColors` context + 5 sections; one unused local `on_hl` dropped, output unaffected). **Deliberately skipped** as not pure string builders: `ui/docs/browser.py::_apply_document_css` (121 lines, mixes palette maths with `setStyleSheet`/`setPalette` on five widgets and creates an overlay scrollbar — candidate for later) and constructors with inline `setStyleSheet` calls (`settings_dialog.__init__`, `preview.__init__`, `people_dialog.__init__`, `window._show_ai_setup_dialog`), which belong to later stages.
- **Suspicious CSS noticed and deliberately NOT changed** (byte-identical was the contract; each is a possible follow-up that needs a visual check, not a safe mechanical fix): (1) the duplicated 489-line block — the settings sections between the two copies share no selector with it so the second copy is probably dead, but equal-specificity rules with different selectors can still interact; `QLabel#inspectorValue` is also emitted a third time with identical properties. (2) The older settings block (orig ~L2757-2826) **re-declares 7 selectors from the newer settings block above it** (`settingsHelpButton`, `settingsCategoryHeading`, `settingsRowLabel`, `settingsRow`, `settingsRow:hover`, `settingsFooter`, `settingsPageSeparator`), five with *different* properties, and the later rule wins — e.g. `settingsRow` radius goes from 11px back to 8px and the category-heading padding is overridden — so the newer design values probably lose to the legacy ones (a likely real visual bug, worth a look). (3) `scope_stylesheet` splits the Studio text on `{` and `,`, so comments before rules get mangled (selectors still work, by accident; noted in the new module docstring). (4) Studio type sizes are floored by `MIN_TEXT_PX`, so heights 0 through ~720 all produce identical text, meaning the height component of the WI-8.1 `_studio_theme_key` guard causes redundant restyles at small sizes (not a correctness problem). (5) `CHECKBOX_CHECK_ASSET` is an absolute checkout path embedded in the CSS (the new test pins it to a fixed value so digests are location-independent).
- **Numbers.** `build_app_stylesheet` per call 0.18 -> 0.13-0.14 ms; studio sheet at h=1191 0.25 -> 0.18 ms (no regression). Functions over 100 lines in `image_triage/`: **107 -> 104**.
- **Tests: new `tests/test_stylesheet_golden.py` (47 tests).** 35 pinned SHA-256 digests + lengths (20 app, 11 studio, 4 people) — a *characterization* test: on mismatch the failure message says so and gives regeneration steps (`UPDATE_STYLESHEET_GOLDEN=1`, documented in the module docstring), so an *intentional* stylesheet change is a deliberate, visible digest update rather than silent drift. Structural tests independent of exact text: sections join in documented order for all 9 themes, the order table lists every `*_rules` function exactly once (twice for the repeated group), every section is non-empty, braces balanced with no nesting/doubled braces/unformatted `{theme.x}`/`None`/object reprs, the preview method delegates with the clamped height. **Mutation-checked**: a section swap, a dropped repeated group, one added trailing space and a type size +1 are all detected.
- **Verified independently**: full suite **1708 passed**, only the 2 known-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py` tests failed; reachability 0 / 0 / 1 (baseline); `test_packaging_scripts.py` + `test_freeze_support.py` pass. (The implementing run also saw one `test_char_move_delete_undo.py::test_moving_a_batch_no_longer_rebuilds_the_view_once_per_record` failure — `moved == 9` instead of 10, a file-move race unrelated to stylesheets, 3/3 passing in isolation; it did not recur on my run.)

### A2 stage 2 done: four long declarative builders split into section helpers, behavior snapshot-identical (2026-10-02)
- **Scope**: the four biggest *declarative* long functions — `build_main_window_actions` (`ui/actions.py`), `CommandPaletteController.build_commands`, `build_main_menu_bar` (`ui/menus.py`) and the `photoedit` CLI's `build_parser` (`photo_terminal/cli.py`). Pure refactor, same objects in the same order with the same attributes; nothing "fixed" along the way (oddities are listed below instead).
- **Method (snapshots before any edit)**: a behavior snapshot was recorded from the unmodified code (a second recording was byte-identical, so it is deterministic), then re-generated after each builder was split and diffed. **Actions**: 131 entries (93 plain + 38 in dict fields) with text, all shortcuts, enabled/checked/checkable, tooltips, status tips, group membership, which handler each signal reaches, and QAction/QActionGroup creation order — from a real sandboxed `MainWindow` and from a recording stand-in window. **Menus**: the full tree (titles in order, each action's text/shortcut/checkable/separator-ness, section headers, nested submenus) over 13 scenarios (each optional menu present/absent; dock actions none/empty/partial/unknown-key/full). **Palette**: `build_commands("main"|"preview")` over 12 hermetic states (all/none/alternate actions enabled, flags on/off, saved data present/absent, docks fake/none, no actions, hidden/visible/bare/full stand-in preview) with callbacks actually invoked against recorders; **every one of the 99 literal ids and all 17 dynamic id patterns appears in at least one state**, and a test fails if a new id is added without a state that emits it. **CLI**: structure read from the argparse objects (not `--help` text, so it doesn't depend on the Python version), 55 parse cases (minimal + full argv per subcommand, 11 rejected lines asserting `SystemExit(2)`), and a test that every option is exercised by some case.
- **Evidence beyond the snapshot diff**: all snapshot files byte-identical pre vs post, with one expected exception — the `render|full` CLI parse case was deliberately *widened* afterwards to exercise more options (I checked the diff: only that input line changed). A live differential loaded the saved original modules next to the refactored ones and ran the same dump functions through both (actions, menus x13, CLI, palette x12 — identical); **30 real `photoedit` invocations** (inspect, render, session ops, masks, two failing lines) through old and new `main` gave identical exit codes, stdout, stderr and produced files after normalizing temp paths/timestamps; **39 deliberate one-line breakages** of the refactored code were each caught by the new tests (files restored, md5 checked); `python -m image_triage.photo_terminal --help` and the `photoedit` entry point still work; `test_packaging_scripts.py` + `test_freeze_support.py` pass.
- **Results (function lines before -> after, helpers created, largest helper)**: `build_main_window_actions` 440 -> **14** (20 helpers; 14 group functions `_file_actions`..`_help_actions` + 5 dict-field helpers + `_create_single_actions`; largest `_ai_workflow_actions`, 54); `build_commands` 473 -> **34** (26 helpers: the `add_action_command` closure became `_action_command_adder` returning the identical callable, 13 action-section methods, 7 data-section methods, `_add_preview_commands` + 4 preview helpers; the lazy-preview logic is untouched — `window._preview_is_visible()` still gates the preview block; largest 86); `build_main_menu_bar` 200 -> **30** (16 helpers, one per top-level menu + View submenus; the View menu is created third but finished after Tools by `_add_view_window_controls` — that ordering is preserved and documented); `build_parser` 242 -> **44** (28 `_add_<name>_parser` helpers in the original order; the repeated `--before/--after/--first/--last` blocks are deliberately not deduplicated). Each action group is a `dict(name=_create_action(...))` so a duplicate name inside a group is a compile-time `SyntaxError`; `_SINGLE_ACTION_GROUPS` lists the groups in original creation order and `_create_single_actions` raises `ValueError` if two groups overlap; a missing field is still caught by the dataclass constructor. **Functions over 100 lines in `image_triage/`: 104 -> 100.**
- **Tests (88 new; 5 files)**: `tests/builder_snapshot_support.py` (shared dump machinery) + `test_actions_snapshot.py`, `test_menu_tree_snapshot.py`, `test_palette_commands_snapshot.py`, `test_photoedit_cli_snapshot.py`, each a SHA-256 digest test plus readable assertions (action counts, shortcut table, icons, ordered top-level menu titles, ordered palette ids, subcommand list and handlers) and structural checks (group disjointness, missing-group error). Each module docstring says it's a characterization test and how to regenerate deliberately (`UPDATE_BUILDER_SNAPSHOTS=1`, mirroring `test_stylesheet_golden.py`; failing dumps are written to `<temp>/image_triage_<module>_actual/` for diffing). Digests are pinned for Windows (key-sequence text, normalized paths). Palette states are computed once per module from the shared session window to stay fast, with everything patched restored (and a test that it is). **A real catch during the work**: the palette test initially failed in the full run because `test_char_view_state_and_shortcuts` re-applies shortcut overrides on the shared window, bringing back an F11 on `zen_mode` that `MainWindow.__init__` clears — the test now pins action shortcuts and `_preview` itself rather than assuming them.
- **Verified independently**: full suite **1797 passed, 0 failed** (1708 + 88 new + 1), no flaky test fired; reachability 0 / 0 / 1 (baseline); snapshot pre/post files diffed by me.
- **Oddities noticed and deliberately left (candidates for a later cleanup, each needs a product decision or a visual check, not a mechanical fix)**: `run_ai_culling` ("Run Review") and `open_ai_workflow_center` both connect to `window._open_ai_workflow_center` (known — see WI-6.3); actions placed in a menu more than once (`guided_ai_cull_preferences` twice in the AI menu, `show_workspace_toolbar` twice in View, `reset_layout` in View and Settings, `ai_review_tag_legend` in AI and Help); actions with no menu entry at all (palette/toolbar only: `download_ai_model`, `run_ai_culling`, `load_saved_ai`, `load_ai_results`, `clear_ai_results`, `review_ai_adapter_labels`, `review_ai_disagreements`, `keyboard_shortcuts`, and 6 of the 9 `AIStateFilter` actions — this list is pinned in the menu test); palette ids `ai.tag_legend` and `help.ai_tag_legend` for the same action, "keyboard shortcuts"/"save workspace" filed under the "Export" section (a leftover of the A14 section rename), `saved_filter.<name>` ids not deduplicated if two presets share a name; "Smart Groups" is both `burst_groups` and `filter_actions[SMART_GROUPS]`; **an ordering dependency in `MainWindow.__init__`**: `ai_state_actions` is filled by `RecordsViewController` *after* the actions builder runs, and `build_main_menu_bar` relies on that having happened; CLI: `--global` and `--mask` share `dest="mask"` with no mutual exclusion, `--first/--last/--before/--after` can be combined on several subcommands, `main(argv: list[str] = None)` has a loose annotation, and an unused `import json` in `photo_terminal/cli.py` that the reachability report doesn't flag because the name also appears in strings.

### A2 stage 3 done: `MainWindow.__init__` split into 28 ordered phase methods, behavior-identical (2026-10-02)
- **Scope.** `MainWindow.__init__` was 1,195 lines / 949 top-level statements (the shape half of the old P6 finding; its *cost* was fixed in WI-8.1). It is now **30 lines**: `super().__init__()` plus 28 calls `self._init_<phase>()` in the original order. Pure mechanical refactor: each phase is a *verbatim contiguous slice* of the old body, so nothing was reordered, renamed or "fixed". Only the first phase takes the constructor arguments (`launch_target`, `quick_view`); no other value is threaded between phases (no cut had a live cross-phase local). Largest phase 92 lines, smallest 17. The phases, in order: window frame and launch state, settings and appearance prefs, child processes and core services, thread pools and controllers, background indexing state, scan/task/search state, enrichment and AI-review label state, job contexts and scope state, records controllers and state, view state and preferences, cull thresholds and display policy, session and saved collections, filter metadata and folder watching, folder tree and drive list, left-rail section widgets, left-rail pages and nav rail, left panel layout, path controls and combos, actions and shortcuts, inspector menus and filter buttons, workspace toolbars, workspace bar and mode bars, center column and docks, menu bar and zen menu, central container and top bar, status bar, view signal connections, appearance restore and startup timers. Each has a one-line docstring.
- **Why this was the risky stage, and how it was proven safe.** `__init__` order *is* behavior here (signal connections, `QSettings` reads, widget parent order, `QTimer.singleShot` registration order, global side effects such as the perf logger and cull classifier). So the proof was tiered, not just "tests pass": (1) **static**: statement-for-statement `ast.dump` equality of the old body against the 28 phases flattened in call order (948 statements, plus the same 456 `self.<attr>` assignments, so no new attributes), and everything outside `__init__` byte-identical; (2) **dynamic ground truth**: a harness recorded, from the *unmodified* code (two baseline runs), the ordered `image_triage` Python call trace (~27,000 events), the ordered Qt/C call trace (~650,000 C calls), the `QTimer.singleShot` order, the `QSettings` operation order (~150 ops), and the resulting state (every simple `vars(window)` attribute, the widget/QObject tree with layouts, QActions, timers, thread-pool maxima, signal receiver counts, app stylesheet/palette, `QSettings` store contents and the AppData file listing), over **10 scenarios** (cold at 800x800, 2560x1400, 1366x768, 1280x720; warm-persisted at 800x800 and 1920x1080; two non-default settings sets including the `COMPACT_CARDS` migration and details view; launch-target + quick-view at 1920x1080 and a launch-target at 1366x768), each both straight after `__init__` and after show + event pump, then diffed against two refactored runs: **0 differences in every ordered trace and every state dump** (phase frames removed and phase-local lambda qualnames normalized in the Python trace). The only post-pump differences were two classes that *also* differ between the two unmodified baseline runs (details-view header-resize timing, async folder scan in the launch scenarios), i.e. noise. (3) **Mutation checks** on the harness: 5 deliberate phase swaps/omissions were all detected (swap cull/session: Python + C + merged + settings-op traces differ; swap status bar/signal connections: *only* the C and merged traces differ while the Python trace and state are identical, which is why the C trace was recorded; swap background-index/scan-state: C + state differ; omit view signal connections: C + state (receiver counts) differ; swap path controls/folder tree: crashes on `drive_list`). The permanent AST test flags all five.
- **Independently re-verified by the coordinating session**: the same flattened-statement comparison written fresh against `git HEAD` (949 vs 949 statements identical, rest of class identical, module-level rest identical); functions over 100 lines in `image_triage/`: **100 -> 99** (recounted: the largest remaining are `settings_dialog.__init__` 639, `preview.__init__` 507, `grid._paint_tile` 414); reachability 0 orphan modules / 0 unused imports / 1 unreferenced method (`_register_child_process`, baseline). Timing: `__init__` median of 7 interleaved fresh processes at 2560x1400 (measured on a 26-phase intermediate; the only later change was splitting two phases): cold 595 -> 591 ms, warm 433 -> 415 ms, i.e. noise-level, no regression.
- **New tests: `tests/test_main_window_init_phases.py` (36).** AST shape (`__init__` is `super().__init__()` plus the phase calls in documented order; phase methods follow `__init__` in call order and stay small; phases are only called from `__init__`; the promises table covers every phase), a static check that **no phase reads an attribute only a later phase assigns**, the cross-phase ordering rules found in this audit (`_preview = None` first; `_settings` opened before any read; `actions` / `workspace_docks` / `inspector_panel` placeholders; `build_main_window_actions` -> `_build_record_filter_actions` (fills `actions.ai_state_actions`) -> `build_main_menu_bar`; `build_workspace_docks` before the menu bar), per-phase "what this phase leaves behind" checks on the shared fixture window, and a runtime check that `ai_state_actions` is filled before the menu bar reads it. The test file contains no machine-specific paths.
- **What this completes.** A2 is closed. The honest remainder is a long tail of 99 functions over 100 lines (mostly widget constructors and paint code; none are declarative builders any more) which are judged low value to split mechanically.
- **Noticed and deliberately NOT changed** (each is a candidate for a separate, visible fix): (1) **`_adopt_menu_bar_shortcuts` appears non-deterministic in the *original* code**: it dedupes with `id()` of transient PySide wrappers, so which shortcut actions get adopted onto the window varies run to run (2-3 distinct sets across 4 identical runs in every scenario; sometimes missing: Compare/C, Clear Filters, Details View / Grid View, Save Current Workspace Preset). That was a real **latent shortcut-loss bug while the menu bar is hidden**; investigated and fixed the same day (see "A2 follow-up" below), which also uncovered a latent Ctrl+K ambiguity. (2) Dead or redundant statements: `sidebar_muted` assigned and never used; `library_header` built and hidden but never placed in a layout; `getattr(self, "_theme", None)` right after `self._theme = None`; `_workspace_toolbar_item_widgets` initialised to `{}` early then reassigned later; `_apply_shortcut_overrides()` runs right after `apply_shortcut_overrides(self.actions)`; `COMPACT_CARDS_KEY` / `VIEW_ZOOM_WIDTH_KEY` / `LEGACY_TOOLBAR_STYLE_KEY` are removed from settings on *every* start, not once. (3) With `browser_mode=details`, an offscreen run showed roughly 59,000 `DetailsHeaderView.paintSection` calls in 3.2 s (a possible repaint loop; may be offscreen-specific, untriaged).
- **Process note.** The implementing agent was cut off by the account's usage limit right after finishing the code and writing its status file; the tree was complete and consistent at that point (nothing half-wired). Two outstanding items from its status file (full suite and reachability on the final 28-phase file) were run by the coordinating session: see the verification line below.
- **Verified independently on the final file**: full suite **1831 passed, 2 failed, 1 skipped, 11 xfailed** (1797 + the 36 new tests = 1833 run); the 2 failures are the known-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports.py::test_diagnose_adapter_writes_stable_json_file` / `test_override_report_outputs_stable_json` (subprocess OSError, failing the same way before this stage); reachability 0 orphan modules / 0 unused imports / 1 unreferenced method (baseline); `test_packaging_scripts.py` and `test_freeze_support.py` pass inside that run. One review fix made to the new test file while verifying: `_class_ast()` re-parsed the ~24,000-line `window.py` once per phase (about 150 parses), which made `test_cross_phase_ordering_rules_found_in_the_stage_3_audit` take **49 s**; it is now `lru_cache`d and the test takes **0.04 s** (the file's six AST tests run in 1.2 s in total).
- **Open observation, cause NOT determined: an intermittent full-suite stall at `tests/test_preview_theme_guard.py::test_main_window_startup_restyles_the_preview_only_once`.** Two full-suite runs (started 19:11 and 19:30) each stopped making progress at 71-72% on exactly this test, with the pytest process using almost no CPU (about 0.06 s per 20 s) and about 3.2 GB resident, for 10+ minutes before being killed. It was **not reproducible** afterwards: the test passes alone in 15 s, passes right after the new test file (43 tests in 72 s), passes in the span of every test file from `test_main_window_init_phases.py` through `test_preview_theme_guard.py` (358 passed in 3m46s), and passed in a third full run (**382.9 s total**, with this test taking 109 s, the known ~100 s figure). A stack-dump plugin (`faulthandler.dump_traceback_later`, 240 s per test) was armed for the third run and never fired, so no stack was captured. What is and is not known: the split is statement-for-statement identical to HEAD and its runtime call/state traces were identical in 10 scenarios, so a stage-3 cause is unlikely but the stall was only ever seen on runs that included the stage-3 tree, and runs 1 and 2 had other work happening on the machine, so a contention-sensitive wait in this test cannot be ruled out. **If it recurs**, run the full suite with a plugin that calls `faulthandler.dump_traceback_later(240, file=<a file>)` in `pytest_runtest_setup` (stderr is lost under `pythonw`), and look at what the main thread is waiting on. Separately, this one test is by far the slowest in the suite (109 s vs 15 s alone, because `make_main_window()` repolishes every widget leaked by earlier tests) and is the obvious candidate for the "patch app stylesheet/palette to no-ops" treatment that `tests/test_preview_lazy_build.py::_fresh_window` already uses.
- **Update (same day): what the stalls were, what is and is not known, and what was fixed.** *Established:* (1) the stopped runs were **not** a slow test: the process stays alive indefinitely (the shell command running it never returned; one ran to its 30-minute background limit), burns almost no CPU, and when inspected with `psutil` has **1 thread where a healthy run has ~26** with 2-3 GB still resident, i.e. everything but the main thread is already gone, as in a process stuck while exiting. PowerShell's `Get-Process` wrongly reports `HasExited=True` for these ghosts; use `tasklist` / `psutil`. (2) The Windows Application log has no `Application Error` / WER crash event for Python, so a native crash is not supported by evidence. (3) It happened in 3 of 6 full runs, at two different tests (`test_main_window_startup_restyles_the_preview_only_once` twice, `test_a_lazily_built_viewer_is_styled_like_a_hand_configured_one` once), and never in isolation or in sub-suites. (4) `tests/conftest.py` arms a **120 s per-test hard timeout** (`faulthandler.dump_traceback_later(120, exit=True)`), but it wrote the dump to `sys.stderr`, which during a test is pytest's capture file and dies with the process, so a kill left no trace at all. *Best hypothesis, NOT confirmed:* a test exceeded 120 s (the first one normally takes ~109 s in a full run), the hard timeout fired, and the Windows `_exit` then hung in a 2-3 GB process. *What was done:* (a) `tests/harness.py::restore_app_state` now sets the stylesheet / palette / font only if they differ from the snapshot. Qt repolishes or re-notifies every live widget on each call even for an identical value, and hundreds of tests restored a state they never changed; the slowest fixture setups in a full run went from 41 s / 38 s / 16 s to 8.4 s or less. (b) The startup test above builds its window through `_fresh_window`: **3.4 s instead of ~109 s**, mutation-checked (disabling the `apply_theme` same-theme guard makes it fail with `2 == 1`, as before). (c) The hard-timeout dump now goes to `image_triage_test_hang.txt` in the temp folder (test name plus every thread's stack, rewritten per test), verified by forcing a 2 s timeout on a slow test. (d) Noted, not changed: `image_triage/perf.py`'s UI-stall watchdog (only when Performance Logging is on) re-arms faulthandler's single global timer every 100 ms, which silently replaces conftest's hard timeout in that process. *Result so far:* the first full run after these changes was clean, **1839 passed, 0 failed in 580 s** (even the two usually-flaky `test_aiculler_cli_reports` tests passed). One clean run is evidence, not proof: **if a run stops again, read `image_triage_test_hang.txt` for the test and its stacks, and check `tasklist` for a 1-thread ghost.** The ghost processes cannot be stopped from the shell (`taskkill` says there is no running instance) and their memory is released only some minutes after they are stopped.

### A2 follow-up done: hidden-menu shortcut adoption was losing random shortcuts, and Ctrl+K was latently ambiguous (2026-10-02)
- **The bug (found by the stage-3 harness, which saw the adopted set change between identical runs of the *unmodified* code).** The classic menu bar is hidden, and Qt does not fire the shortcuts of a hidden menu's actions, so `MainWindow._adopt_menu_bar_shortcuts` registers every shortcut-carrying menu action on the window. It remembered visited actions as a `set` of `id(action)` of the PySide wrappers that `QMenu.actions()` returns. Those wrappers are temporary: once one is dropped, Python can reuse its address for a different action's wrapper, and that action was skipped as "already visited". Which shortcuts were lost therefore varied per launch. Measured before the fix on a real window: **7 of the 33 shortcut-carrying menu actions were not adopted in a single run** (Compare `C`, Compare Current AI Group, Details View, Grid View, Next AI Top Pick, Quick Rerank, Save Current Workspace Preset...); a synthetic 6-menu, 4-deep tree lost ~300 of ~480 on the first round, and adopting twice gave 16 then 24 of 48.
- **The fix (window.py).** `seen` is now a `dict[int, action]` that keeps every visited wrapper alive, so an address cannot be reused while the walk runs.
- **The second, hidden bug the fix would have exposed.** The Command Palette is bound twice: the `open_command_palette` QAction (menu entry, `Ctrl+K`) and the main window's own `QShortcut("Ctrl+K")` created by `CommandPaletteController.setup_shortcuts`. When both are active on the window Qt logs `QAction::event: Ambiguous shortcut overload: Ctrl+K` and fires **neither**. I reproduced it with a simulated Ctrl+K press on a real window: palette action adopted -> palette does not open; action not adopted -> opens. So before the fix, Ctrl+K worked only in launches where the random skip happened to drop the palette action, and fixing the skip alone would have made Ctrl+K dead on every launch. The adopt method now **leaves `open_command_palette` out** (comment in the code): the QShortcut handles the key, follows user remaps through `apply_shortcut`, and the menu entry still displays its hint. Checked for other collisions: among the window's adopted actions and `QShortcut`s, Ctrl+K was the only key claimed twice.
- **Tests: new `tests/test_menu_shortcut_adoption.py` (6).** A synthetic nested tree where every action wrapper is dropped immediately (25 rounds, all shortcuts must be adopted, nothing without a shortcut may be); adopting twice registers nothing twice; and, on a fresh real window, every menu shortcut except the palette's is adopted, the palette action is deliberately not adopted, no two window-level shortcuts share a key (a standing guard for the Ctrl+K class of bug), and a simulated Ctrl+K opens the palette with no "Ambiguous shortcut" message. **Mutation-checked both ways**: removing the palette exclusion fails exactly the three Ctrl+K tests; reverting to the id-only `set` fails the synthetic, adopt-twice and real-window tests; `window.py` restored and md5-verified after each. **A test-isolation lesson from this work**: the first version used the suite's shared window. In the full suite that window had a re-applied F11 on the Zen Mode action (another test does this after construction, so it was not adopted: a false failure), and my Ctrl+K test's `show()`/`hide()` on it broke two `test_topbar_rebuild_guard.py` tests (their `burst_groups` button was no longer in the rebuilt bar). All four real-window tests now build their own disposable window (`_fresh_window`) instead.
- **Not verified by hand:** pressing the newly reliable shortcuts in the running app (C for Compare, the Details/Grid View keys, Ctrl+Alt+S for the workspace preset...). A shortcut that *was* randomly missing before and is now always present can still behave differently from what you remember if the grid/preview also handle the same key themselves; worth a quick manual try.
- **Verified independently (final state of the tree: stage 3 + this follow-up + the test-infrastructure changes above)**: full suite **1839 passed, 0 failed, 1 skipped, 11 xfailed** (1797 + 36 stage-3 tests + 6 shortcut tests = 1839; 580 s); reachability 0 orphan modules / 0 unused imports / 1 unreferenced method (baseline). Test files touched: new `tests/test_main_window_init_phases.py` and `tests/test_menu_shortcut_adoption.py`; edited `tests/test_preview_theme_guard.py`, `tests/harness.py`, `tests/conftest.py`. Source touched: `image_triage/window.py` only (the 28-phase `__init__` split plus the `_adopt_menu_bar_shortcuts` fix); `preview.py` was mutated and restored during checks and is byte-identical to HEAD.
